# Description of the Six Chakras

---

-> Now, following the authority of the <term>Tantras|A body of esoteric scriptures focusing on ritual, meditation, and spiritual practice</term>, the sequence of the six <term>Chakras|Energy centers located along the spine in the subtle body</term> and related elements is extracted. <-
-> It is spoken of as the first sprout of the fulfillment of Supreme Bliss. <-

Through the knowledge of <term>Mahayoga|The "Great Yoga," referring here to the highest form of union with the divine</term>, he who is familiar with the majesty of the six-petaled lotus <note>original: "shat-bhoja-vibhava"</note> is alone capable of revealing the inner truth, and no other. May the best of the wise, the eldest, who possesses the compassion of the moon <note>original: "kspanatha-karuna"</note>, clarify the inner truth of the six limbs and their accompanying power <note>original: "shat-saha-vibhavam"</note>. 

Now, the most compassionate <term>Purnananda Svami|A 16th-century Bengali saint and the author of this text</term>, desiring to uplift the world sunk in the mire of suffering, and wishing to speak of <term>Kundalini|The primordial cosmic energy envisioned as a sleeping serpent at the base of the spine</term> Yoga within the six chakras for the sake of attaining the knowledge of truth that effects liberation, makes a declaration to gain the attention of the practitioner <note>original: "sadhaka"</note> by saying "Now" <note>original: "atha"</note>. 

"Now" implies: following the description of those spiritual actions previously shown before the description of the six chakras in his own work, the *Shritattvacintamani* (The Precious Jewel of the Truth of Reality). 

The meaning of the verse is as follows: The "first sprout of the fulfillment of Supreme Bliss" is being spoken. "Supreme Bliss" <note>original: "paramananda"</note> refers to <term>Brahman|The ultimate, infinite reality or absolute soul in Hindu philosophy</term>, as the <term>Shruti|Sacred Vedic scripture heard by ancient seers</term> states: "Brahman is eternal Supreme Bliss." Its "fulfillment" <note>original: "nirvaha"</note> is the completion of its realization. For that realization, the "first sprout" is the nature of the first manifested practice <note>original: "karma"</note> which serves as its cause. This is what is being spoken by me, Purnananda. 

What is the nature of this "sprout"? It is "extracted in the sequence of the six chakras and related elements according to the Tantras." Following the authority of the Tantras—that is, the Tantric tradition—it is the sequence of the six chakras known as <term>Muladhara|The root center at the base of the spine</term>, <term>Svadhisthana|The sacral center</term>, <term>Manipuraka|The solar plexus center</term>, <term>Anahata|The heart center</term>, <term>Vishuddha|The throat center</term>, and <term>Ajna|The third-eye center between the brows</term>. By the word "etc." <note>original: "adi"</note>, the author includes the related channels <note>original: "nadi"</note>, the <term>Lingas|Symbolic forms of Shiva representing the creative pillar of light</term>, the five voids <note>original: "vyoma-panchaka"</note>, and the deities Shiva and Shakti.

<summary>This introductory page sets the stage for a detailed explanation of the six chakras, positioning the work as a guide to achieving spiritual liberation and "Supreme Bliss" through Kundalini Yoga as taught in the Tantric tradition.</summary>
<keywords>Purnananda Svami, Shat-chakra-nirupana, Kundalini Yoga, Brahman, Tantra, Muladhara, Svadhisthana, Manipuraka, Anahata, Vishuddha, Ajna</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the introductory commentary on the "Shat-chakra-nirupana." The previous page ended with the word "Now," signaling the transition into the technical description of the yogic body. This page presents the first verse of the text and begins the detailed explanation of the spinal column and the primary energy channels (nadis).</meta>

<page-num>2</page-num>

# Description of the Six Chakras

In the space outside the <term>Meru|The spinal column, compared to the central mountain of the universe</term>, on the left and the right, are placed the two channels of the Moon and the Sun. In the middle is the <term>Sushumna|The central and most important energy channel</term> channel, whose nature consists of the three <term>Gunas|The three fundamental qualities of nature: harmony, passion, and inertia</term> and embodies the Moon, the Sun, and Fire. Like a string of blooming Datura flowers, her body extends from the middle of the backbone to the head. The channel named <term>Vajra|'The Diamond' or 'Thunderbolt' channel, located inside the Sushumna</term> also extends from the region of the genitals to the head, shining within the middle channel. ॥ 1 ॥

The "sequence" <note>original: "kramah"</note> mentioned refers to the systematic procedure of meditation, the awakening of <term>Kundalini|The serpent power</term>, her journey to and from the <term>Brahma-padma|The 'Lotus of Brahman' located at the crown of the head</term>, and the ritual union of <term>Shiva and Shakti|The masculine and feminine principles of the divine</term>. This sequence, obtained through the practice of Yoga, is considered identical to the practice itself. Therefore, the "sprout" <note>original: "ankuro"</note> I am describing is the essential nature of those actions which result in the direct realization of the Absolute <note>original: "Brahma-sakshatkari"</note>, involving the Yoga of the six chakras as prescribed in the <term>Tantras|Scriptural texts on esoteric practice</term>. This is the general intended meaning.

Now, because the described Yoga cannot be achieved without knowledge of the six chakras and the <term>Nadis|Subtle energy channels or nerves</term>, these necessary channels are first defined in the three verses beginning with "In the space outside the Meru..." 

**Meru** refers to the **Meru-danda** <note>literally "Meru-staff"</note>. In common understanding, this should be known as the backbone <note>original: "prishthasthi"</note> extending from the base of the spine to the neck. 

Regarding the phrase "In the outer region... the Moon and the Sun": **Shashi** means the Moon, and **Mihira** means the Sun. These refer to the two channels representing the Moon and Sun, known as <term>Ida|The lunar, cooling channel on the left</term> and <term>Pingala|The solar, heating channel on the right</term>. They are situated on the left and right sides respectively. 

As stated in the *Bhuta-shuddhi Tantra*: 
"On the left of the Meru is **Ida**, and on the right is **Pingala**." 

Their nature as the Moon and Sun is further explained in the *Sammohana Tantra*: 
"The channel called **Ida** on the left is white and has the nature of the Moon. She is the embodiment of the Goddess, the very form of nectar. On the right, however, is the one named **Pingala**, which has the nature of the masculine Sun. She is the Great Goddess of a fierce nature <note>original: "raudratmika"</note>, shining with the brilliance of pomegranate blossoms <note>original: "dadimi-keshara-prabha"</note>."

<summary>This page introduces the first verse of the text, defining the three primary energy channels (Ida, Pingala, and Sushumna) and their relationship to the physical spinal column (Meru-danda).</summary>
<keywords>Meru, Sushumna, Ida, Pingala, spinal column, Moon and Sun channels, Tantric anatomy, Purnananda</keywords>

It expands on their trajectory from the base of the spine to the nostrils.</meta>

<page-num>३</page-num>

# Description of the Six Chakras

...and so on. These two channels <note>referring to Ida and Pingala</note> reach from the root in a straight form up to the <term>Ajna Chakra|The 'Command Center' or third eye chakra located between the eyebrows</term> and then enter the nostrils. As it is said in the *Yamala Tantra*:

> Ida and Pingala are respectively on its left
> and right. Those two vessels are straightened through the distinction of left and right.
> Encompassing all the lotus-centers, they reach the auspicious nostrils.

Here, in the phrase "those two vessels and" <note>original: "shire te ca"</note>, the word "and" <note>original: "ca"</note> is used in the sense of "or." Therefore, they reach the nostrils by either encompassing all the lotuses through the division of left and right, or by following a <term>Veni-bandha|A braided or intertwined arrangement, like a plait of hair</term> sequence where they wrap around all the lotus-centers. By this, two different theories regarding their positioning are shown. Elsewhere, their position is described as being shaped like a bow, as follows: "The two supreme channels, Ida and Pingala, are <unclear>situated</unclear> in the shape of a bow." 

The text explains the manner of this bow-shape: That which is connected to the left <term>mushka|literally 'testicle', but here referring to the base of the pelvic region</term> clings to the Sushumna. Leaning toward the right <term>jatru|The collarbone or the junction of the shoulder and arm</term>, it stays in the heart, curved like a bow. Passing between the left shoulder and collarbone, it reaches the right nostril. Similarly, the one rising from the right side of the groin goes to the left nostril. 

The meaning is that these channels, arising from the left and right sides of the groin, reach as far as the space between the eyebrows. There, the three channels—Ida, Pingala, and Sushumna—form a knot in the shape of a <term>Triveni|A 'Triple Braid' or confluence of three rivers</term> and then meet the nostrils. As it has been said:

> The goddess Yamuna is in the Ida, and the Sarasvati is in the Pingala.
> The Ganges resides in the Sushumna; the union of these three is threefold.
> They are joined at the base of the genitals and separated at the eyebrows.
> This is called the 'Union of the Triple Braid'; bathing there yields great
> fruit. <note>This refers to internal meditation as a form of spiritual purification equivalent to bathing in India's most sacred rivers.</note>

"In the middle" means within the hollow center of the <term>Meru-danda|The spinal column</term>. As it is said: "That which is situated in the middle of the Meru extends from the root to the <term>Brahma-randhra|The 'Hole of Brahman' at the crown of the head</term>," and so on. Similarly, in the <term>Tripurasara-samuccaya|A collection of essential teachings on the goddess Tripura</term>: "She who goes through the hollow interval within the staff-like support of the head," and so on. 

In this context, although the channel situated to the left of the Meru is the Ida, which contains the nectar of the Moon, O Shive! <note>An address to the Goddess Parvati</note> and the one on the right is named Pingala, associated with the Sun; outside of those...

<summary>This page describes the precise anatomical paths of the Ida, Pingala, and Sushumna energy channels, comparing their intertwined journey to a braid or a bow and identifying them with the three sacred rivers of India.</summary>
<keywords>Ida, Pingala, Sushumna, Triveni, Yamuna, Sarasvati, Ganges, Meru-danda, subtle anatomy, yoga</keywords>

--- Page 4 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of the subtle body's central energy channel, moving from the side channels (Ida and Pingala) to a detailed description of the Sushumna and its internal layers. It clarifies the physical and metaphysical location of these channels relative to the spinal column.</meta>

<page-num>४</page-num>

-># Description of the Six Chakras<-

Regarding the statement in the *Tantra Chudamani* <note>A seminal work on Tantric rituals and the goddess-oriented Shakta tradition</note> that "between those two, the Sushumna is joined with fire," it might seem to suggest that the Sushumna is located outside of the <term>Meru|The spinal column, named after the mythical Mount Meru which acts as the axis of the universe</term>. However, that statement reflects a different school of thought. According to the view of this author, as stated in the following verse—"all the lotuses are within the hollow of the Meru"—the six lotuses <note>the chakras</note> are situated within the central cavity of the spine. Since the Sushumna acts as the support for these lotuses, the Sushumna itself must be located within that central spinal cavity.

The phrase "composed of three <term>Gunas|The three fundamental qualities of nature: Sattva (purity), Rajas (activity), and Tamas (inertia)</term>" means that the Sushumna takes the form of a triple-thread through its distinct internal layers: the Sushumna, the Vajra, and the <unclear>Chitrini</unclear>. Alternatively, "triple-layered" could refer to the way these three channels—Sushumna and the others—intertwine in a sequence of two, then one, then one again. Or, it may mean that the three Gunas (Sattva, Rajas, and Tamas) are contained within her. Specifically: the Chitrini channel is composed of <term>Sattva|The quality of light, goodness, and harmony</term>, the Vajra is composed of <term>Rajas|The quality of passion, energy, and movement</term>, and the Sushumna is composed of <term>Tamas|The quality of darkness, stability, or inertia</term>.

Regarding their appearance as "Moon, Sun, and Fire": The Chitrini is of the nature of the Moon, and is therefore white in color. The Vajrini is of the nature of the Sun, and thus has the lustrous glow of <note>original: "dadimi-keshara-prabha"</note> pomegranate filaments. The Sushumna is of the nature of Fire, and is therefore red in color. 

The author cites evidence for this three-fold description from the *Bhuta Shuddhi* <note>A text detailing the "purification of the elements" within the body</note> after describing the characteristics of the Sushumna: 
> Inside that <note>the Sushumna</note>, two finger-widths upward, is the one called Vajra, and likewise the <unclear>Chitrini</unclear>. Thus, the Sushumna is triple-layered, moving like a <note>original: "lalanti lalana yatha"</note> playful woman. She is the flow of the qualities beginning with Sattva, and she embodies the Moon, Sun, and Fire.

Regarding the phrase "standing from the <term>Kanda|The 'bulb' or root center of all energy channels, located at the base of the spine</term> to the head": The Kanda is the root of all the <term>Nadis|The subtle channels or conduits through which life-force (prana) flows</term>. Its characteristics are described as follows:
> Two fingers above the anus, and two fingers below the genitals. Four fingers in width, the root-bulb is shaped like a bird's egg. From that spot, seventy-two thousand channels arise.

Starting from that bulb-shaped center, the channel stays "in the head"—meaning it extends up to just below the twelve-petaled lotus, which resides within the pericarp <note>the seed-vessel or center</note> of the downward-facing thousand-petaled lotus <note>the Sahasrara chakra at the crown of the head</note>. This is supported by the *Guru-Paduka-Stotra* <note>Hymn to the Sandals of the Guru</note>, which says: "I worship the twelve-syllabled lotus adorned by the stalk that is the hollow of the <term>Kundalini|The primordial energy envisioned as a coiled serpent at the base of the spine</term>." Thus, the Chitrini acts as the hollow stalk...

<meta>Catchword: Chitrini</meta>

<summary>This page defines the internal structure of the Sushumna (the central energy channel), identifying its three sub-layers (Vajra and Chitrini) and their associations with the three Gunas and celestial energies. It also provides the precise anatomical location of the Kanda, the root-bulb from which all 72,000 nadis originate.</summary>

<keywords>Sushumna, Vajra, Chitrini, Meru, Kanda, subtle anatomy, Gunas, Sattva, Rajas, Tamas, Bhuta Shuddhi, Tantra Chudamani, Sahasrara</keywords>

--- Page 5 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical debate regarding the internal structure of the subtle body, specifically focusing on the exact physical and metaphysical extent of the Sushumna channel and its relationship with the spinal column (Meru). It addresses potential contradictions in sacred texts regarding whether the side channels (Ida and Pingala) are inside or outside the spine.</meta>

<page-num>५</page-num>

-># Description of the Six Chakras<-

If the central channel <note>the Chitrini</note> were to end at a certain point, then the Sushumna, which serves as its container, would also have to end at that same point. Otherwise, if the Sushumna were to continue up to the very top of the thousand-petaled lotus <note>the Sahasrara chakra at the crown of the head</note>, it would contradict the description in verse forty, which states that the lotus blossoms in "empty space" (*shunya-desha*). This "empty space" could not exist if that area were still enclosed by the Sushumna channel.

On this point, some scholars cite a passage from the *Nigamatattvasara* <note>original: "Nigamatattvasara"; an essential compendium of Tantric philosophy and practice</note> as evidence, which states that "three channels are said to be located within the middle-back of the Meru." Based on this, they argue that the three channels—Ida, Pingala, and Sushumna—are all situated inside the spinal column. 

However, that view is incorrect. The fact that the Ida and Pingala channels are located *outside* the Meru is established by the testimony of all the Tantras. Furthermore, the author of this text has already clearly and explicitly stated that they are "in the region outside the Meru." 

Moreover, if the Ida and Pingala were located inside the spine, it would be impossible for them to connect from the heart to the collarbone in a bow-like curve, as they are described to do. Therefore, when the *Nigamatattvasara* mentions "three channels," it is referring to the triad of the Sushumna, the Vajra, and the Chitrini <note>the three concentric layers of the central highway</note>, rather than including the Ida and Pingala in that internal group.

Here, the author describes the manner in which the Sushumna extends from the root of the body to the head:

"The Sushumna is like a creeping vine <note>original: "chavya-valli"; the chavya is a type of pepper plant known for its climbing, clinging nature</note> clinging to the Meru and moving upward. Reaching the end of the neck, O Beautiful-Faced One <note>a traditional address to the Goddess Parvati</note>, it emerges, turns sideways, and supporting itself on the stalk of the Shankhini channel, it goes to the Abode of Brahman <note>the Brahmarandhra or the spiritual aperture at the crown of the head</note>."

Similarly, it is said elsewhere: 

"Located in the shape of a bow, the Sushumna takes the form of the sacred syllable *Om*. Having branched off and separated from the backbone, it turns sideways and moves toward the forehead. Attached to the <term>Kundalini|The primordial evolutionary energy residing at the base of the spine</term> in the space between the eyebrows, it enters the <term>Brahmarandhra|The 'Opening of Brahman,' the spiritual gateway at the crown of the head</term> through its 'mouth'."

The word "emerges" (*galita*) means it comes out from the Meru column. "Branched off" (*ghutito bhinna*) carries the same meaning. From this, we understand that the Meru column <note>the physical spine</note> itself only extends as far as the neck. 

Regarding the phrase "supporting itself on the stalk of the Shankhini": he now provides the definition of the Shankhini channel. 

Lord Shiva said: 
"The Sarasvati and Kuhu channels are located on the sides of the Sushumna. The Gandhari and Hastijihva channels are on the front and back of the Ida. Between the Gandhari and the Sarasvati is the channel known as the Shankhini."

<summary>This page defines the anatomical path of the Sushumna, clarifying that while it begins within the spine, it branches out at the neck to reach the crown of the head. It also distinguishes the internal layers of the spine from the external side-channels, Ida and Pingala.</summary>
<keywords>Sushumna, Meru, Ida, Pingala, Shankhini, Chitrini, Brahmarandhra, Nigamatattvasara, subtle anatomy, spinal column</keywords>

--- Page 6 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion regarding the anatomy of the subtle body, specifically refining the description of the Chitrini channel and the physical extent of the Meru-danda (spinal column) in response to conflicting scriptural interpretations.</meta>

<page-num>१</page-num>
-># Investigation into the Six Chakras<-

Within that <note>referring to the Vajra channel described previously</note> is the **Chitrini** channel. She is resplendent with the **Pranava** <note>the sacred syllable "Om"</note> and can be reached by yogis through the practice of yoga. She is as delicate as a spider’s thread and extends through all the lotuses <note>the chakras</note> from the **Meru** <note>the spinal column, named after the cosmic mountain</note>. There is also a channel named **Shankhini** which is said to extend to the corner of the left ear. Elsewhere it is said: "O **Ambika** <note>a title for the Goddess</note>, the Shankhini channel goes obliquely from the hollow of the throat to the forehead; it is joined and embraced by the Chitrini as far as the head." Furthermore, starting from the **Kanda-mula** <note>the "root-bulb," the source of all subtle channels located between the anus and genitals</note> between the **Sarasvati** and **Gandhari** channels, it follows a curved path to the throat. One end meets the opening of the left ear, and the other end reaches the top of the head.

On this point, some scholars argue that the Meru-danda <term>Meru-danda: the spinal column, viewed as the central axis of the microcosm</term> extends from the feet all the way to the **Brahmarandhra** <note>the "Opening of Brahman" at the crown of the head</note>. They cite a passage from the *Nigamatattvasara* <note>original: "Nigamatattvasara"; a Tantric compendium</note> as evidence: "The Meru-danda, which is like a bone-stake, is said to support the fourteen worlds and extend from the feet to the crown." 

However, this view is questionable. The backbone is the Meru-danda, and its existence from the root-bulb up to the neck is a matter of direct observation. One cannot use a scriptural statement to contradict something proven by the senses. Furthermore, if the Meru-danda were a single, solid bone-stake extending from the feet, then the action of bending and stretching the feet would be impossible. Therefore, the Meru-danda exists only from the root-bulb upwards. 

The true meaning of the *Nigamatattvasara* passage is this: regarding the body from the feet to the crown, that which acts as a supporting "stake" starts from the root-bulb (two finger-widths above the genitals) and goes upward. This is confirmed in the *Bhuta-shuddhi* <note>original: "Bhuta-shuddhi"; a text on elemental purification</note>: "Within it, two finger-widths above, are the **Vajra** and the **Chitrini** channels." || 1 ||

**"Within it"** means within the Vajra channel. **"Resplendent with the Pranava"** means that where it passes through the **Ajna chakra** <note>the "command" center or third eye</note>, it is intensely illuminated by the light of the Pranava (Om) within. For this reason, the author says in the thirty-sixth verse that it is followed by the "cluster of the morning sun..." <unclear>re-</unclear>

<summary>This page defines the Chitrini and Shankhini channels and provides a rationalist argument for the spinal column (Meru-danda) beginning at the pelvic floor rather than the feet, using both logic and anatomy to interpret scripture.</summary>
<keywords>Chitrini, Shankhini, Meru-danda, Nigamatattvasara, subtle anatomy, spinal column, Kanda-mula, Pranava</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical description of the subtle anatomy, moving from the physical spinal column (Meru-danda) to the energetic channels (nadis) contained within it, specifically focusing on the internal structure of the Chitrini channel and the Brahma-nadi.</meta>

<page-num>७</page-num>
->## Investigation into the Six Chakras<-

<warning>White spots or ink stains partially obscure several lines of text.</warning>

...situated in the middle. Piercing them, she shines resplendently by the way she is <unclear>woven</unclear> through them; she is the very form of pure consciousness. Within her is the **Brahma-nadi** <term>Brahma-nadi: the "Channel of Brahman," the innermost subtle passage through which the soul ascends</term>, which extends from the hollow of the mouth of **Hara** <note>original: "Hara-mukha-kuhara"; refers here to the opening of the Svayambhu-linga at the base of the spine</note> to the Primeval God. || 2 ||

It is said that her light or brilliance is "mingled between heaven and earth." She is compared to a spider’s thread, being as subtle as a spider’s web. She shines resplendently by "piercing through that woven arrangement." This means that she shines by passing through the centers <note>original: "karnika," the pericarp or seed-vessel of a lotus</note> of the six lotuses <note>the chakras</note>, like a thread passing through a string of gems.

However, there is a statement in the fourth chapter of the *Kalpasutra* <note>a ritual manual</note> which says: "Six lotuses are born in the void-hollow of the **Chitrini**; in their petals the Great Goddess, the **Serpent**, sports." This statement is considered <unclear>baseless</unclear> because of the grammatical mismatch between the singular subject "Serpent" <note>original: "Bhujangi," referring to the Kundalini</note> and the plural verb "sport" <note>original: "viharanti"</note>. 

Even if it were considered authentic, this would be the meaning: Because these are the words of Shiva, the plural is used for the singular <note>a common poetic or honorific device in sacred texts</note>. "In the void of the hollow of the Chitrini" is a grammatical use of the locative case to indicate the container or location. Therefore, the Great Goddess, the Serpent <note>the Kundalini energy</note>, having reached the six lotuses through the <unclear>Chitrini</unclear> void—that is, the hollow in the middle of the Chitrini channel which serves as the path for outward movement—sports (shines) in their petals as she pierces through each chakra in order. 

Alternatively, the meaning is that the six lotuses which "sport" (manifest) within the **Sushumna** <term>Sushumna: the primary central channel of the subtle body</term> are reached by the Serpent through the opening of the Chitrini; she becomes situated in their petals and then later sports in the thousand-petaled lotus <note>the Sahasrara at the crown of the head</note>. One should not conclude from that scriptural passage that the six lotuses are physically located *inside* the narrow hollow of the Chitrini itself. 

"Within her" means within the Chitrini. "The Brahma-nadi": the word "nadi" (channel) comes from the root *nad*, meaning "to move." It is called a "nadi" because one moves toward Brahman <note>the Ultimate Reality</note> by this path.

<summary>This page defines the Chitrini and Brahma-nadi channels, explaining how they thread through the chakras like a string through beads and clarifies a scriptural point regarding the location of the lotuses.</summary>
<keywords>Brahma-nadi, Chitrini, Kundalini, Serpent, Sushumna, subtle anatomy, Kalpasutra, chakras</keywords>

<page-num>८</page-num>
# Investigation into the Six Chakras

<warning>There are white opaque marks (possibly correction fluid or damage) obscuring several words throughout the page.</warning>

->She is resplendent like a garland of lightning, shining in the minds of sages. She has<-
->the form of a delicate thread, extremely subtle, the awakening of pure knowledge,<-
->full of all bliss, and her very nature is pure consciousness. The Door of Brahman<-
->shines in her mouth; this is the region attainable through the stream of nectar.<-
->Sages call this the "place of the knot" and the mouth of the channel known as Sushumna. || 3 ||<-

The word "channel" <note>original: "nadi"</note> is used here because, by its etymological derivation, it is the "Path of Brahman" <note>original: "Brahma-padavi"</note>. It is the void or hollow space within the **Vichitrini** channel <note>also called the Chitrini-nadi</note>, which serves as the path for **Kundalini**—who is the embodiment of the "Sound-Brahman" <term>Shabda-Brahman: the cosmic sound or vibration from which the universe manifests</term>—to travel into the presence of the Supreme Shiva.

The conclusion is that there is not another separate channel *inside* the Vichitrini; rather, it refers to this inner space. It extends from the hollow of the mouth of **Hara** <note>referring to Shiva</note>—which is the opening of the **Svayambhu-linga** <note>the "self-existent" symbolic form of Shiva at the base of the spine</note>—up to the **Supreme Point** <note>original: "Parama-bindu"</note> situated in the center of the thousand-petaled lotus. The rest of the meaning is easy to understand. || 2 ||

The author further describes her with another verse, beginning with "garland of lightning" <note>original: "vidyunmala"</note>. The phrase "form of a shining thread" means that the **Kundalini** resides there, shining and resplendent, as thin as the fiber within a lotus stalk. "Full of all bliss" means she is composed of eternal joy. "Her nature is pure consciousness" <note>original: "shuddhabodhasvabhava"</note> means she is the state where one's own meditation <note>bhava</note> leads to the realization of Truth or pure knowledge.

"In its mouth" <note>original: "tadasye"</note> refers to the mouth of the **Brahma-nadi**, which is the hollow of the mouth of Hara. The "Door of Brahman" <term>Brahma-dvaram</term> is the path of entry and exit for the Kundalini to reach the presence of Shiva. 

"The region attainable through the stream of nectar" <note>original: "sudhadharagamyapradesham"</note> is explained as follows: the "stream of nectar" is the flow of immortality resulting from the perfect union <note>original: "samarasya"</note> of the Supreme Shiva and His Power <note>Shakti</note>. This region or gateway is where this nectar is attained by the Shiva and Shakti residing in the **Muladhara** <note>the Root Chakra</note>. "That very thing" refers to this gateway and the area near it; it is the "place of the knot" <term>Granthisthanam: a significant energetic junction or blockage in the subtle body</term> of the **Sushumna**, the junction where the **Kanda** <note>the root-bulb or source of all subtle channels</note> and the Sushumna meet.

<summary>This page describes the "Door of Brahman" (Brahma-dvaram), the entrance to the innermost spiritual channel through which the Kundalini energy ascends. It identifies this gateway as a place of bliss and nectar, situated at the junction (granthi) of the primary subtle channels at the base of the spine.</summary>

<keywords>Brahma-dvaram, Kundalini, Sushumna, Chitrini, nectar of immortality, Hara-mukha-kuhara, subtle anatomy, yoga philosophy</keywords>

<meta>This page transitions from the general description of the Sushumna channel to a detailed visualization of the first energy center, the Muladhara (Root) Chakra. It explains the physical location and symbolic geometry of this center, continuing the technical guide for the practitioner's meditation.</meta>

<page-num>८</page-num>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras

<warning>There are white opaque marks/stains obscuring parts of the text, particularly in the middle section, though the underlying characters remain mostly legible.</warning>

->Now, the Foundation Lotus is attached to the mouth of the Sushumna,<-
->located below the genitals and above the anus.<-
->It has four crimson petals. Its face is turned downward, joined with<-
->four golden-colored letters from *Va* to *Sa*. || 4 ||<-

->In this lotus resides the square region of Earth, shining brightly,<-
->surrounded by eight spears. It is a glowing yellow color,<-
->with limbs as soft as lightning. In its lap sits the very<-
->seed-mantra of the Earth. || 5 ||<-

"The mouth of the Sushumna" is how those who know the **Agamas** <term>Agamas: a collection of scriptures detailing spiritual practice, philosophy, and temple building</term> speak of it; this is the collective meaning of the third verse.

After describing the channel, the **Muladhara Chakra** <term>Muladhara: the "Root-Foundation" energy center located at the base of the spine</term> is now described along with its primary and secondary parts in nine verses, beginning with "Now, the Foundation Lotus" <note>original: "athadhara-padmam"</note>. "Attached to the mouth of the Sushumna" means the petals are joined at the junction point where the **Kanda** <term>Kanda: the "bulb" or root-source from which all 72,000 psychic channels (nadis) originate</term> meets the Sushumna on all four sides. "Below the genitals and above the anus" refers to the area from the root of the penis down to the boundary of the Sushumna. "Four crimson petals" means it is a red-colored lotus with four petals. "Joined with four letters from *Va* to *Sa*" refers to the sacred sounds *Va*, *Sha* (palatal), *Sha* (retroflex), and *Sa*. In the system of the six chakras, the connection of letters to the petals should be contemplated in a clockwise direction. This is stated in the **Vishvasara Tantra** <term>Vishvasara Tantra: an important esoteric text regarding the nature of the universe and the body</term>: "The petals of the lotuses are said to be made of all the letters; the mind should contemplate them written in a clockwise arrangement." || 4 ||

Regarding the phrase "In this lotus resides the square region of Earth": this means that within the pericarp <note>the seed-vessel or center</note> of this lotus sits the **Earth Mandala** <term>Prithvi-mandala: the symbolic geometric representation of the Earth element</term> in the shape of a square. The text describes this region further as "shining brightly." This region is equipped with eight spears <note>original: "shula"</note> that shine in the eight directions. He clearly states that this region is yellow: "In the Muladhara is the square Earth-wheel, O sweet-speaking one; it is yellow in color and surrounded—"

<summary>This page describes the Muladhara Chakra, the first energy center at the base of the spine, detailing its location, its four crimson petals, and the yellow square representing the Earth element at its center.</summary>
<keywords>Muladhara, Foundation Lotus, Earth Mandala, Sushumna, Vishvasara Tantra, Kanda, Bija mantra, Seed-syllable</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical description of the Muladhara (Root) Chakra. While the previous page (page 8) introduced the physical location and the square yellow region of Earth, this page (page 10) elaborates on the symbolism of the "eight spears" and provides instructions for visualizing the deities residing within this center—specifically the child Brahma and the god Indra.</meta>

<page-num>१०</page-num>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras

<warning>The page has several white stains/faded spots affecting legibility in some areas.</warning>

->He is adorned with four arms and mounted upon the King of Elephants.<-
->In his lap sits the one who is as bright as the rising sun;<-
->A child, the Creator, whose four arms are radiant,<-
->And whose lotus-face possesses the beauty of four divided parts. || 6 ||<-

The phrase "with eight spears like clan-mountains" <note>original: "astasulaih kulacalaih"</note> refers back to the previous verse. What kind of spears are these "clan-mountains"? They are described as having tips shaped like the breasts of a beautiful woman. Alternatively, the "clan-mountains" refer to the seven primary mountains of the world: Nilachala, Mandara, Chandrashekhara, Himalaya, Suvela, Malaya, and Suparvata. This interpretation follows the *Nirvana Tantra* <term>Nirvana Tantra: a specialized manual for tantric rituals and philosophy</term>, which states: "O Goddess, these seven clan-mountains reside in the four corners." Just as there are seven primary mountains upon the Earth, the eight spears here are like these clan-mountains.

The phrase "in its lap" <note>original: "tad-ante"</note> refers to the middle of that Earth-region where the **Seed-Mantra** <term>Seed-Mantra (Bija): a single-syllable sound representing the essence of a specific element or deity</term> of the Earth, the sound *Lam*, resides. This seed is yellow in color. As it is said: "In the center of the seed is the Seed of Indra, yellow, four-armed, holding a thunderbolt in his great hands, and seated upon the elephant **Airavata** <term>Airavata: the white, multi-tusked elephant that serves as the mount of Indra</term>." Thus, the yellow color applies to both the seed and the deity. || 5 ||

Next, the meditation on the Earth-Seed is described with the verse beginning "He is adorned with four arms..." <note>original: "catur-bahu-bhusam"</note>. There is no contradiction in saying the Earth-Seed and Indra’s Seed are the same. "In his lap" means that in the lap of the Earth-Seed—specifically within the **Bindu** <term>Bindu: the metaphysical "point" or dot representing concentrated power at the center of a mantra</term>—the Creator Brahma resides. This is confirmed by the saying: "In the **Muladhara** is the Earth-Seed; in its Bindu sits Brahma. In the lap of that child-like form sits the Lord of the Immortals (Indra), mounted on an elephant." This indicates that Brahma is visualized as a child. Another school of thought suggests that the Lord of the Immortals (Indra) sits in the lap of the Earth-Seed. In our own view, there is no difference between the Earth-Seed and Indra’s Seed, based on the principle that "the deities are the form of the mantra, and the mantra is the form of the deity." According to the *Nirvana Tantra*: "O fair-faced one, the Seed of Indra is on the left side of the **Linga** <term>Linga: here, a subtle psychic pillar within the chakra</term>, the perfectly established abode of Brahma."

<meta>This page continues the commentary on Verse 6 of the *Investigation into the Six Chakras*, focusing on the visualization of the deity Brahma within the Muladhara (Root) Chakra. It addresses scholarly debates regarding Brahma's iconography—specifically his four arms and four faces—and cites various Tantric authorities like the Vishvasara Tantra and Bhuta-shuddhi to justify his appearance and attributes.</meta>

<page-num>११</page-num>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras

<warning>The page contains several white splotches (likely liquid damage or correction fluid) obscuring portions of the text.</warning>

...above the sound-vibration <note>original: "nādopari"</note> is the most beautiful one. There dwells Brahma, the Creator, the Lord of Creatures. Here, regarding the statement that Brahma’s position is above the sound-vibration of the Earth-seed <term>Earth-seed (Dhara-bija): the syllable "Lam," which represents the element of Earth</term>, there is another school of thought. This view holds that his position is within the central point <note>original: "vinda-madhye," likely a variant of bindu</note>, to the left of the "inaccessible" pillar-symbol <term>Linga: a symbolic representation of Shiva or the creative principle</term>. Therefore, it is said in the *Sharada* <term>Sharada: refers to the *Sharada Tilaka*, an influential 11th-century Tantric compendium</term> that the base is understood in many ways due to differences in tradition.

How is he described? As having "shining Veda-arms" <note>original: "lasad-veda-bāhu"</note>. On this point, some scholars, seeing Brahma depicted with two arms, interpret "shining arms like the Vedas" to mean that the Sama Veda and others reside in his arms. They attempt to establish this view, but that is incorrect. Nowhere in the scriptures is Brahma seen holding the actual Vedas with his hands. Rather, Brahma should be meditated upon as having four arms; the *Bhuta-shuddhi* <term>Bhuta-shuddhi: a ritual for purifying the elements of the body</term> states this clearly regarding his form:

> ...and know, O Goddess <note>original: "Shive"</note>, that Brahma is a child, red in color, with four arms. Know him, O Parvati, as having four faces and seated upon the back of a swan.

Regarding the phrase "the beauty of his lotus-face divided into four parts" <note>original: "mukhāmbhoja-lakṣmīś-caturbhāga-bhedaḥ"</note>: this means that the distinctiveness or specific arrangement of Brahma’s limbs arises from these four divisions; essentially, it means Brahma has four faces. Alternatively, "lotus-faced beauty" and "divided into four parts" can be read as two separate descriptions of his face. The term "four-part Veda" <note>original: "caturbhāga-veda"</note> implies the same meaning as before, in a non-distinct way. Even though Brahma’s specific hand-gestures are not explicitly named in this verse, we can infer them from the meditation on his female energy, Brahmi, as described in the *Vishvasara Tantra*:

> One should meditate on Brahmi, who is red-colored, wearing a radiant black antelope skin, and holding a staff <note>original: "danda"</note>, a water-pot <note>original: "kamandalu"</note>, a rosary, and making the gesture of fearlessness.

Because the *Saptashati Hymn* <term>Saptashati: also known as the Devi Mahatmya, a central text of Goddess worship</term> shows that a male deity and his female power <note>original: "Shakti"</note> carry the same weapons, scholars say the same should be imagined for Brahma here. However, in truth:

> One should think of the Primal Form <note>original: "ādimūrtti," referring here to Brahma</note> adorned with pure ornaments, holding the gestures of fearlessness and boon-granting, a water-pot, and a rosary.

Following this meditation from the *Yamala* <term>Yamala: a class of ancient Tantric texts focused on the union of Shiva and Shakti</term> intended for other rituals, one should visualize the gestures of fearlessness, boon-granting, and the rest. The other details are clear. || 6 ||

<summary>This page discusses the iconography of Brahma within the Root Chakra, arguing he has four arms and four faces despite some conflicting interpretations, and lists his symbolic tools like the water-pot and rosary.</summary>
<keywords>Brahma, Muladhara Chakra, Vishvasara Tantra, Bhuta-shuddhi, iconography, four arms, four faces, Brahmi, meditation</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Muladhara (Root) Chakra, shifting focus from the male deity Brahma to the presiding female power (Shakti) of this center, the Goddess Dakini. It addresses her iconography, her role in granting spiritual knowledge, and resolves potential contradictions regarding her appearance and the orientation of the chakra lotuses by citing established Tantric authorities like the Maya Tantra.</meta>

<page-num>१२</page-num>

->Investigation into the Six Chakras<-

<warning>White correction fluid or damage partially obscures text on several lines, notably lines 1, 2, 14, 15, 18, and 19.</warning>

# Here dwells the Goddess named Dakini, her four arms shining, her eyes red. Resplendent as many suns rising at once, she ever bestows the light of pure intelligence upon those whose minds are pure. || 7 ||

In this verse, "Here dwells," the author describes the presence of the power <term>Shakti: the feminine creative energy of the divine</term> known as Dakini within the Foundation Lotus <term>Muladhara: the "root-support" or lowest chakra located at the base of the spine</term>. The grammatical connection is: in this lotus, the Goddess named Dakini resides.

What is she like? She is one who "ever" (at all times) "bestows the light of pure intelligence" <note>original: "śuddha-buddheḥ... prakāśaṃ vahantī"</note>, meaning she is the one who brings about the realization of the ultimate truth <term>Tattva-jnana: knowledge of the essential principles of reality</term>. The implication is that by meditating upon her here, practitioners of yoga <term>Yogis</term> attain that essential knowledge. This Goddess is the presiding deity of the chakra. As it has been said: "The mouth [of the lotus] is endowed with the letters *Va*, *Sha*, *Ssa*, and *Sa*, and is presided over by Dakini."

Furthermore, it is said: "Dakini, Rakini, Lakini, Kakini, Shakini, and Hakini are, in order, the presiding goddesses of the six lotuses."

Regarding her specific meditation-form <term>Dhyana: a visualized description used for meditation</term>, it is stated elsewhere: "One should meditate upon Dakini in the Muladhara for the sake of nectar. She has red eyes and a red complexion. She strikes fear into the hearts of the 'animal-like' <term>Pashu-jana: individuals bound by worldly instincts and ignorance</term>. She holds a spear <term>Shula</term> and a skull-topped staff <term>Khatvanga</term> in her hands. In her left hands, she carries a sword and a cup filled with nectar <note>original: "sudhā-pūritaṃ caṣakam"</note>. She is single-faced, extremely fierce with formidable fangs, and is fond of sweetened rice <term>Payasanna</term>. Her body is surrounded [by her attendants]."

Additionally, in other ritual contexts <note>original: "karmāntaroktā"</note>, she is described as: "Radiant with a vermilion mark <term>Tilaka</term> on her forehead, her eyes anointed with black collyrium, dressed in black garments, and adorned with various ornaments." When such a description is found, she should be meditated upon as wearing black garments.

Here, the author addresses the question of how Brahma and all the other deities residing in the six chakras should be visualized—whether facing upward or downward—depending on the specific state of consciousness. In the *Shaktananda-tarangini* <term>Shaktananda-tarangini: "The River of the Bliss of Shakti," a 16th-century Tantric compendium by Brahmananda Giri</term>, quoting the *Maya Tantra*, Parvati asks: "Since they are in the lower part, how can their faces be directed downward?"

The Great God <term>Mahadeva: a title for Shiva</term> replied: "O Goddess, every lotus is 'all-facing' <note>original: "sarvato-mukham," meaning their energy radiates in all directions</note>. The movement [of energy]..."

<summary>This page describes the Goddess Dakini, the presiding Shakti of the Muladhara Chakra, detailing her fierce red iconography and her role in granting spiritual wisdom. It also introduces a technical discussion from the Maya Tantra regarding the multidirectional nature of the chakra lotuses.</summary>

<keywords>Dakini, Muladhara, Shakti, Tattva-jnana, Dhyana, iconography, Shaktananda-tarangini, Maya Tantra, spiritual intelligence</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the detailed description of the Muladhara (Root) Chakra, moving from the description of the presiding Goddess Dakini on page 12 to the inner geometric and energetic structures of the chakra's center, specifically the Triangle and the vital air residing within it.</meta>

<page-num>१३</page-num>

<header>Investigation into the Six Chakras | 13</header>

<warning>White opaque stains/spots scattered across the page, partially obscuring some text.</warning>

**Within the region of the mouth of the nerve called Vajra, there shines ever, positioned in the center of the pericarp, a Triangle named Traipura. It shines like lightning, is delicate, and takes the form of Desire. Within it dwells the vital air named Kandarpa, surrounding it on all sides; he is the Lord of Life, laughing at the beauty of a cluster of Bandhujiva flowers, with the radiance of ten million suns. || 8 ||**

In the meditation on the states of being <term>Bhava-chintayam: a specific mode of tantric contemplation</term>, one should imagine the faces of the lotuses as downward-facing. However, in the paths of renunciation <term>Nivritti-marga: the spiritual path of withdrawing from worldly desires</term>, they are always seen as upward-facing. Other matters here are easily understood. || 7 ||

Now, the Triangle in the pericarp <term>Karnika: the central seed-pod of a lotus</term> of the Root Chakra is described by the phrase "Within the region of the mouth of the Vajra." The "mouth of the Vajra" <note>original: "vajra-vaktram"</note> refers to a point two finger-widths above the anus and below the root of the genitals. This mouth exists within the hollow interior of the pericarp of the Foundation Lotus <term>Muladhara</term>. The connection is that the Triangle shines there.

As for the nature of the Triangle, it is called **Traipura**. The significance of the name "Traipura" <note>original: "traipurākhyam"</note> is that the Great Goddess Tripura Sundari <term>The "Beautiful One of the Three Cities," a major Tantric deity</term> has her residence within the letter "Ka," which is a constituent of the seed-syllable of desire <term>Kama-bija: the mantra 'Klim' or specifically the 'Ka' element</term> located inside the Triangle. This is stated in the *Shaktananda-tarangini* <note>A famous 16th-century tantric manual by Brahmananda Giri</note> when introducing the discussion on the principle of the letter "Ka": "In the midst of them stays the Goddess, the Beautiful Sovereign Deity."

It is described as **delicate** <note>original: "komalaṃ"</note>, meaning very smooth or supple. Likewise, it is **the form of Desire** <note>original: "kāmarūpam"</note>, meaning that through which desire <term>Kama</term> is perceived or experienced; that is to say, it is of the nature of the chamber of the god of love. Therefore, it has been said: "That Triangle should be known as the beautiful Seat of Power" <term>Shakti-pitha: a sacred site of the feminine divine energy</term>.

This Triangle is located above the Earth seed-syllable <term>Dhara-bija: the syllable 'Lam' representing the earth element</term>. This is confirmed in the *Sammohana Tantra* regarding the Earth seed: "The three lines—Rama, Jyeshtha, and Raudri—form the Triangle above it."

The author then describes the presence of the Kandarpa air <term>Kandarpa-vayu: a vital breath associated with desire and vitality</term> within that Triangle. **Everywhere** means it pervades the entire Triangle. He is called the **Lord of Life** <note>original: "jīveśaḥ"</note> because he is the sustainer of all living beings. As it is said:

<sig>२</sig>

<vocab>षट्चक्रनिरूपणम् (Shat-chakra-nirupanam), वज्रा (Vajra), त्रिकोण (Trikona), त्रैपुराख्य (Traipurakhya), कामरूप (Kamarupa), कन्दर्प (Kandarpa), जीवेश (Jivesha), मूलाधार (Muladhara), सुषुम्ना (Sushumna), त्रिपुरासुन्दरी (Tripura Sundari), शक्तिपीठ (Shakti-pitha), धराबीज (Dhara-bija), शाक्तानन्दरङ्गिणी (Shaktananda-tarangini)</vocab>

It provides anatomical and esoteric placements for these structures, citing authoritative texts like the Shaktananda-tarangini and Sammohana Tantra.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical commentary on the Muladhara (Root) Chakra, completing the discussion on the vital breath (Kandarpa-vayu) and the relationship between the Prana and Apana breaths before moving to the description of the Svayambhu-linga located within the central triangle.</meta>

<page-num>१४</page-num>

<header>Investigation into the Six Chakras | 14</header>

<warning>The page has several white spots/stains (likely paper degradation or ink loss) obscuring small portions of the text across multiple lines.</warning>

> ->**In its center dwells the Self-born One [Svayambhu] in the form of a Linga, tender like the streaks of molten gold, with his face turned downwards, revealing the light of knowledge and meditation. He is shaped like a fresh leaf. Like the lightning and the orb of the full moon, his smile is soft and continuous. He dwells in Kashi, full of bliss, and takes the form of a whirlpool in the river. || 9 ||**<-

Regarding the statement: "The vital breath <term>Prana</term> resides in the region of the throat, and the downward breath <term>Apana</term> in the region of the anus," etc.—since this prescribes that the air situated in the rectal region is *Apana*, the Kandarpa air <note>original: "kandarpa-vāyu"</note> situated in that same region is also considered a part of the *Apana* breath. Consequently: "The *Apana* pulls the *Prana*, and the *Prana* pulls the *Apana*. Just like a hawk tied to a rope is pulled back even after it flies away."

Similarly, it is said that "in conflict or in harmony, they leave this [body]." In "conflict" <note>original: "viṣambāde"</note>—meaning when they move in opposite directions—these two breaths, *Prana* and *Apana*, pull against one another. In "harmony" <note>original: "saṃvāde"</note>—meaning when they move without obstruction—the meaning is that they leave this body. In that statement, because the *Apana* air (alternatively named Kandarpa) pulls the *Prana*, it is understood as the sustainer of life <term>Jiva-dharaka</term>; thus the phrase "Lord of Life" <term>Jivesha</term> is used.

This is precisely why the author says in the tenth verse: "Just as the life of the worlds is maintained by the regulation of inhalation and exhalation" <note>original: "śvās-occhvāsa-vibhañjanena"</note>. By this, the author himself explicitly states that the *Prana* and *Apana* breaths, which take the form of inhalation and exhalation, are the sustainers of life. Other points are clear. || 8 ||

Now, the author describes the position of the Self-born Linga <term>Svayambhu-linga: the "self-originated" form of Shiva</term> within the Triangle with the phrase, "In its center." The connection is that in the center of the Triangle, the Self-born One—the Shiva-linga named Svayambhu—shines.

"Tender like the streaks of molten gold" <note>original: "druta-kanaka-kalā-komalaḥ"</note> means he possesses a soft radiance like the limbs of heated gold. "With his face turned downwards" <note>original: "paścimāsyo"</note> means he is facing downwards. This is stated in the *Kalikulamrita* <note>A classic Tantric text dedicated to the lineage of the Goddess Kali</note>: "There stands the Great Linga, Svayambhu, always blissful..."

<summary>This page concludes the explanation of how the Prana and Apana breaths sustain life through their opposing forces and begins the description of the Svayambhu-linga, the "Self-born" symbol of Shiva residing in the Root Chakra's central triangle.</summary>

<keywords>Svayambhu-linga, Prana, Apana, Kandarpa-vayu, Muladhara Chakra, Kalikulamrita, Vital Breath, Kashi</keywords>

--- Page 15 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical commentary on Verse 9 of the Shat-chakra-nirupana, specifically focusing on the detailed attributes of the Svayambhu-linga (the "Self-born" pillar) located within the Root Chakra (Muladhara).</meta>

<page-num>१५</page-num>

<header>Investigation into the Six Chakras | 15</header>

<warning>The page has minor white spots/scratches across the text, likely from physical wear or scanning artifacts.</warning>

Regarding the phrase: **"With his face turned downwards, active, and moved by the seed-syllable of desire."** <note>original: "adhomukhaḥ kriyāvāṃśca kāmabījena cālita"</note>

Regarding: **"The light of knowledge and meditation."** <note>original: "jñānadhyānaprakāśa"</note> This means that his light or radiance consists of both knowledge and meditation. The implication is that through knowledge <term>Jnana; the realization of the absolute</term>, his formless aspect <term>Nirguna; without qualities</term> is revealed, and through meditation <term>Dhyana</term>, his manifest aspect <term>Saguna; with qualities</term> is revealed. This is the essential nature of the Self-born One <term>Svayambhu</term>.

The text further describes him through an analogy: **"shaped like a fresh leaf."** <note>original: "prathamakiśalayākārarūpa"</note> This refers to the appearance of a newly sprouted leaf bud. His form tapers from a thick base to a very fine point, much like the slender stalk <note>original: "śalākā"</note> found in the center of a Champaka flower, and he is dark-complexioned <note>original: "Shyama"</note> in color.

As stated in the *Waves of Tantric Bliss*: <note>original: "Shaktananda-tarangini"</note>

> One should meditate on the Self-born Linga in the center, which has an opening and faces backwards. O Supreme Goddess, meditate there on Shiva, who is beautiful and dark-hued.

However, another scripture, the *Yamala*, states: "In the Root Chakra, one should remember the divine and beautiful triangle... situated between the lower and the upper, standing amidst blue clouds, with a radiance equal to ten million flashes of lightning." This description of the Goddess <term>Kundalini</term> as being "in the midst of blue clouds" implies that the Self-born Linga also possesses a blue color. This is not a contradiction, as "blue" <note>original: "Nila"</note> and "dark-hued" <note>original: "Shyama"</note> are considered synonymous in this context.

Regarding the phrase: **"His smile is soft and continuous, like the rays of lightning and the orb of the full moon."** This means he possesses a shimmering radiance that flows softly, like the intense yet cool light of the moon's rays.

Regarding: **"A dweller in Kashi, full of bliss."** <note>original: "Kāśīvāsī vilāsī"</note> Here, "dwelling in Kashi" implies a state of being equivalent to residing in the holy city of Kashi <note>the city of Varanasi, sacred to Shiva</note>. Just as one enjoys supreme spiritual bliss in Kashi, so too does he. Alternatively, "residence" can mean "exultation"—a state of being blossomed by desire. By the statement "moved by the seed-syllable of desire" <note>Kamabija</note>, it is suggested that this Self-born One is the Lord of the Universe <term>Vishveshvara</term>, who traditionally dwells in Kashi.

Regarding: **"In the form of a whirlpool in the river."** This describes the manner of his appearance. Just as in a river's whirlpool...

<summary>This page provides a detailed symbolic commentary on the Svayambhu-linga (Self-born pillar) in the Muladhara Chakra, describing its shape, its dual nature (formless and manifest), and its association with the city of Kashi and the Lord Shiva.</summary>

<header>Investigation into the Six Chakras | 16</header>

<warning>The page has several areas of white fluid/correction marks obscuring parts of the text, particularly on the left margin of the second half of the page.</warning>

Above him <note>Referring to the Self-born Linga described in the previous verse</note> shines the World-Bewilderer <term>Jagannmohini; the primordial energy that creates the illusion of the material world</term>, as fine as the fiber of a lotus-stalk. With her own mouth, she gracefully covers the opening of the Door of Brahman <term>Brahmadvara; the entrance to the Sushumna, the central channel of the subtle body</term>. Resembling the spiral of a conch shell, she is the dwelling place of the beauty of a fresh lightning-streak. Like a sleeping serpent, she is coiled three and a half times around Shiva <note>Here, Shiva is manifested as the Linga</note>. || 10 ||

---

The text uses an analogy: like in swirling river water, the center is sunken, but within that sunken center, something is slightly raised, much like the middle of a conch shell. This "Self-born One" <term>Svayambhu; the phallic representation of Shiva</term> is situated above the Seed-syllable of Desire <term>Kamabija; the energetic vibration of creation</term>.

As stated in the *Nectar of the Kali Lineage*: <note>original: "Kali-kulamrita"</note>
> Above the filaments is a beautiful triangle with an opening. There sits the Great Pillar <term>Mahalinga</term>, the Self-born, who is always blissful. He faces downwards, is active, and is moved by the Seed-syllable of Desire.

Elsewhere, it is said that the goddess Dakini <note>The guardian deity of the Root Chakra</note> is in that pericarp <term>Karnika; the center of the lotus flower</term> within a triangle. In its center is a subtle, reddish-glowing opening, which is the Seed-syllable of Desire. There resides the Self-born Linga, facing downwards and possessing the luster of red lac <note>original: "alaktaka-prabham"</note>. This is an alternative view. || 8 ||

The text now explains the position of the Kundalini power upon the Self-born Linga with the phrase: **"Above him..."** <note>original: "tasyorddhve"</note>. The meaning of these two verses is as follows: By the regulation of the Kundalini's inhalation and exhalation, the individual soul <term>Jivatma; the life-spark</term> of all worlds is sustained and held within the limits of the body. That Goddess, like a cluster of intoxicated bees <note>A metaphor for the humming sound of the life-force or the vibration of the spirit</note>, produces speech. She shines in the "hollow of the lotus," which refers to the hollow of the triangle in the center of the Root Chakra <term>Muladhara</term>, positioned above that Self-born Linga. This is the specific combined meaning of the two verses.

**"Shining as fine as the fiber of a lotus-stalk"** <note>original: "visha-tantu-sodara-lasat-sukshma"</note> means she is extremely subtle.

**"World-Bewilderer"** <note>original: "jaganmohini"</note> means she takes the form of Maya <term>creative illusion</term> for all the worlds.

**"The opening of the Door of Brahman..."**

<meta>This page follows the description of the Self-born Linga and the entrance to the central channel. It shifts focus to the Kundalini herself, specifically her role as the source of all sound, speech, and the breath of life.</meta>

<page-num>१०</page-num>
<header>Investigation into the Six Chakras</header>
<warning>White stains/liquid damage spots partially obscure some characters across the page.</warning>

> ->Humming like a swarm of intoxicated bees, the **Kulakundalini** <note>The primal serpent power residing in the root chakra</note> clearly produces sweet speech through sequences of delicate poetry, various compositions, and their intricate divisions. It is by her that the life of all worlds is sustained through the rhythm of inhalation and exhalation. She shines within the cavity of the root-lotus <term>Muladhara</term> as a brilliant, intense row of lights. || 11 ||<-

This means that she covers the opening of the **Self-born Linga** <term>Svayambhu-linga</term> with her own mouth in a sweet and gentle manner. Regarding the phrase *the dwelling place of the beauty of a fresh lightning-streak*: because anything "fresh" or "new" is observed to have greater brilliance, the word "fresh" is used here to indicate the attainment of even more light. Therefore, the sense is that she is the abode of a steady and wondrous play of lightning flashes, possessing a light equal to a group of highly illuminated and constant lightning bolts.

The text explains the sequence of generating speech through the phrase *sequences of delicate poetry, various compositions, and their intricate divisions*. **Delicate poetry** <term>komala-kavya</term> refers to sentences describing forms, qualities, and sentiments <note>original: "rasa"</note> using gentle words. **Composition** <term>bandha</term> refers to "pictorial poetry" <term>chitra-kavya</term> such as the "lotus-arrangement" or "horse-arrangement" <note>Highly structured poems where letters are arranged to form visual shapes</note>. **Structure** <term>rachana</term> refers to other prose and verse sentences. **Divisions** <term>bheda</term> are their specific varieties. **Intricate divisions** <term>atibheda</term> refers to the entire collection of sentences in other languages like Sanskrit and Prakrit. **Sequence** <term>krama</term> is the method conforming to the usage of the scriptures and common practice. The refined meaning is that she generates speech through these various methods, because **Kundalini** is the mother of all speech, whether Sanskrit, Prakrit, specific or general.

As it is said in the <term>Sarada-tilaka</term> <note>A significant 11th-century Tantric text attributed to Lakshmana Deshikendra</note>:
> From the splitting Supreme Point <term>Para-bindu</term> arose the unmanifest sound. That sound, reaching the form of the **Kundalini** within the bodies of living beings, manifests itself as letters through the intricate divisions of prose and verse.

From the phrase "prose, verse, etc.," the word "etcetera" encompasses all speech...

<vocab>कुलकुण्डली (Kulakundalini): The goddess energy at the root, मूलाम्बुज (Mulambuja): The root-lotus or Muladhara chakra, स्वयम्भुलिङ्ग (Svayambhu-linga): The naturally existing phallic symbol of Shiva, कोमलकाव्य (Komala-kavya): Soft or lyrical poetry, चित्रकाव्य (Chitra-kavya): Constrained or visual poetry, गद्यपद्य (Gadya-padya): Prose and verse, सारदातिलक (Sarada-tilaka): The Ornament of Goddess Sharada (a text)</vocab>

<page-num>१८</page-num>
<header>Investigation into the Six Chakras</header>

<warning>White opaque stains/damage on the left and right margins, partially obscuring some characters.</warning>

This is understood. The *Kadimata* <note>An important school of Tantric tradition</note> describes the manifest form: "Through the impact of the **Will-Power** <term>Iccha-shakti</term> of the Self upon the Breath-of-Life <term>Prana-Vayu</term>, the highest sound, known as **Supreme** <term>Para</term>, is produced in the Root-Chakra <term>Muladhara</term>. That same sound, when carried upward and expanded in the **Sacral-Chakra** <term>Svadhisthana</term>, attains the name **Perceived** <term>Pashyanti</term>. Gradually rising further, it is called **Intermediate** <term>Madhyama</term> when combined with the faculty of Intellect in the **Heart-Chakra** <term>Anahata</term>. Thus, moving upward from those two into the **Throat-Chakra** <term>Vishuddha</term>, it becomes known as **Articulated** <term>Vaikhari</term> in the region of the throat."

From there, it passes through the throat, head, palate, lips, and teeth; it resides at the root, tip, and back of the tongue, as well as the tip of the nose, in sequence. In this way, the letters from *A* to *Ksha* <note>The complete Sanskrit alphabet</note> are produced from the throat, palate, lips, and the combination of throat and lips. This is enough detail for now.

The text describes another meditation on Kundalini elsewhere:
"One should meditate on the Goddess **Kundalini** as she entwines the **Self-born Linga**. She is *Shyama* <term>Shyama</term> <note>Often translated as 'dark,' but here interpreted as 'youthful perfection'</note>, subtle, the very form of creation, and the essence of creation, preservation, and dissolution. One should contemplate her as she flows upward, transcending the universe, the very form of Knowledge. Furthermore, one should meditate on the Goddess Kundalini as the embodiment of one's own **Chosen Deity** <term>Ishta-devata</term>. She is forever sixteen years old, with full, high breasts, endowed with fresh youth, and adorned with all varieties of ornaments. She has the luster of the full moon, is red in color, and her eyes are ever-restless."

Regarding the word **red** (*rakta*), the author of the *Shaktananda-tarangini* states that this should be understood as applying specifically to the context of the **Beautiful Goddess** <note>Tripura Sundari</note>. In reality, however, for the meditation on all deities, Kundalini should be visualized as red in color and not as dark-colored (*shyama*). This is because in almost all Tantras and in the *Sarvasangraha*, Kundalini is described as having the "form of a lightning flash." 

The use of the word *Shyama* signifies that she is "warm in the cold season and cool in the hot season, possessing the luster of molten <unclear>gold</unclear>; such a woman is called *Shyama*." <note>This is a traditional poetic definition of an ideal woman, used here to reconcile the description of her being 'dark' with her having 'golden/lightning' radiance.</note> It does not refer to a dark color; thus, all the scriptures are in harmony.

In the *Kankalamalini* Tantra, the meditation on Kundalini within the **Door of Brahman** <term>Brahma-dvara</term> before she pierces the chakras is described:
"She resembles ten million moons, is the embodiment of the **Supreme Brahman**, has four arms and three..." <note>The text cuts off here at the bottom of the page.</note>

<summary>This page explains how the sound of speech originates in the root chakra and evolves into articulated letters. It also debates the physical appearance of Kundalini, concluding that the term "Shyama" refers to her youthful perfection and golden radiance rather than a dark color.</summary>

In the middle of it is the **Supreme Energy** <term>Parama-Kala</term>, most skilled, subtler than the subtlest, the highest, who holds the stream of nectar trickling from the lineage of eternal bliss. She is the **Portion** <term>Kala</term>, the very form of the power of sound <term>Nada-shakti</term>, whose body is non-different from Kundalini. He is Brahma, He is Shiva, He is the Sun, He is indeed the Supreme Lord. He is Vishnu, He is the Vital Breath, He is the total Fire, He is the Moon." By meditating on Kundalini in this way, one is liberated from all sins. Of her...

<header># Investigation into the Six Chakras</header>

<warning>White opaque stains/paint spots obscure various parts of the text, particularly in the middle and lower sections of the page.</warning>

By meditating on this [Energy] which shines like ten million suns within the opening of the **Root-Chakra** <term>Muladhara</term>, a man becomes a Lord of Men and a Master of Words. He suddenly becomes one who finds delight in all branches of learning. His health remains constant, and his innermost soul is filled with boundless, great bliss. Pure in character, through his speech and poetic compositions, he serves the teachers of all the gods. || 13 ||

---

[Commentary on Verse 12]:
The phrase *In the middle of the crest* refers to the **Supreme Energy** <term>Parama-Kala</term> situated above the three coils; the meaning is that she is in the form of Sound <term>Nada</term>. This is according to the author of the *Secrets of Lalita* <note>original: "Lalita-Rahasya-kara"</note>. The term *Most Skilled* <note>original: "atikushala"</note> means she possesses the skill or fitness required to bring about the act of creation.

Regarding the phrase *Lineage of Eternal Bliss* <term>Nityananda-parampara</term>: this means she whose sequence or connection follows a specific order originating from Eternal Bliss. The sense is as follows: from **Eternal Bliss** <term>Nityananda</term>, which is the Absolute without attributes <note>original: "Nirguna Brahman"</note>, there is the manifestation of the Absolute with attributes <note>original: "Saguna"</note>. From the Saguna comes **Energy** <term>Shakti</term>; from Energy comes **Sound** <term>Nada</term>; from Sound comes the **Point** <term>Bindu</term>; from the Point comes **Kundalini**; and this **Consciousness-Energy** <term>Chit-kala</term> is the inner form of that Kundalini. This is the "Lineage of Eternal Bliss."

Alternatively, "She who holds the stream of nectar trickling from the lineage of eternal bliss" is a single compound adjective. By the "Lineage of Eternal Bliss," it is meant that from Eternal Bliss comes the Point, then the **Command-Chakra** <term>Ajna-chakra</term> in the brow, then the **Purity-Chakra** <term>Vishuddha-chakra</term> in the throat, and so on in a sequence down to the **Root-Chakra** <term>Muladhara</term>. She is the one who holds or receives that **Stream of Nectar** <term>Piyusha-dhara</term> as it trickles down through this succession. The rest is clear. || 12 ||

---

[Commentary on Verse 13]:
Now the author speaks of the results of meditating on the Kundalini in the Root-Chakra with the words: *By meditating on this...*

*Within the Root-Chakra* <note>original: "Mula-chakra-antara"</note>: based on the statement "from the Root-Chakra, then [one should know] the Point," this refers to the Root-Chakra <term>Muladhara</term>, the first of the six. *Within the opening* refers to the hollow or cave in the center of that chakra. The meaning is: "shining like ten million suns" within that space.

Regarding the phrase *The teachers of all the gods*: According to the lexicon of Amarasimha <note>original: "Amarokti," referring to the Amarakosha</note>, terms like "lion," "tiger," or "bull" (and here, "teachers/great ones") when applied to men indicate the highest or best of their kind. <unclear>Text obscured</unclear>

->There is another lotus, pleasingly red like a flood of vermilion, situated within the central channel <term>Sushumna</term> at the region of the root of the banner <note>the genitals</note>. It is surrounded by six petals of a lightning-like color, marked with the letters starting from **Ba** and ending with **La**, shining with their dots <term>bindu</term>. || 14 ||<-

The phrase *those who dwell among the teachers of the gods* <note>original: "suraguru-vattinah"</note>: by the word "teachers" <note>original: "guru"</note>, the foremost of gods like Brahma, Vishnu, and Shiva are understood. Therefore, the meaning is that the practitioner serves those primary gods and pleases them through hymns and poetic works. The rest of the verse is clear.

Thus, this is the final determination of the meaning of the great statement:
The **Root-Chakra** <term>Muladhara Chakra</term> has four petals and is red in color. Its petals are joined with the four golden-colored letters **Va**, **Sha** <note>palatal s</note>, **Sha** <note>retroflex s</note>, and **Sa**. Within its pericarp <note>the central seed pod of the lotus</note> is the **Earth Region** <term>Dhara-mandala</term>, which is four-sided <note>a square</note>, marked by eight spears, and yellow in color. 

In its center resides the **Seed-syllable of Earth** <term>Dhara-bija</term> <note>the sound "Lang"</note>, which is four-armed, seated upon the elephant **Airavata**, yellow in color, holding a thunderbolt in its hand, and having four arms.

In the center of the dot <term>bindu</term> of the Earth Seed-syllable is **Brahma** in the form of a child. He is red in color, has four arms, four faces, and holds a staff, a water-pot, a rosary, and makes the gesture of dispelling fear <note>original: "abhaya-hasta"</note>.

Upon a red lotus within that pericarp is the presiding power <term>Shakti</term> of the chakra, **Dakini**. She is red in color, has four arms, and holds a spear, a staff with a skull <note>original: "khadvanga"</note>, a sword, and a drinking cup.

In the middle of the pericarp is a triangle shaped like a lightning bolt. Within it is the **Wind of Desire** <term>Kama-vayu</term> and the **Seed-syllable of Desire** <term>Kama-bija</term> <note>the sound "Klim"</note>, which is red. Above that is the **Self-Existent Linga** <term>Swayambhu-linga</term>, which is dark-colored. Above it is the **Energy of Kundalini**, coiled three and a half times. Above that, at the tip of the Linga, resides the **Particle of Consciousness** <term>Chit-kala</term>, standing in the form of a staff. || 13 ||

Having described the **Root-Chakra**, the author now describes the **Support-of-the-Self Chakra** <term>Svadhisthana Chakra</term> through the five verses beginning with "A flood of vermilion" <note>original: "Sindura-pura"</note>.

The meaning of the first of these [Verse 14] is as follows: *At the root of the banner* <note>original: "dhvaja-mula-deshe"</note> means at the base of the male organ. There is *another*—meaning, distinct from the Root-Chakra—*lotus, pleasingly red like a flood of vermilion*. This means a lotus of a red color that is as heart-stealingly beautiful as if it were filled with vermilion powder. This connects with the implied verb "there exists."
