<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Svadhisthana Chakra (the second energy center), detailing its structure, the letters on its petals, and transitioning into the description of its inner elemental region (the Water Region).</meta>

<page-num>22</page-num>
<header>### Investigation into the Six Chakras</header>

->Within that [lotus], there is a shining, white-rayed lotus-region belonging to **Varuna** <note>the god of the waters</note>. It is adorned with the shape of a half-moon, as white as the autumn moon, and contains the stainless seed-syllable **Vang** <note>the mantra of water, often written as Vam</note>, which is seated upon a **Makara** <note>a mythical sea-creature often depicted as a crocodile or shark</note>. || 15 ||<-

The phrase *situated within the central channel* <note>original: "saushumna-madhya-ghatitam"</note> means it is constructed or located inside the **Sushumna** <term>Sushumna</term> <note>the primary central nadi or energy channel of the subtle body</note>. Likewise, it is *surrounded by its limbs or petals* <gloss>1</gloss> which means it possesses six petals. The phrase *ending with Purandara* <note>original: "purandarantaih"</note> indicates that **Purandara** refers to the letter **La**. Therefore, it is surrounded by petals marked with the six letters ending with **La**—specifically **Ba**, **Bha**, **Ma**, **Ya**, **Ra**, and **La**. These letters are described as having the color of lightning. Through this, the connection of these specific letters to the segments of the petals is established. 

The author further describes the letters as *shining with their dots* <gloss>1</gloss> <note>original: "savindu-lasitaih"</note>. This means they exist together with a **Bindu** <term>Bindu</term> <note>the nasal 'dot' or 'm' sound in Sanskrit, representing concentrated creative power</note> and are thus manifested or radiant. Here, the Bindu acts as the quality that causes the letters to shine. It is similar to the grammatical usage in the phrase "the wine-drinker falls," where the act of drinking (the adjective) is understood as the cause of the falling (the action). Alternatively, it may mean the light of the dots themselves; meaning, the letters exist alongside the radiance of those Bindus. || 14 ||

<margin>Variant 7</margin>
Now, the author describes the location of the **lotus-region** <term>ambhoja-mandala</term> within the pericarp <note>the central seed pod</note> of this lotus with the words *within that* <note>original: "tasyantare"</note>. Inside that lotus—in its very center—is the **lotus-region**, a circular area shaped like a lotus that is *shining* <note>original: "pravilasat"</note> or luminous. 

What is it like? It has a *white radiance* <note>original: "vishada-prakasham"</note>, meaning it is white in color. Furthermore, it is *adorned with the form of a half-moon* <note>original: "ardhendu-rupalasitam"</note>, which means it is joined with or contains a crescent moon shape. This is the **Water Region** <term>Jala-mandala</term>. As stated in the **Sarada** <note>the *Sarada-Tilaka*, a major Tantric manual</note>: "The region of Water is a lotus, while the region of Earth is a square marked with a thunderbolt," and so on. 

**Raghava Bhatta** <note>a famous 15th-century commentator</note> describes the characteristics of this region: "One should draw a half-moon and place two lotuses on either side of it." As the Great Teachers have said: "The half-moon joined with lotuses..."

<summary>This page concludes the analysis of the six letters on the Svadhisthana's petals and introduces the Water Region (Jala-mandala) found in its center, described as a white crescent moon associated with the god Varuna and the seed-syllable Vang.</summary>
<keywords>Svadhisthana, Varuna, Water Region, Jala-mandala, Makara, Bindu, Vang, Sushumna, Raghava Bhatta</keywords>

--- Page 23 ---
<meta>This page continues the detailed description of the Svadhisthana Chakra (the second chakra located at the genitals). It concludes the commentary on Verse 15 regarding the deity Varuna and moves into Verse 16, which describes the meditation upon Lord Vishnu (Hari) who resides within this center.</meta>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras

->May **Hari** <note>a name for Lord Vishnu, the preserver deity</note> himself, seated in its lap <note>the center or 'bindu' of the water-seed syllable</note>, protect [us]. He radiates a beautiful splendor of blue light, wears yellow garments, and carries the grace and pride of early youth. He wears the **Shrivatsa** <term>Shrivatsa</term> <note>a lucky mark or curl of hair on Vishnu's chest</note> and the **Kaustubha** <term>Kaustubha</term> <note>a legendary jewel said to be the most valuable in the world, worn by Vishnu</note>, and his four arms are uplifted. || 16 ||<-

Regarding the word **Disk** <note>original: "bimbam"</note>: The author explains the position of the **Varuna-seed** <term>Varuna-bija</term> <note>the mantra 'Vam' associated with the water element</note> within the center of the mandala. The phrase *becoming weary* <note>original: "tāmyati"</note> refers to the well-known deity who presides over the waters <note>Varuna</note>. He is *mounted on a Makara* <note>original: "makarādhirūḍham"</note>—a mythical sea creature—and holds a noose in his hand. 

Within the lap of that seed-syllable is the **Varuna-seed**, which is white and rides the Makara. In its lap is **Hari**, who is dark-colored <note>original: "shyamam"</note> and possesses four arms. This letter **Va** <note>the seed-syllable</note> belongs to the *Ya-group* of letters <note>the semi-vowels: ya, ra, la, va</note>. This is clearly explained in the *Chakra of the Family and Non-Family* <note>original: "kula-akula-cakre"</note> and the *Mantra of the Elemental Script* <note>original: "bhūtalipimantra"</note>, where this letter **Va** is identified with the qualities of the semi-vowels. The rest is clear. || 15 ||

Next, the author describes the presence of **Vishnu** within the lap of the Varuna-seed in the verse beginning with *In its lap* <note>original: "tasyāṅke"</note>. Just as **Brahma** was seen residing within the dot <note>bindu</note> of the **Earth-seed** <note>the 'Lang' mantra of the Root Chakra</note>, the same logic of visualization is applied here: Vishnu is situated within the dot above the head of the seed-syllable. This principle should be understood for all the following chakras. The word *seated* <note>original: "kalitaḥ"</note> means situated there. 

The phrase *radiating a beautiful splendor of blue light* describes him as possessing the charming beauty of blue radiance. This implies that his body is a mass of blue energy. Regarding his meditation, the *Gautamiya Tantra* says: "The **Kaustubha** is a jewel on his heart, shining with the brilliance of ten thousand suns. Below it is the **Vanamala** <note>a garland of forest flowers</note>, shining like ten thousand moons. Above the Kaustubha is the **Shrivatsa**, also brilliant as ten thousand moons." 

Regarding the weapons in Hari's hands, another Tantra <note>original: "tantrāntare"</note> states: "In the lap [of the seed-syllable] is **Hari**, dark-colored and four-armed, holding a noose, and carrying a conch, discus, mace, and lotus, and wearing yellow clothes."

<summary>This page describes the deity Vishnu's appearance and placement within the Svadhisthana (Water) Chakra, specifically emphasizing his blue radiance and traditional ornaments like the Kaustubha jewel.</summary>
<keywords>Vishnu, Hari, Svadhisthana, Varuna-bija, Kaustubha, Shrivatsa, Makara, Meditation, Tantra</keywords>

--- Page 24 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical description of the Svadhisthana (the second chakra). It completes the meditation on Lord Vishnu from the previous page and introduces the presiding Goddess (Shakti) of this center, Rakini, followed by the spiritual benefits of concentrating on this chakra.</meta>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras

...[holding] a noose, the deity **Varuna** <note>the god of the waters</note>—this connects with the previous description. In other sources, he is described as dressed in yellow garments, with a peaceful form, and adorned with a **Vanamala** <term>Vanamala</term> <note>a garland of forest flowers typically worn by Vishnu</note>. Since the creator **Brahma** is seen seated upon a swan in the **Muladhara** <note>the first, root chakra</note>, here in this center, **Vishnu** should be meditated upon as seated atop his eagle-mount, **Garuda**. || 16 ||

## ## Right here <note>within the Svadhisthana Chakra</note> truly shines eternally that **Rakini**. She possesses the splendor and beauty of the interior of a blue lotus. Her limbs are beautified by her hands, which are raised and hold various weapons. She is adorned with divine garments and ornaments, and her mind is intoxicated <note>original: "matta-citta"; here meaning filled with divine or ecstatic bliss</note>. || 17 ||

## ## The person who meditates upon this stainless lotus called **Svadhisthana** finds that his enemies—such as the faults of **Ahamkara** <term>Ahamkara</term> <note>the ego or "I-maker" that binds one to the material world</note> and others—are destroyed...

The author now describes the presence of the power or **Shakti** <term>Shakti</term> named **Rakini** within this lotus. The phrase "Right here" is self-evident. He provides a specific meditation <note>original: "dhyana"</note> for Rakini:

->“One should meditate on **Rakini**: she is dark-complexioned <note>original: "shyamam"</note>, holding a trident and a lotus, her two other hands holding a drum and a sharp axe. She is fierce, with three red eyes and light radiating from her curved fangs. She is radiant, the Goddess of Goddesses, situated upon a double lotus, with a stream of blood flowing from one nostril <note>original: "raktadhara-eka-nasam"</note>. Her mind is focused on white rice <note>she is the presiding deity of the "fat" tissue in the body and is offered rice</note>, and she grants all desired fruits.”<-

Because Rakini is seen here within another lotus, it should be understood that throughout this system, the **Six Shaktis** <note>the goddesses of the six chakras</note> are each situated upon a red lotus located inside the main chakra. || 17 ||

Next, the author describes the "fruit" or spiritual reward of meditating on the **Svadhisthana** lotus. Regarding the name: the word *Sva* refers to the **Supreme Lingam** <note>original: "param lingam"; the symbol of the ultimate reality or Shiva</note>, and *Adhisthana* means "abode"; thus, it is known as "His Own Abode."

By the phrase "faults of ego and others," the text refers to the internal enemies such as lust, anger, greed, and so on. The meaning is that the **six internal enemies** <note>the "shad-ripu" or six hurdles of the soul: lust, anger, greed, delusion, pride, and envy</note> are diminished. 

Regarding the phrase "in the mass of the wonderful darkness of delusion" <note>original: "moha-adbhuta-timira-caye"</note>: this refers to the dense, nearly impenetrable darkness of spiritual ignorance. The seeker then attains the **Sun of Knowledge** to destroy the darkness of the illusion of **Maya** <term>Maya</term> <note>the deceptive power of the universe that makes the temporary seem permanent</note>. The rest of the meaning is clear.

<summary>This page describes the iconography of the Goddess Rakini within the Svadhisthana Chakra and explains that meditating on this center destroys the "six enemies" of the soul, leading to spiritual enlightenment.</summary>
<keywords>Rakini, Svadhisthana, Vishnu, Garuda, Ahamkara (Ego), Six Enemies (Shad-ripu), Sun of Knowledge, Maya</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes the description of the benefits of the Svadhisthana Chakra and provides a detailed prose summary of its visual components. It then transitions to Verse 19, which introduces the third energy center, the Manipura Chakra, located at the navel.</meta>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras
->3<-

...[his enemies] are destroyed in that very moment. Such a Lord of Yogis <note>original: "yogīśaḥ"</note>, even if he were amidst a mass of darkness caused by the confusion of worldly delusion, becomes like a sun of radiant light. He composes beautiful works in prose and verse, a wealth of nectar-like speech. || 18 ||

## The Manipura Chakra

Above that <note>the Svadhisthana</note>, at the root of the navel, is a lotus shining with ten petals, possessing the luster of a dark rain-cloud. Within its center <note>original: "jaṭhare," literally "belly"</note> are the letters from **Ḍa** to **Pha** <note>the Sanskrit letters ḍa, ḍha, ṇa, ta, tha, da, dha, na, pa, and pha</note>, blue in color and adorned with the crescent moon. One should meditate there on the region of **Vaishvanara** <note>the Fire-god, Agni</note>, which is a triangle as red as the rising sun. Outside of this triangle are three **Swastika** signs, and within it is the Seed-syllable <term>Bija</term> <note>the foundational sound-vibration</note> of Fire itself. || 19 ||

---

### Summary of the Svadhisthana Chakra
This is the definitive conclusion regarding the meaning of the great statements: The **Svadhisthana Chakra** is vermilion-colored with six petals. These petals are marked with the six letters—**Ba, Bha, Ma, Ya, Ra, La**—which flash like lightning and are topped with the nasal dot <note>original: "bindu"</note>. In the center <note>original: "karnika," the pericarp</note> of this lotus is the region of the element Water, white in color and shaped like a crescent moon within an eight-petaled lotus.

In the center of this region is the Seed-syllable **Vam**, the sound of **Varuna** <note>the god of the waters</note>. He is seated upon a **Makara** <term>Makara</term> <note>a mythical sea-creature, often appearing as a crocodile with a fish tail</note> and holds a noose in his hand. In his lap sits **Vishnu**, mounted on **Garuda** <note>the divine eagle</note>. He is four-armed, holding a conch, discus, mace, and lotus. He wears yellow garments, a forest garland <note>original: "vanamala"</note>, and bears the sacred **Shrivatsa** mark and the **Kaustubha** jewel. He appears in the form of a youth.

In the center of this same lotus, seated upon a red lotus, is the Power <term>Shakti</term> named **Rakini**. She is dark-complexioned and four-armed, carrying a trident, a lotus, a drum, and a battle-axe. She has three eyes and curved tusks, giving her a terrifying appearance, yet she wears white garments and is fond of the stream of red nectar <note>original: "rakta-dhara," often interpreted as blood or red spiritual essence</note>. || 18 <note>This marker indicates the completion of the commentary on the second chakra</note> ||

### Commentary on the Manipura Chakra
The author now describes the **Manipura Chakra** in the three verses beginning with "Above that."

**"Above that"** means above the Svadhisthana. **"Shining with ten petals"** implies a lotus made visible or illuminated by ten petals. What kind of lotus? It has the **"luster of a full cloud,"** meaning it is dark or blackish-blue in color. **"Adorned with the letters from Da to Pha"** indicates that the ten letters—ḍa, ḍha, ṇa, ta, tha, da, dha, na, pa, and pha—are located in its center <note>original: "jathara"</note>. That is the meaning...

<summary>This page concludes the description of the Svadhisthana (second chakra) and begins the investigation of the Manipura (third or navel chakra), detailing its ten petals and its association with the element of Fire.</summary>
<keywords>Svadhisthana, Manipura, Rakini, Vishnu, Varuna, Agni, Vaishvanara, Bija, navel chakra, yoga anatomy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical description of the Manipura Chakra (the Navel Center) introduced on the previous page. It details the visual iconography of the deity Rudra and the goddess Lakini, and provides a scholarly commentary on the symbolic geometry of the Fire element region.</meta>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras
->26<-

<note>Translator's Note: The manuscript on this page contains several white stains and smudges that partially obscure the text, particularly in the opening verses and the early lines of the commentary.</note>

## One should meditate on Him mounted on a ram, glowing like the newly risen sun, his body radiant with four arms. In his lap <note>original: kroḍe</note> resides the form of Rudra forever, possessing the hue of pure vermilion. His white body is adorned with ornaments and smeared with ash; he appears as an old man <note>original: vṛddharūpī</note> with three eyes. He is the giver of boons to the worlds, his hands gleaming with the gesture of fearlessness—the creator and destroyer of the universe. || 20 ||

## Here resides Lakini; she is the benefactress of all, her body radiant with four arms. She is dark-complexioned <note>original: śyāmā</note>, dressed in yellow garments and various...

---

Regarding the phrase "with the moons": The "moon" refers to the crescent moon <note>original: ardhacandra</note>. Because it moves through the pair of subtle paths <note>the dual flow of breath</note>, it is accompanied by the nasal dot <note>original: bindu</note>; thus, it means the letters are accompanied by these marks. "Of Vaishvanara" refers to the Fire-god, Agni. "Like the red sun" <note>original: aruṇa-mihira-sama</note> means resembling the early morning sun. 

Regarding the phrase "called Swastikas": The **Swastika** <note>a solar symbol of well-being</note> is here described as a central peak with four faces. As stated by the scholar **Raghavabhatta**, this refers to a specific arrangement of two lines. By these three types of Swastikas, the triangle of the chakra is bounded according to the desired form. In this Fire-region resides the Seed-syllable <term>Bija</term> **Ram**. || 18 <note>This commentary refers back to the elements of Verse 19 on the previous page.</note> ||

Regarding the phrase "white body from ornaments and ash": This means his body is made white by the combination of ash-smearing and various decorations. "One should meditate on him on a ram": The specific details of this meditation are given in other texts: "One should remember him seated on a ram, holding a rosary and a spear <term>shakti</term>." 

Since no other specific hand positions are mentioned in this verse, one should visualize the gestures of granting boons <term>varada</term> and fearlessness <term>abhaya</term> in the other two hands, as seen in other meditation manuals. "Giver of boons to the worlds" means he grants desired gifts. "Hands gleaming with fearlessness" indicates he holds the <term>mudra</term> of protection. Although some traditions place him on a bull, here **Rudra** is to be meditated upon as seated on a ram <note>the animal associated with the Fire element</note>; the rest is clear. || 20 ||

Regarding the phrase "Here resides": "With various arrangements" <note>original: vividha-racanā</note> refers to the goddess being decorated through the varied use of gems, pearls, and other ornaments, creating a multicolored effect upon her person.

<summary>This page describes the iconography of the deity Rudra and the goddess Lakini within the Manipura Chakra, emphasizing the symbols of the Fire element such as the ram and the seed-syllable Ram.</summary>
<keywords>Manipura Chakra, Rudra, Lakini, Ram Bija, Fire Element, Agni, Swastika, Raghavabhatta, meditation iconography</keywords>

--- Page 27 ---
<meta>This page concludes the visual and symbolic description of the Manipura Chakra (Navel Center). It completes Verse 21 and provides a detailed commentary on the iconography of the goddess Lakini and the god Rudra, as well as the specific Sanskrit letters associated with the ten petals of this lotus.</meta>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras
->27<-

...adorned with various ornaments, her mind intoxicated with divine fervor. By meditating upon this Navel Lotus, one gains the absolute power to destroy or preserve the world. Speech, accompanied by the wealth of all knowledge, resides forever in the lotus of that practitioner's face. || 21 ||

This is the meaning. Alternatively, it means the place where ornamentation is maintained through various arrangements. 

Here, the specific meditation on **Lakini** is described: One should meditate on the blue-colored Goddess, possessing three faces and shining with three eyes on each. She is of fierce form with protruding fangs, holding a thunderbolt <note>original: vajra</note> and a spear <note>original: śakti</note>, and displaying the gestures of fearlessness and boons in her right and left hands respectively. 

In the lotus located at the navel, which has ten petals and a shining pericarp <note>the seed-bearing center of the lotus</note>, the king of practitioners should contemplate that **Lakini**, who eats meat and possesses a heart fond of yellow and red <note>referring to her preference for specific offerings</note>. The instruction "having meditated on this" refers to the meditation on the navel lotus.

The navel lotus is called **Manipura**. According to the *Gautamiya Tantra*, it is called Manipura because "it is divided or filled with gems <note>original: maṇi</note> like a jewel-city." The rest of the text is clear. This leads to the final determination of the meaning of the great statement: 

The Navel Lotus is the color of a heavy rain cloud and has ten petals. Upon those petals are the ten blue-hued letters accompanied by the nasal dot <note>original: savindu</note>: **Da, Dha, Na, Ta, Tha, Da, Dha, Na, Pa, and Pha**. 

In its pericarp is the Region of Fire <note>original: vahnimandala</note>, which is triangular in shape, red in color, and marked on its sides with **Swastikas** <note>solar symbols of well-being</note>. In the center of that region is the Seed-Syllable of Fire, **Ram**, which is red, mounted on a ram, and possesses four arms holding a thunderbolt, a spear, and the gestures of boons and fearlessness. 

In the lap of the Seed-Syllable resides **Rudra**. He is mounted on a bull, red in color, with two arms displaying the gestures of boons and fearlessness. His body is smeared with white ash and he wears white ornaments; he appears in the form of an old man with white-filmed eyes. 

On a red lotus within the pericarp of the main lotus sits the power known as **Lakini Shakti**. She is blue-complexioned, with three faces and three eyes. Her four arms hold the thunderbolt and spear, and grant fearlessness and boons. She has terrifying fangs and a great desire for meat and "celestial food" mixed with blood. || 21 ||

<summary>This page concludes the description of the Manipura (Navel) Chakra, detailing the fierce iconography of the goddess Lakini and the god Rudra, alongside the geometric and phonetic symbols of the fire element.</summary>
<keywords>Manipura Chakra, Navel Center, Lakini, Rudra, Fire Element, Ram (Seed-Syllable), Tantric Meditation, Iconography</keywords>

<meta>This page transitions from the description of the third center, the Manipura (Navel Chakra), to the fourth center, the Anahata (Heart Chakra). It begins the detailed iconography of the heart lotus, its elemental associations (Air), and the beginning of the meditation on its presiding deity and seed-syllable.</meta>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras
->22<-

Above that <note>referring to the Manipura Chakra described on the previous page</note>, in the region of the heart, is a most beautiful lotus, shining with the luster of the **Bandhuka** flower <note>original: bandhūka; a bright scarlet flower</note>. It is adorned with twelve vermilion-colored letters beginning with **Ka**. Known by the name **Anahata**, it is like the celestial wish-fulfilling tree <note>original: surataru/kalpataru</note> that grants even more than what is desired. Here lies the region of Air, smoky in appearance and beautified by a six-pointed star. || 22 ||

In its center, one should meditate upon the charming seed-syllable of the Wind <note>the letter **Yam**</note>, grey like a mass of smoke, shining with four arms, and mounted upon a black antelope...

***

He now describes the **Heart Lotus** <note>original: hṛdipañkaja</note>. The phrase "Above that" and so on encompasses six verses. "Lotus in the heart": the grammatical connection is formed by adding the action "one should meditate on such a lotus in the heart." 

**Very beautiful** means extremely smooth or pleasing. 

**Beginning with Ka**: it should be understood that these are the twelve letters—*ka, kha, ga, gha, ṅa, ca, cha, ja, jha, ña, ṭa,* and *ṭha*. 

**Adorned with them** means it is joined or decorated with these letters. 

**Known by the name Anahata**: the "unstruck" sound <note>original: anāhata</note>, which consists of the divine vibration of the <term>Sound-Brahman</term> <note>Śabda-Brahman; the ultimate reality expressed as sound</note>, is perceived there. The meaning is that the lotus is named **Anahata** because of the traditional saying: "That lotus is proclaimed by the sages as the one called Anahata." 

Because it is in the form of the **Celestial Tree** <note>the Kalpataru or wish-fulfilling tree</note>, it is described as "granting more than what is desired." This is an "implicative adjective," meaning its nature as a divine tree is the reason it fulfills all wishes. 

With the words "**of the Air**," he describes the location of the <term>Region of Air</term> <note>Vāyu-maṇḍala</note> within the pericarp <note>the central seed-vessel</note> of that lotus. || 22 ||

Regarding the center of the Air region, he describes the <term>Seed-syllable of Air</term> <note>Vāyu-bīja</note> with the words "**In its center**." 

**The letter of the wind** refers to the seed-syllable **Yam**, which is **charming** or captivating to the mind. 

**Grey like a mass of smoke**: the meaning is that its color is a smoky-grey because its form is enveloped by trails of smoke. 

**Mounted upon a black [antelope]**: this means he is mounted on a <term>blackbuck antelope</term> <note>original: kṛṣṇasāra</note>. 

Just as a noose is seen in the hand of Varuna <note>the god of the Waters</note>, here in the hand of **Vayu** <note>the god of the Air</note>, a goad <note>original: aṅkuśa; a tool used to guide elephants, symbolizing control</note> should be meditated upon. 

Inside the seed-syllable of Air, he describes the presence of the Lord **Isha**...

<summary>This page introduces the Anahata (Heart) Chakra, describing it as a twelve-petaled red lotus associated with the element of Air and the wish-fulfilling tree. It details the iconography of the air region's seed-syllable, Yam, which is depicted as smoky-grey and mounted on a black antelope.</summary>
<keywords>Anahata Chakra, Heart Lotus, Air Element, Yam seed-syllable, Black Antelope, Vayu, Sound-Brahman, Wish-fulfilling tree</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the description of the Anahata (Heart) Chakra. Following the description of the elemental region and the seed-syllable "Yam" on the previous page, the text now focuses on the presiding deities of this center: the male deity Isha and the female Power (Shakti) Kakini.</meta>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras
->28<-

...supreme. In its center is the pure Treasure-house of Mercy, known by the name **Isha** <note>original: īśābhidhaṃ; a name for Shiva as the "Lord" or "Ruler"</note>, who with his two hands grants fearlessness and boons to all the three worlds. || 23 ||

Here indeed dwells **Kakini**, yellow as a fresh flash of lightning, auspicious and three-eyed. She is adorned with every ornament and is the benevolent bringer of joy to all people. In her hands, she carries a noose, a skull-bowl, and makes the gestures of granting boons and fearlessness. She is intoxicated <note>divinely ecstatic</note>, her heart is softened by the flow of full nectar, and she wears a garland of bones. || 24 ||

***

The phrase **In its center** is clear. In all scriptures, because Shiva is described as having three eyes, this deity <note>Isha</note> is also **three-eyed**. One should meditate upon him as wearing **silken garments** <note>original: kṣaumāmbara</note> as prescribed in other ritual texts. Likewise, he is beautiful, radiant with the light of ten million moons, and glowing with his matted hair <note>original: kaparda</note>. || 23 ||

---

<margin>-</margin>Now he describes the presence of the <term>Shakti</term> <note>the feminine creative power or goddess aspect</note> **Kakini** within the pericarp <note>the central seed-vessel of the lotus</note> with the verse beginning **Here indeed**. **Intoxicated** refers to a state where the usual ego-driven consciousness has slipped away into divine bliss. **Her heart is softened by the flow of full nectar**: this means her heart—her inner consciousness—is saturated with the nectar of immortality <note>original: sudhā-rasa</note> which is completely satisfying, making her heart tender and inclined toward the welfare of others. Alternatively, it means her heart is loosened and expanded with supreme bliss, saturated with the nectar dripping from within the **Thousand-Petaled Lotus** <note>original: sahasradala-kamala; the crown chakra at the top of the head</note>. The rest is clear. It should be understood that she wears **black garments**, as established in the meditation verse: "One should meditate on the eternal, moon-faced Kakini, who wears black clothes and is adorned with various ornaments, for the attainment of success in mantras." || 24 ||

<summary>This page describes the presiding deities of the Heart Chakra (Anahata): Isha, a compassionate form of Shiva, and Kakini, a golden-hued Shakti who is intoxicated by the nectar of the crown chakra.</summary>
<keywords>Isha, Kakini, Heart Chakra, Anahata, nectar of immortality, Shiva, Shakti, iconography, meditation</keywords>

--- Page 30 ---
<meta>This page continues the detailed exploration of the Anahata (Heart) Chakra. Following the description of the deities Isha and Kakini on the previous page, the text now focuses on the inner sanctum of the chakra: the triangular space of Power (Shakti) and the presence of the Shiva-linga within it.</meta>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras
->30<-

## Within the heart of this lotus-pericarp shines the Power known as the Triangle, 
## whose soft body is like ten million flashes of lightning. She dwells there, and 
## within her is the Shiva-linga named Bana <note>original: bāṇākhya-śivaliṅga; "Bana" refers to a specific type of naturally occurring stone linga, often found in the Narmada river</note>, which is radiant with the 
## luster of gold. At its head, it possesses a subtle opening, like a jewel that is 
## the abode of exuberant beauty. || 25 ||

***

He describes the triangle in the middle of this lotus pericarp <note>the central seed-vessel of the heart lotus</note> with the verse beginning **Within the heart**. **Known as the Triangle** <note>original: trikoṇābhidhā</note>: by calling this Power <term>Shakti</term> a triangle, it is indicated that the triangle points downwards <note>in Tantric symbolism, a downward-pointing triangle represents the feminine creative energy or Yoni</note>. The grammatical connection is "She dwells." This triangle is located in the region below the Seed-syllable of Air <note>the syllable "Yam" mentioned in previous verses</note>.

This is stated elsewhere: "In its lap is Isha <note>the Lord</note>, and below him, in the triangle, is the **Bana-linga**." He specifies the nature of the Linga named Bana: it has a **subtle opening at its head**. This means that due to the union of the half-moon and the Point <term>Bindu</term> on the top of the Shiva-linga, there is an opening or hole within that Point in the form of the **Void** <note>original: śūnya; the state of pure potentiality or emptiness</note>.

As it is said: "Or rather, the Bana-linga within the triangle is adorned with golden ornaments. The Lord has the half-moon on his head; in the center is the supreme **red lotus**." The phrase **in the center the red lotus** refers to an upward-facing, red-colored, eight-petalled lotus located below the pericarp of the heart lotus. **Mental worship** <note>original: mānasa-pūjā</note> should be performed upon this lotus.

This is described as follows: "In its center is a red eight-petalled lotus, and there stands the **Wish-Fulfilling Tree** <note>original: kalpataru</note>, as well as the beautiful seat of the Chosen Deity <note>original: iṣṭa-devatā</note> shining under a moon-canopy." After further describing it as filled with various fruits and flowers and adorned by sweet-voiced birds, the text says: "There, one should meditate on their Chosen Deity according to the path described in the respective ritual manuals." Enough of this elaboration; let us return to the subject at hand.

He provides an illustration of the light of the Point atop the Bana-linga with the phrase **like a jewel**. Just as there is a glow within a jewel that has been pierced with a hole <note>like a bead</note>, so too is the light of the Point atop the Shiva-linga.

<summary>This page describes the innermost symbolic elements of the Heart Chakra: a downward-pointing triangle representing feminine Power and the golden Bana-linga. It also introduces a secondary eight-petalled lotus used for mental worship and meditation on one's personal deity.</summary>
<keywords>Anahata, Bana-linga, Triangle, Shakti, Bindu, Mental Worship, Wish-Fulfilling Tree, Heart Chakra</keywords>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras
->31<-

## He who meditates on this heart-lotus—which is the celestial tree of heaven <note>original: surataru; the wish-fulfilling Kalpa-vriksha</note>, the abode of all, the seat of the God, and is beautified by the Swan <note>original: haṃsa; here representing the individual soul or the breath</note> resembling the steady flame of a lamp in a windless place; which bears the beauty of the filaments shining within the orb of the sun—that person becomes a master of speech, and like the Lord of the worlds, is capable of both protection and destruction. || 26 ||

***

**The Meaning:** Regarding the phrase **abode of exuberant beauty** <note>original: prollāsa-lakṣmyālayaḥ</note>: "exuberant" <note>prollāsa</note> refers to the arising of desire <term>Kama</term>; thus, it means the place possesses that beauty. || 25 ||

->He describes the fruit of contemplating the heart-lotus with the two verses beginning with **He who meditates**.<-

The meaning is as follows: the person who meditates on the lotus in the heart becomes a **master of speech**, equal to **Brihaspati** <note>the preceptor of the gods and the lord of wisdom and eloquence</note>. In this way, the connection to the action should be understood throughout, using the nominative case for terms like "the Lord" and so on. 

Here, the text describes the presence of the individual soul <term>Jivatma</term> in the form of a swan within the pericarp <note>the central seed-vessel</note> of the lotus with the phrase: **beautified by the Swan which is like the flame of a lamp in a windless place**. This means it is beautified by a "Swan" that is similar to the tiny flame of a lamp where there is no air <note>representing a state of perfect stillness and meditation</note>. The idea is that the individual soul exists in the form of a swan, shaped like the very steady flame of a lamp undisturbed by the wind, located within the pericarp of the lotus.

He then makes known the presence of the sun’s orb within this pericarp: **which bears the beauty of the filaments shining within the orb of the sun**. This means it bears the beauty of the **filaments** <note>original: kiñjalka; the stamens of the lotus</note> or the "splendor of the filaments" that are shining and appearing within the center of the pericarp, decorated or illuminated by the **orb of the sun**. 

In this context, being "decorated by the sun's orb" is an adjective describing the filaments, not an adjective for the "interior." If it were describing the interior, then since every lotus possesses the beauty of filaments, the specific mention of it here would be useless. Of the sun—

<summary>This page describes the meditation on the heart-lotus (Anahata Chakra), comparing the individual soul to a steady flame and promising the practitioner mastery of speech and divine power. It provides a technical commentary on the symbolic light and the "Swan" of the soul residing within the sun-disk of the heart.</summary>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras
->32<-

> He becomes a **lord of Yogis** <note>original: yogīśo</note>, dearer than the dearest to the **company of women** <note>original: kāntā-kulasya</note>. He is a lord of wisdom, an achiever, one who has **conquered his senses** <note>original: jitendriya-gaṇo</note>, and is capable of unwavering focus in meditation. With prose and verse...

---

In regards to being "adorned with filaments within the orb," because such specific filaments do not exist elsewhere, this description is meaningful. Furthermore, if the pericarp <note>the central seed-vessel of the lotus</note> is viewed as the **orb of the sun**, then the beauty of all the filaments arises from that sun’s light; this would not be possible if they were located in only one part. 

Therefore, within that pericarp, first there is the **Sphere of Air** <term>Vayu-mandala</term> pervading the pericarp; above that is the **Sphere of the Sun** <term>Surya-mandala</term>. Above that, one should meditate on the triangle of the **Fire of Air** <note>referring to the elemental hierarchy within the heart center</note> and so forth. This is appropriate. In the rituals for mental worship <note>original: Mānasārcamāyāṃ; likely referring to the Mānasollāsa or a similar manual of internal liturgy</note>, the sequence of worship—"Salutations to the Sphere of Fire with its ten kalas <note>aspects or rays</note>," and so on—shows the spheres of Fire, Sun, and Moon positioned one above the other. This established layout is followed in the explanation. Enough on this detail.

The word **Lord** <note>original: Īśvara</note> here means the **Creator** <term>Srishtikartta</term>. The phrase **capable of preserving the jewels** means the one who performs the act of maintenance and dissolution <note>original: sthiti-pralaya-karttā</note>. Thus, the deeper meaning is that such a person becomes a master of creation, maintenance, and destruction. || 26 ||

**Lord of Yogis** <note>original: Yogīśaḥ</note>: This refers to one who is highly skilled in his duties and is **dearer than the dearest to the company of women** <note>original: kāntā-kulasya</note>, meaning he is beloved by the feminine collective. 

**One who has conquered his senses** <note>original: Jitendriya-gaṇaḥ</note>: This means he is counted among—or regarded as equal to—those who have mastered their senses. 

**Capable of unwavering focus in meditation**: This means he is capable and empowered to perform **contemplation on the Absolute** <term>Brahma-chintana</term> with a steady and absorbed mind. 

**Carrier of the stream of poetic water**: This describes one who carries or bears an uninterrupted flow of "water" in the form of poetry. He becomes like **The Deity for whom Lakshmi is the Stage** <note>original: Lakṣmī-raṅga-daivataḥ</note>. This means he becomes equal to the god **Vishnu**. Alternatively...

<meta>This page continues the technical commentary on Verse 27 regarding the Anahata (Heart) Chakra. It transitions from general praise of the practitioner’s powers to a specific breakdown of the Sanskrit terminology used in the verse, followed by a detailed summary of the visualization of the heart center, including the deity Isha and the Shakti Kakini.</meta>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras
## 33

->He carries a stream of the nectar of poetry; he is a deity to the company of women, and is able to enter<-
->the city of another in an instant. // 27 //<-

**Lakshmi** <note>original: Lakṣmīḥ</note> refers to prosperity or wealth. **Rangana** <note>original: raṅgaṇam</note> refers to the experience of bliss. **Daivata** <note>original: daivatam</note> refers to a collective or host of deities, similar to how one might refer to "the people." Therefore, the phrase "a deity to the company of women" implies one who is a source of bliss and prosperity for them. This is achieved through a specific grammatical derivation <note>specifically the 'na' suffix in the sense of a possessive compound</note>. Thus it is said:

> Having enjoyed the greatest of pleasures in this world, at the end, he goes to the place of liberation.

Regarding the phrase "able to enter the **city of another** <note>original: para-pure; this can also imply 'another's body' in Yogic literature</note> in an instant": even when a fortress is difficult to observe due to gatekeepers and guards, the practitioner becomes capable of entering another's city. This means they acquire the ability to become invisible, travel through the sky, and other such powers that serve as the cause for such entry.

Here, some readers prefer the word **Daivatam** <note>original: daivatam</note> as a neuter noun. They argue that even when describing a person, it retains its neuter gender. However, this is incorrect. According to the **Amarakosha** <note>the most celebrated Sanskrit lexicon</note>, the word *Daivata* is listed as being both masculine and neuter when referring to a god, and feminine when using the word *Devata*. Therefore, the reading with the masculine ending <note>daivataḥ</note> is also perfectly valid. In the sense presented here, that interpretation is even clearer; the rest is straightforward.

The following is the definitive conclusion on the meaning of this great passage:

The **Heart Lotus** <term>Hrit-padma</term> is the color of a Bandhuka flower <note>a bright red flower, *Pentapetes phoenicea*</note>. It is marked with the **Bindu** <term>the point of concentrated energy</term> which shines like vermilion. It has twelve petals, inscribed with the twelve letters: **ka, kha, ga, gha, ṅa, ca, cha, ja, jha, ña, ṭa, and ṭha**. 

In its pericarp <note>the central seed-vessel</note> is the smoke-colored **Sphere of Air** <term>Vayu-mandala</term> in the shape of a hexagon. Above that is the **Sphere of the Sun**. In the center of that is a triangle resembling millions of flashes of lightning. Above that is the **Seed-Syllable of Air** <note>the mantra *Yaṃ*</note>, smoke-gray in color, four-armed, and holding a hook <note>original: aṅkuśa</note>, seated upon a **black antelope** <note>original: kṛṣṇasāra; the traditional mount/vahanam for the element of Air</note>.

In the lap of this Seed-syllable is the Lord **Isha** <note>a form of Shiva</note>. He is as white as a swan, has two arms, and makes the gestures of **granting boons** and **dispelling fear** <term>Vara and Abhaya mudras</term>. He has three eyes. 

Also within this pericarp, seated upon a red lotus, is the goddess **Kakini Shakti**. She is gold-colored, four-armed, and carries a noose and a skull-cup while making the gestures of granting boons and dispelling fear. She is dressed in yellow garments, adorned with all varieties of ornaments, and wears a garland of bones. Her heart is softened <note>or "moistened"</note> by the drinking of nectar. In the middle—

<meta>catchword: In the middle—</meta>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras
## 24

In the triangle is the **Bana-linga Shiva** <term>a sacred stone representation of the deity Shiva, specifically the 'forest' or 'arrow' linga</term>, whose head is shaped like a half-moon and a dot <note>the 'bindu' or point of concentrated energy</note>. He is of a golden color and is radiant with the arousal of desire. Below that is the **individual soul** <note>original: jīvātman</note> in the form of a **Swan** <term>Hamsa; a symbol of the breath and the soul's purity</term>, resembling the steady flame of a lamp. Below the pericarp of this center is a red-colored, upward-facing eight-petalled lotus. There stands the **Wishing-Tree** <note>original: kalpavṛkṣa</note> and a jeweled altar adorned with a moon-canopy, flags, and other ornaments; this is the place for mental worship. // 27 //

---

->The stainless lotus named **Vishuddha** <term>meaning 'The Pure'</term> is in the throat; it is of a<-
->smoky-grey luster. It is illumined by all the sixteen vowels, which are crimson and<-
->shining upon its petals, for the one of radiant intellect. Within it sits the<-
->region of Ether, famous as the full moon, circular in form, situated upon an<-
->elephant white as snow, and wearing white-colored garments. // 28 //<-

---

Now, he describes the **Vishuddha Chakra** <note>the purification center</note> in the region of the throat in four verses, beginning with "The stainless lotus named Vishuddha..." It is called the "Pure" lotus because it spreads purification <note>original: viśuddhi</note> for the individual soul through the sight of the **Swan** <note>Hamsa</note>. It is also called the "Great Supreme Ether."

The meaning is as follows: the lotus named **Vishuddha** resides in the throat. He characterizes the lotus as **stainless** <note>original: amalam</note>; because it is composed of light, it is free from impurity. It is **illumined** <note>made manifest</note> by **all the vowels**—the sixteen vowels starting with 'A' and ending with the 'Visarga' <note>the breathing sound 'ḥ'</note>—for the practitioner of **radiant intellect** <note>original: dīpta-buddheḥ</note>. This refers to a seeker whose intellect has been made bright because they are free from the impurity of attachment to worldly objects through constant yoga practice.

In what way are the vowels present? They are **shining upon the petals**. By this, it is indicated that since there are sixteen vowels, this lotus has sixteen petals. This is stated clearly elsewhere: "Above that [the heart] is a sixteen-petalled lotus of a smoky color, joined with the sixteen crimson vowels adorned with the Bindu <note>the nasal 'm' sound</note>."

<summary>This page concludes the visualization of the Heart Chakra (Anahata) and introduces the Throat Chakra (Vishuddha), describing its sixteen petals, its association with the element of Ether, and its smoky-grey color.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the detailed commentary on the Vishuddha (Throat) Chakra. # Investigation into the Six Chakras 35

->With arms shining with the noose, the gesture of fearlessness, the elephant-goad, and the gesture of granting boons, his body is beautiful.<-
->On the lap of that Mantra eternally resides the one whose body is half-integrated with the Daughter of the Mountain.<-

The phrase "adorned with the region of Ether and a red-tinged filament" refers to the center of the lotus. Similarly, the "region of Ether famous as the full moon" indicates that this space is perfectly circular and white in color, just like the full moon. This is supported by the teachings in the *Sarada-tilaka* <note>a significant 11th-century Tantric compendium</note>, which states that "wise men know these regions possess the same colors as their presiding elements." Since the element of **Ether** <note>original: nabhas</note> is white, its region is understood to be white as well. This "region of Ether" is circular in shape and is situated within the **pericarp** <term>the central seed-vessel of the lotus</term>.

Regarding the phrase "wearing white-colored garments": within the region of Ether, there eternally resides the deity known as **Sada-Shiva** <term>the "Eternal Shiva," representing the highest state of consciousness in this chakra</term>. This is established in the second verse of this section, which states that the god known as Sada-Shiva dwells there constantly. 

How is he described? He is "upon a body shining on an elephant with the shade of snow." The term **elephant with the shade of snow** <note>original: himacchāyā-nāga</note> is explained through a grammatical compound meaning an elephant characterized by a resemblance to snow. Alternatively, it can be interpreted as an elephant that is exactly like the color of snow, where the middle word of the comparison is omitted, resulting in the meaning: "a white-colored elephant." Here, the word **Naga** means elephant. This is clearly stated in the text *Bhuta-shuddhi* <term>a manual on the purification of the elements</term>: "Within that is the **Seed-Mantra of Ether** <note>the syllable HAM</note>, situated upon a pure white elephant." The term **Haima** <note>usually meaning golden</note> here, through a specific grammatical derivation, indicates a "white elephant" as pure as snow. Therefore, the phrase means: "one whose body is brilliantly manifest upon a white elephant." // 28 //

Again, how is he described? "With arms shining with the noose, the gesture of fearlessness, the elephant-goad, and the gesture of granting boons, his body is beautiful." The practical meaning is that he possesses four hands holding a **noose** <term>pāśa; representing the power to bind ignorance</term>, an **elephant-goad** <term>aṅkuśa; representing the control of the senses</term>, and making the gestures of **granting boons** and **dispelling fear**. 

Furthermore, whose "Mantra" is this? It refers to the one who is identical to the **Seed-Mantra of Ether**, which is **HAM** <term>the vibration associated with the throat center and the space element</term>.

<vocab>षट्चक्रनिरूपणम् (Shat-Chakra-Nirupana), व्योममण्डल (Vyoma-mandala), सदाशिव (Sada-Shiva), हिमच्छायानाग (Hima-chhaya-naga), हैमगज (Haima-gaja), हम् (HAM), भूतशुद्धि (Bhuta-shuddhi)</vocab>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 36

->...resembling snow. Three-eyed, five-faced, with ten graceful arms, and richly dressed in a tiger-skin garment; the god always known and celebrated in the scriptures as Shiva.<- — 26

The implication here is that the **Seed-syllable of Ether** <term>original: nabho-bija; the sound "Ham"</term> is situated within the **pericarp** <term>the central seed-vessel of the lotus</term>, and one should meditate upon it in the form described. The author further specifies the nature of **Sada-Shiva** <term>the "Eternal Shiva," the presiding deity of this center</term> by stating he is "integrated with the body of the Daughter of the Mountain." This means he is in the form of **Ardhanarishwara** <note>the composite androgynous form of Shiva and Parvati, representing the inseparable nature of masculine and feminine principles</note>. 

He is described as "resembling snow," which indicates a white color. However, because he is in his half-woman form, the left half of the body is the color of gold, while the right half is white. This is explicitly stated in other texts: "One should see the god Sada-Shiva there, clothed in white, half-integrated with the body of the Daughter of the Mountain, with a body that is part silver and part gold." This occurs within the "lap" or center of the Seed-syllable of Ether. 

Furthermore, the *Nirvana Tantra* <note>a foundational text of the Shakta tradition</note>, in the section concerning the **Vishuddha Chakra** <term>the "Pure" or Throat Center</term>, states: "In the middle of the mystical diagram is a bull on a great throne. Upon it sits **Gauri** <note>the Golden Goddess, Parvati</note> eternally, and on the right side is Sada-Shiva. He has three eyes and five faces, with three eyes on each face. His limbs are smeared with sacred ash, and he resembles a mountain of silver. The god wears a tiger skin and is adorned with garlands of snakes." 

The phrase "Gauri sits there eternally" means she exists as half of Shiva's body, which the same text clarifies: "Gauri, the Mother of the World, shares half the body of Shambhu <note>Shiva</note>." 

Regarding the "ten graceful arms": this means he possesses ten hands praised for their beauty. Since the specific weapons held in these ten arms are not named in this particular verse, one should look to other meditations <note>original: dhyana</note> for guidance. Another meditation states: "I worship the one whose limbs are bright with infinite ornaments, holding a trident, a battle-axe, a sword, a thunderbolt, fire, the king of cobras, a bell, an elephant-goad, a noose, and making the gestures of dissipating fear and granting boons." These are the implements one should imagine in his hands. The term "famous" means he is widely known as the supreme deity. The rest of the text is easy to understand. — 26

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 37

->In this lotus dwells **Sakini**, pure as the ocean of nectar. Clad in yellow robes, she holds an arrow, a bow, a noose, and a goad in her four lotus-hands. In the **pericarp** <term>the central seed-vessel of the lotus</term> is the full circle of the Moon, free from the mark of the "hare" <note>In Indian mythology, the shadows on the moon are seen as a hare (sasha), rather than a "man in the moon." A moon without the hare is perfectly pure and spotless.</note>. This is the great gate to liberation for those of pure senses and auspicious character. — 30<-

---

This verse describes the presence of the power **Sakini** within the pericarp of the lotus. The phrase "ocean of nectar" <note>original: *sudhasindhoh*</note> indicates her purity. The connection is as follows: the goddess named Sakini dwells within the pericarp of the **Vishuddha Chakra** <term>the "Pure" center at the throat</term>. 

The author further describes her as "pure as the ocean of nectar." This means she possesses a white luster similar to the ocean of nectar. Just as the ocean of nectar is composed of immortal fluid and is cooling and white, so too are the white rays of Sakini, who is of the nature of light, free from scorching heat.

In another meditation text, Sakini is described more specifically: "One should meditate upon the Goddess who is the embodiment of light, adorned with three eyes and five beautiful faces. In her lotus-hands, she holds a noose, a goad, a book, and the **gesture of knowledge** <term>jnana-mudra; a hand position where the index finger and thumb touch</term>. Within the throat-lotus, she intoxicates all **uninitiated people** <term>original: *pashu-jana*; literally 'animal-like people,' referring to those governed by worldly instincts</term>; she resides within the **bone tissue** <note>each goddess of the six chakras is traditionally associated with one of the seven bodily tissues (dhatus)</note>. She delights in offerings of milk-rice and is exhilarated by the intoxication of honey. Thus should the lord of practitioners meditate upon Sakini."

Because light is naturally white, she is described here as being white in color. Regarding the two different descriptions of her weapons <note>the first verse mentions a bow and arrow, while the second mentions a book and a gesture</note>, the choice is left to the will of the practitioner.

This Goddess exists within the lunar circle <term>Chandra-mandala</term> inside the pericarp. This is confirmed in the *Ocean of the Waves of Love-Union* <note>original: *Premayoga-tarangini*</note>: "There dwells the power Sakini in the auspicious circle of the Moon." The verse also notes that within this pericarp, the full, spotless circle of the Moon—the "one without the hare"—shines brilliantly. This circle...

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 38

->In this place, the seeker whose mind is calmed and who has attained complete Yoga by fixing his consciousness here without limit, becomes a great poet, eloquent, and wise. He becomes a seer of the three periods of time <note>original: *trikālānāṃ darśī*; referring to the past, present, and future</note>, a benefactor to all, and is liberated from disease and sorrow. He lives a long life and, like the shining Sun or a Swan, he destroys endless calamities. — 31<-

The author especially praises the results of this meditation with the phrase "Great Liberation" and so forth. This circle is called the Great Gate to Liberation, the Freedom of **Nirvana** <term>the state of final liberation from the cycle of rebirth</term>, for those of pure senses and self-control. The meaning is that by relying on the path of Yoga through meditation on this center, one attains ultimate freedom.

Regarding the phrase "The fortune of one of desired character" <note>original: *shriyam-abhimatashilasya*</note>: this refers to one who desires or attains spiritual "Fortune" <note>original: *Lakshmi*; used here to mean spiritual prosperity or success</note>. This specific meaning applies to the practitioner of pure senses and refined nature.

Here is the arrangement of the Moon-circle and other elements within the **pericarp** <term>the central seed-vessel or "heart" of the lotus</term>:
1. First, within the pericarp is the **Circle of Space** <term>original: *nabhomandala*; the element of Ether associated with the throat</term>.
2. In the center of that is a triangle.
3. Within that triangle is the Moon-circle.
4. In the center of the Moon is the **seed-syllable of Space** <term>the mantra 'Ham'</term>, and so on.

To explain further: One should meditate on the full moon situated within the triangle in the pericarp. Within that, one should meditate on Space as having a golden luster, mounted upon an elephant <note>The elephant is the "vehicle" or animal symbol for the element of Space/Ether</note>. In that space is the god **Sada-Shiva**, wrapped in white garments. Thus, Space itself is envisioned as being enveloped in white light. — 30

The author now explains the fruit of meditating on the **Vishuddha Chakra** <term>the "Pure" center located at the throat</term> with the verse beginning "In this place..." (Verse 31).

Regarding "Complete Yoga of the Self" <note>original: *atmasampurnayoga*</note>: This refers to a practitioner whose knowledge or union (*Yoga*) is complete because it is filled with the all-pervading nature of **Brahman** <term>the ultimate, infinite Reality</term>. "One who has attained complete Yoga" means one who has successfully reached this state of realization. 

As has been stated by the **Venerable Teacher** <note>Likely referring to the philosopher Adi Shankaracharya</note>: "One should remain as the nature of full consciousness, steady like the full and boundless ocean." And then...

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Vishuddha (Throat) Chakra. # Investigation into the Six Chakras 36

The **Sadhaka** <term>spiritual practitioner</term> who, in this place <note>the Vishuddha or Throat Chakra</note>, fixes his consciousness without limit and becomes perfected in **Yoga** <term>the state of spiritual union</term>, becomes a wise man. Even without formal instruction, he becomes a knower of the meaning of all the scriptures. He becomes one of peaceful mind, his consciousness resting upon the qualities of tranquility. This means his mind is endowed with virtues such as: compassion toward all beings, freedom from greed, gentleness, modesty, steadiness, radiance, forgiveness, fortitude, purity, absence of malice, and lack of pride.

Regarding the phrase "Seer of the three periods of time" <note>original: *trikālānāṃ darśī*; referring to the past, present, and future</note>: here, some who follow the path of knowledge say that the practitioner sees everything through the power of Yoga, which functions as a form of worldly perception. Others, however, say that for such a person, because of the direct perception of the Self, all objects of knowledge appearing therein become directly visible to them.

He is "freed from disease and sorrow." Because he has mastered the **Mantra** <term>sacred sound or chant</term>, he is free from illness; because he has cut the "noose of **Maya**" <term>the veil of illusion or worldly deception</term>, he is free from grief. He is a "living being" <note>original: *sajīvī janaḥ*</note> who manifests with great excellence; this applies to all his actions.

Regarding the "destruction of endless calamities": the word "limit" means a boundary; therefore, "endless calamities" are those without end. This refers to the various misfortunes arising from the bondage of actions, both good and bad. For the destruction of these—meaning the cessation of misfortune so that it cannot coexist with the present, resulting in ultimate **Moksha** <term>liberation from the cycle of rebirth</term>—the light of the **Hansa** <term>literally 'Swan'; here referring to the Inner Soul or Antaratma</term> becomes manifest. This light resides within the pericarp of the **Sahasradala** <term>the Thousand-Petaled Lotus at the crown of the head</term>. The rest is clear.

---

Therefore, this is the determination of the meaning of the great teachings: At the root of the throat is the **Vishuddha Chakra** <term>the 'Pure' or Throat center</term>. It has sixteen petals of a smoky-purple color. Its filaments are reddish. It is joined with the sixteen vowels, which are red and marked with the **Bindu** <term>the nasal dot representing concentrated energy</term>.

Within the **pericarp** <term>the central seed-vessel of the lotus</term> is the Circle of Space, which is circular and white. In the center of that is a triangle, and within that is the Moon-circle. Above that is the seed-syllable of Space, *Ham*. It is white, dressed in white garments, and seated upon a white elephant. It has four arms, holding a noose, a goad, and making the gestures of granting boons and dispelling fear.

In the lap of that seed-syllable, seated upon a great lion-throne which rests upon a bull, is **Sada-Shiva** <term>the Eternal Shiva</term>. Because he takes the form of **Ardhanarishvara** <term>the Lord who is half-woman; representing the union of masculine and feminine</term>, one half of his body has the golden luster of the Goddess Gauri, and the other half has the white radiance of Shiva.

<keywords>Vishuddha Chakra, Sada-Shiva, Ardhanarishvara, Hansa, Throat Chakra, Yoga virtues, liberation, Moksha</keywords>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 40

> The lotus named **Ajna** <term>the command center</term> is like the moon, a dwelling-place for meditation that shines with light. Its form is adorned with the two letters 'Ha' and 'Ksha.' It has two petals <note>original: *netra-patram*, literally 'eye-petals'</note> and is beautifully white. Within it is **Hakini**; she is white like the moon, possesses six faces, and holds a book, a ritual gesture, a skull-cup, and a drum. She is of pure heart. **[Verse 32]**

He <note>referring to the deity Sada-Shiva from the previous section</note> has five faces, three eyes, and ten arms. In his ten hands, he holds a trident, a chisel, a sword, a thunderbolt, fire, the king of serpents, a bell, a hook, a noose, and the gesture of fearlessness <note>original: *abhaya-mudra*</note>. He is clothed in a tiger skin, his entire body is smeared with ashes, and he is adorned with a necklace of snakes. He bears a downward-facing half-moon on his head that drips nectar. In the pericarp of this center, within the circle of the moon and situated above him, is the power <term>Shakti</term> named **Sakini**. She is white in color, has four arms—holding a noose, a hook, a bow, and an arrow—wears yellow clothing, and has five faces and three eyes. **[Commentary on Verse 31]**

Now, he describes the **Ajna Chakra** located between the eyebrows in seven verses, beginning with the words "The lotus named Ajna." It is called **Ajna** because it is there that the "Command" <note>original: *ājñā-saṃkramaṇaṃ*</note> of the Guru is communicated. Regarding the name "Ajna": here, because of the transmission of the command between the eyebrows, it is known by the name **Ajna**. The phrase "there is a lotus" is understood by implication.

This lotus is situated between the eyebrows, as it is said: "Entering through the palate and throat and moving upward to the end of the eyebrows is the white, beautiful lotus. It has two petals, is marked by the letters *Ha* and *Ksha*, and is the seat of the Mind."

The author further describes the lotus as being "like the moon" <note>original: *himakara-sadṛśam*</note>, meaning it is pure white in color. Likewise, it is "shining with the light of the dwelling-place of meditation." This means its brilliance comes from the light—the power in the form of light—that belongs to the state of meditation. In this context, its parts are illuminated by the power of meditation like a radiant abode. Because the letters are the seeds of the various divine arts or digits, the word "digits" <note>original: *kalā*</note> is used. The "eye-petaled" <note>original: *netra-patram*</note> refers to it having two petals, and it is "beautifully white."

<meta>This page continues the technical commentary on Verse 32 of the Shatchakra Nirupana, moving from the general description of the Ajna Chakra (Command Center) to the specific qualities of its internal components: the Mind, the letters Ha and Ksha, and the presiding goddess Hakini.</meta>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 41

Within the interior of this lotus resides the **Mind** <note>original: *manas*</note>, famous for its subtle form. In the "womb" <note>the central triangle or yoni</note> of its pericarp is the seat of **Itara-Shiva** <note>a specific form of the god Shiva manifested in this center</note>. The text states it is "very white because of Ha and Ksha." This means that it is naturally white and becomes exceedingly brilliant through its association with the letters **Ha** and **Ksha** <note>the two Sanskrit syllables written on the petals of this chakra</note>. This implies that the letters Ha and Ksha themselves are white, since an intensity of whiteness can only occur by adding white to white.

Alternatively, the **Ajna Chakra** is like the "maker of frost" <note>original: *himakara*, a poetic name for the Moon</note>, possessing cool rays like the nectar-filled rays of the moon, and is thus beautifully white. In this view, the letters Ha and Ksha should be understood as being variegated or multi-colored <note>original: *karbura*</note>. This is confirmed in the **Dialogue between Ishvara and Kartikeya**: "Above that is the Ajna Chakra, white and adorned with two petals. It shines with the variegated letters Ha and Ksha and is colored by the presiding Mind."

Regarding the presence of the power <term>Shakti</term> named **Hakini** in the pericarp of this center, the text says, "Within that..." She is famously known by the name Hakini. She holds a book <note>representing knowledge or *vidya*</note>, a ritual gesture <note>a *mudra*</note>, and the gestures of granting boons and fearlessness. Because one sees six types of implements in her hands in this description, she is six-armed. 

However, some scholars read the phrase "gesture of knowledge" <note>original: *vidyā-mudrām*</note> as a single compound word. They interpret this as the "gesture of exposition" <note>the teaching mudra</note> and argue that she has only four arms. Yet, in various manuscripts, the words are written with separate grammatical endings, a point which wise students should consider carefully.

He further describes the details of Hakini through another meditation verse <note>a *dhyana* verse used for visualization</note>:

->One should meditate upon that Goddess **Hakini**, who dwells within the chakra and is white in color. She holds a small drum, a rosary of beads, a skull-cup, a book, and a ritual gesture. She possesses six faces, each shining with three eyes. She is fond of food prepared with turmeric and is joyful, intoxicated by honey <note>or divine nectar</note>. Seated firmly upon a white lotus, she is delighted by the "honey-ocean" of the King of the Gods.<-

The rest of the meaning is clear. **[Verse 32]**

Regarding the state of the Mind in this lotus, he says, "Within the interior of this lotus..." The term "subtle form" means it is beyond the reach of the physical senses <note>original: *atīndriyam*</note>. Now, one might ask: since the mind is beyond the senses, its existence...

<meta>This page continues the detailed exploration of the Ajna Chakra (the Command Center located between the eyebrows). Following the description of the goddess Hakini, the text now focuses on the internal symbols of the center: the Itara-linga (a subtle form of Shiva) and the Pranava (the sacred sound Om).</meta>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 42

> Manifested by the sign of the phallus <term>linga</term>, possessing the splendor of a chain of lightning; the supreme seat of the creative power <term>Kula</term>, the thread of Brahman, the awakening, the primordial seed of the Vedas; one should meditate upon it with a very steady heart, in due order. || 33 ||

By what evidence is this known? The author says it is **"well-known."** This refers to knowledge that is famous because it has been received through an unbroken lineage of ancient masters who realized it through direct perception. Furthermore, it is proven by the authority of the scriptures, which state: "The mind is that which performs both resolve and doubt." Here, the location of the **Mind** is said to be above the **Seed of the Vedas** <note>the syllable Om</note> described hereafter, according to the specific statements that follow.

For this reason, the author describes the position of the Shiva-linga: "in the womb <note>the central triangle</note> of its pericarp." Within the triangle at the center of the lotus resides the "Seat of Itara-Shiva," an entity whose nature is known by that specific name. This connects back to the previous description.

The phrase **"manifested by the sign of the phallus"** means it shines forth in the form of a phallus. This Linga is white in color. As the text *Purification of the Elements* <note>original: *Bhuta-shuddhi*</note> states: "Within that is the Itara-linga, crystal-like and possessing three eyes."

The Linga is further qualified as **"having the splendor of a chain of lightning."**

The author now describes the location of the **Om** <term>Pranava</term> by saying **"the supreme,"** etc. The meaning is that the **Primordial Seed of the Vedas**—the sound Om—also resides within the pericarp.

He describes this Seed as the **"supreme seat of Kula."** *Kula* refers to the Power <term>Shakti</term>; here, it refers specifically to the triangular seat. Thus, it is the "Supreme Seat of Kula" because its location is a triangle that excels due to its lightning-like form. The conclusion is that the Seed-syllable resides in the middle of this triangle.

This is stated clearly in other texts: "In the pericarp, one finds the Self <note>Atman</note> staying within a triangle in the shape of the Om-sound. It is like a burning lamp, facing upwards, a beautiful form of Sound <term>Nada</term>. The Dot <term>Bindu</term> <gloss>the nasal M-sound at the end of Om</gloss> is the form of the 'M' sound, and above that is the abode of the Mind."

Now, if it is argued that the "Supreme Seat of Kula" is inseparable from the support of the Om-sound, then one must consider the **Root Support** <note>Muladhara</note>, the **Self-Abode** <note>Swadhisthana</note>, the **Jewel-City** <note>Manipura</note>, the **Unstruck** <note>Anahata</note>, the **Pure** <note>Vishuddha</note>, and the **Command Center** <note>Ajna Chakra</note>, as well as the Dot...

<meta>This page transitions from the physical and symbolic description of the Ajna Chakra (the Third Eye) to the specific fruits of meditating upon it. It also provides a technical clarification on the sequence of meditation and the location of subtle elements like the Chitrini channel and the Sixteen Bases of the body.</meta>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 43

> He whose soul is meditation becomes a lord among practitioners <term>sadhakendra</term> and a lord among sages <term>munindra</term>, able to enter the bodies of others <note>literally "another's city"</note> at will. He is all-knowing and all-seeing, a benefactor to all, and a master of the meaning of all scriptures. Devoted to the practice of non-duality <note>original: advaita-achara-vadi</note>, he shines with the fame of supreme and extraordinary spiritual powers. Long-lived, he himself becomes the creator, sustainer, and destroyer of the three worlds. || 34 ||

---

The **position of the digits** <term>kalapada</term>. The "Awakener" <term>nibodhika</term>, the "Half-moon" <term>ardhendu</term>, the "Sound" <term>nada</term>, and the "End of Sound" <term>nadanta</term>; the "Mind-transcending" <term>unmani</term>, the "Cakra of Vishnu," and "Shiva of the Brow-Circle." In the statement regarding these, one might ask how the "position of the digits" <note>kalapada</note> is mentioned separately in the counting of the **sixteen bases** <term>shodasha-adhara</term> <note>the sixteen vital points of the body used in Hatha Yoga</note>.

The answer is: No, that **position of the digits** is not located within the Ajna Chakra, but rather in the region of the Void above the Great Sound <term>maha-nada</term> which will be described later. We will clarify this when discussing the Great Sound. 

Regarding the phrase **"awakening the thread of Brahman"**: the **thread of Brahman** <note>Brahma-sutra</note> refers to the *Chitrini* channel <term>nadi</term>. Its "awakening" means its illumination. This is why it was previously described as being "manifested by the sacred syllable Om" <note>original: pranava-vilasita</note>. 

The author states that "one should meditate upon it... in due order." This refers to the symbols previously shown: the Goddess Hakini, the Mind, and the seeds of the *Itara-linga* and the Vedas <note>the syllable Om</note>. One should contemplate all of these with a very steady heart, following the sequence. 

The "order" mentioned here is not simply the order in which the words appear in the text. Rather, because the sequence of the actual sounds/vibrations <note>shabda-krama</note> is more powerful than the sequence of the written words <note>patha-krama</note>, the sequence of the sounds is followed. 

Therefore, one should meditate in this order: first, the Power <term>shakti</term> **Hakini** within the pericarp of the lotus; above her, in a triangle, the **Itara-linga**; above that, in another triangle, the **sacred syllable Om** <term>pranava</term>; and above that, the **Mind** <term>manas</term>. || 33 ||

The author now describes the result of this meditation in the verse beginning with **"He whose soul is meditation..."** One who is devoted to meditation in the manner described above becomes a "lord among practitioners." The ability to "quickly enter the bodies of others" is a power that applies to all such perfected ones. A "lord among sages" <note>munindra</note> is a sage <note>muni</note> who is fully endowed with the yoga of meditation and its perfections.

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 44

...he is the most excellent, all-knowing, and all-seeing in the manner previously described. Through a wisdom of discrimination <term>viveka-buddhi</term> that accords with the methods of scriptural practice, he perceives both the past and the future. Regarding the phrase **"one who speaks of the practice of non-duality"** <note>original: advaita-achara-vadi</note>: this refers to the determination of non-duality based on Vedic scriptural statements such as "A quarter of Him is all beings" <note>Rig Veda 10.90.3: pado'sya vishva bhutani</note> and "All this is indeed Brahman." 

It also refers to statements such as: "I am the Divine and not another; I am indeed Brahman and do not suffer." By such words, one determines that Brahman alone is the one true Reality <term>Sat</term>, and everything else is unreal <term>Asat</term>, for the entire collection of the perceived world appears only as a reflection of Brahman. Non-duality <term>Advaita</term> is this firm conviction. One who practices according to such knowledge—specifically through the Yoga of meditating on the unity of the individual soul <term>jivatman</term> and the supreme soul <term>paramatman</term>—and who is inclined to speak of this truth, is called a "practitioner of non-duality." This is the intended meaning.

The phrase "attainment of the supreme and extraordinary" refers to the **Great Power** <term>mahasiddhi</term> for which he is primarily famous. The statement that "He himself is the creator, sustainer, and destroyer in the house of the three worlds" is intended as praise. Alternatively, it means that because the practitioner's soul merges into the Supreme Soul <term>Paramatman</term> at the end of the body’s life, he becomes one with the very Being who performs creation, maintenance, and dissolution. || 34 ||

---

Desiring to explain the union with the Sacred Syllable <term>Pranava</term>—the sound *Om*—within the Command Center <term>Ajna Chakra</term>, the author first qualifies the nature of this syllable:

> Within this chakra dwells the Inner Soul <term>Antaratman</term>, eternally pure and wise. Its light is like that of a lamp, appearing in the form of the syllable Om <term>Pranava</term>. Above it is the Half-moon, and above that shines the *Bindu* <note>the 'point' of concentrated energy</note> in the form of the letter 'M'. Foremost among them is the Sound <term>Nada</term>, which laughs like a continuous stream of white nectar. || 35 ||

**"Within that"** <note>tad-antar</note>: the meaning of this is that inside this chakra, in the middle of the previously mentioned triangle, is the light of the letter-form that composes the Syllable Om. This form is created by the union <note>sandhi</note> of the letters 'A' and 'U'...
