# Investigation into the Six Chakras 67

> ...That accomplished man, whose mind is disciplined, shall always possess full power; he shall have the power to create and to destroy, the ability to move through the air, and speech that is perfectly pure. ॥ 45 ॥

> Here resides the sixteenth digit of the moon; she is the sister of the young sun, pure and resembling the hundredth part of a thread from a lotus stalk. [46]

In the three worlds—heaven, earth, and the underworld—he is no longer bound nor takes on a physical form <note>original: *na śarīrī bhavati*; meaning he is no longer subject to the cycle of reincarnation</note>. He remains as one **liberated while living** <term>Jivanmukta; a state where one is spiritually free although the physical body still exists</term> until the fruits of his past actions <term>Prarabdha-karma; the portion of past karma that is currently being experienced in the present life</term> are exhausted. Upon the death of the body, he attains final liberation. 

Regarding the fact that neither merit nor sin arises for such a person, the **Kularnava Tantra** states: "Even by a hundred horse-sacrifices <note>original: *aśvamedha*; the highest Vedic merit-making ritual</note> or a hundred slayings of a priest <note>original: *brahma-hatyā*; considered the gravest possible sin</note>, those in whose heart the Supreme <note>original: *Brahma*</note> dwells are not tainted by merit or sin." 

Similarly, in the **Bhagavad Gita**: "He has no purpose to gain by what is done, nor any by what is not done in this world; nor does he depend on any being for any purpose" [3.18]. The **Subodhini** <note>a famous commentary on the Bhagavad Gita</note> explains this: for the one who has attained the **Knowledge of Truth** <term>Tattva-jnana</term>, no merit comes from actions performed, and no sin or obstacle arises from the failure to perform daily rituals.

The scripture <term>Shruti</term> also speaks of the destruction of accumulated merit and sin: "When the mind is dissolved and all mental constructs <note>original: *sankalpa-vikalpa*</note> are burnt away, merit and sin vanish; [there remains only] **Sadasiva**, whose essence is Power <note>original: *shaktyātma*</note>, the Peaceful One." 

In the **Bhagavad Gita** it is also said: "The fire of knowledge reduces all actions <note>original: *karma*</note> to ashes" [4.37]. 

For such a person, there is "full power," meaning a power that can accomplish all things. The text demonstrates the effect of this power with the phrase "always able to create." This means he has the power to create desired things and to "destroy" or withdraw undesired things. "Movement through the air" <note>original: *khagatiḥ*; a supernatural power or siddhi</note> refers to the ability to travel through the celestial path. His "perfectly pure speech" refers to a mastery of beautiful prose and poetry. This applies to all his endeavors. ॥ 45 ॥

Now, here, inside the triangle within the pericarp of the **Thousand-Petaled Lotus** <term>Sahasrara</term>—which was previously described as "the void within that" and the "pure manifestation of all digits of the moon"—there sits...

<summary>This page describes the state of a Jivanmukta (one liberated while living), who is beyond the reach of merit and sin and possesses supernatural powers like creation, destruction, and flight. It introduces the "sixteenth digit of the moon" located within the Sahasrara's central triangle.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the detailed analysis of the Sahasrara (the thousand-petalled lotus at the crown of the head). # Investigation into the Six Chakras 68

### ...Supreme! Her body is delicate like ten million flashes of lightning; she shines facing downwards and bears the stream of nectar trickling from the sequence of perfect bliss. ॥ 46 ॥

### The supreme digit called Nirvana resides within her; she is more excellent than the supreme—

The author explains that the **Ama-kala** <term>the sixteenth digit of the moon; representing the point where the lunar and solar energies meet</term> and the **Nirvana-kala** <term>the digit of liberation; an even subtler energy within the crown chakra</term> are the supreme foundations of the **Bindus** <term>points of concentrated spiritual power</term>, as described previously in general terms. Now, wishing to clarify their specific nature and the qualities that distinguish them from others, he first describes the nature of the Ama-kala with the words "Here resides..."

The phrase "sister of the young sun" <note>original: śiśu-sūrya-sodara</note> indicates that this digit is red in color. The phrase "the sixteenth [digit] of the moon" <note>original: candrasya ṣoḍaśī</note> identifies her as the Ama-kala. The mention of the "lotus stalk thread" <note>original: nīraja-tantu</note> should be understood as a fiber split lengthwise a hundred times to illustrate her extreme fineness.

She is pure, free from any stains <note>original: kalaṅka-rahitā; referring to the "spots" on the moon, symbolizing her absolute purity</note>, and is a form of the Supreme Power of Consciousness <term>Chic-shakti; the energy of pure awareness</term>. Indeed, she is the very embodiment of that power. Therefore, it is stated in the **Prabhasa-khanda** <note>a section of the Skanda Purana, a classic Hindu scripture</note>: "The supreme Maya <note>the creative power of the divine</note> resides within the bodies of the embodied," which serves as a description of the Ama-kala.

The "sequence of eternal bliss" <note>original: nityānanda-paramparā</note> means she is born from the lineage of the Ultimate Reality <note>original: Brahma</note>. "Bearing the stream of trickling nectar" <note>original: ati-vigalat-pīyūṣa-dhārā-dharā</note> means she is the source of an abundant flow of the nectar of immortality <note>original: amṛta</note>.

Alternatively, the entire phrase can be read as a single description: "She who bears the stream of nectar trickling from the sequence of eternal bliss." The meaning is this: Shiva is the Supreme Point <term>Bindu</term> and Shakti is the Supreme Nature <term>Prakriti</term>. Through the union of Shiva and Shakti in perfectly balanced bliss <note>original: samarasa-ānanda</note>, the "stream of nectar"—the supreme nectar of immortality—flows forth. She holds this stream constantly and without interruption. The rest of the verse is clear. ॥ 46 ॥

The author now describes the nature of the **Nirvana-kala** <term>the digit of final liberation</term> with the words "The digit named Nirvana." She is supreme, the most excellent, the highest digit, being even more superior than the previously mentioned...

<summary>This page provides a detailed commentary on Verse 46 of the Shat-cakra-nirupana, describing the Ama-kala as a radiant, red, and incredibly fine energy source that distributes divine nectar. It transitions into Verse 47, introducing the Nirvana-kala, which represents an even higher state of spiritual subtlety.</summary>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 68

> ...being the thousandth part of the tip of a hair split a thousand times. She is the presiding deity of all beings, the Goddess who is the dawn of eternal awakening. She is curved like the half-moon and possesses a brilliance equal to all the suns shining at once. ॥ 47 ॥

In the reading, this means she is the "most excellent of all." She is located within—situated in the lap of the **Ama-kala** <term>the sixteenth digit of the moon; the point of union between solar and lunar energies</term>. Within that is the curved seventeenth digit called **Nirvana** <term>liberation; the final stage of consciousness</term>, as mentioned in previous statements. Regarding "the tip of a hair" <note>original: keśāgrasya</note> and so on, the connection is that she remains as subtle as a single part of a hair split into a thousand pieces. "The presiding deity of all beings" <note>original: bhūtānām-adhidaivatam</note> means she is the very form of the consciousness residing in the heart. "The dawn of eternal awakening" <note>original: nitya-prabodhodaya</note> indicates she is the one who brings about the knowledge of ultimate Truth. "Curved like the half-moon" <note>original: candrārdhāṅga-samāna-bhaṅguravatī</note> means she possesses a curved shape like the **Ama-kala**, which itself is the form of half the moon. "Brilliance equal to all the suns" <note>original: sarvārkat-tulya-prabhā</note> means she shines with a red color, similar to the light produced if twelve suns were to rise simultaneously. ॥ 47 ॥

The author describes the form of the supreme **Bindu** <term>the metaphysical point of infinite potential</term> with the words "Within her..." In the middle space—the lap—of this **Nirvana-kala**, shines the supreme, primordial **Nirvana-shakti** <term>the power of liberation</term>. She is called primordial <note>original: apūrvā</note> because she appeared at the very beginning of creation, and nothing existed before her. She is the Supreme Brahman in the form of Power. 

The author further qualifies the **Nirvana-shakti**: "The brilliance of ten million suns" <note>original: koṭyāditya-prakāśā</note>. "The Mother of the three worlds" <note>original: tribhuvana-jananī</note> means she is the place of origin for the entire manifest universe, comprising the heavens, the earth, and the underworld. Because she is equal to the ten-millionth part of the tip of a hair, she is extremely subtle. She is "the bearer of the unending stream of overflowing love." "Love" <note>original: prema</note> here signifies the blossoming of the state of Bliss.

<summary>This page describes the Nirvana-kala and Nirvana-shakti, the increasingly subtle layers of consciousness within the crown chakra, emphasizing their extreme fineness and blinding solar brilliance.</summary>
<keywords>Nirvana-kala, Nirvana-shakti, Sahasrara Chakra, Bindu, Ama-kala, Divine Mother, Supreme Brahman, Kundalini Yoga</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the description of the Sahasrara (crown chakra), moving from the Nirvana-kala to the even more subtle Nirvana-shakti. It explains the relationship between the individual soul (Jiva) and the supreme energy, and details the hierarchical structure of the subtle sounds (Nada) and points of light (Bindu) within the crown.</meta>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 70

> She is like a ten-millionth part [of a hair]. Extremely subtle, even more than the tip of a hair, she holds a continuous stream of flowing nectar <note>original: prema-dhārā, literally "stream of love," here interpreted as the nectar of bliss</note>. She is the soul-form of all beings and joyfully carries the realization of Truth into the minds of sages. ॥ 48 ॥

The "stream of supreme nectar" <note>original: paramāmṛta-dhārā</note> refers to that which is produced by the blossoming of spiritual emotions through the bliss of divine sap. The verse means she sustains such a stream, flowing without end or interruption. 

"Soul-form of all beings" <note>original: sarveṣāṃ jīva-bhūtā</note>: The individual soul <term>Jiva; the finite living spark</term> is merely a fragment of this Shakti. As it has been said: "Just as sparks fly out from a blazing fire, O Goddess, [so do souls arise from Her]. When that supreme Point <term>Bindu; the metaphysical seed of creation</term>—which is beyond hearing—falls even upon the earth, then suddenly, O Goddess, it becomes endowed with consciousness." This means that the one called "Soul" is actually the consciousness derived from the Shakti residing within the supreme Point, which is the union of Shiva <term>the masculine principle of pure consciousness</term> and Shakti <term>the feminine principle of creative energy</term>. The rest is clear. 

This **Nirvana-shakti** is situated in the place below the **Nirvana-kala** and above the **Nibodhika** <term>the Awakener; a subtle form of sound energy</term> which is in the form of sound (*Nada*). 

To explain: "**Nibodhika**, having the nature of fire, is located within the Nirvana-sphere. Above the unmanifest sound is she who has the great brilliance of ten million suns <unclear>koti-aditya-uru-prabha</unclear>. **Nirvana-shakti** is the supreme, the womb-form <note>source or origin</note> of all. Within that Shakti, one should recognize **Shiva**, who is changeless and stainless. It is here that the **Kundalini-shakti** sports with the Supreme Soul."

**Nibodhika** is composed of unmanifest sound and has the nature of fire. Furthermore, the teacher **Raghava-bhatta** <note>a renowned 15th-century commentator on Tantric philosophy</note> explains that Sound (*Nada*) has three states: 
1. **Unmanifest Sound** <note>original: avyakta-nāda</note>, which is pure sound/vibration due to a predominance of the quality of inertia <term>Tamas; the guna of darkness or potentiality</term>.
2. **Nibodhika**, which is sound slightly bound to letters/articulation due to a predominance of the quality of passion <term>Rajas; the guna of activity and movement</term>.
3. **The Point** <note>original: Bindu</note>, which is the concentrated form due to a predominance of the quality of goodness <term>Sattva; the guna of light and harmony</term>.

Consequently, Sound, the Point, and the Awakener (*Nada, Bindu, Nibodhika*) take the forms of the Sun, the Moon, and Fire. According to the words of the *Sharada-tilaka* <note>a classic manual of Tantric liturgy</note>, these three also represent the energies of Knowledge, Will, and Action. Therefore, by describing her position as being above the fire-formed **Nibodhika**, it is stated that the **Nirvana-shakti** is situated above the spheres of the Sun, Moon, and Fire.

It details the metaphysical hierarchy of sound and light (Nada, Bindu, and Nibodhika) within the highest chakra, situating the Shakti above the energies of the Sun, Moon, and Fire.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the detailed exploration of the Sahasrara (the thousand-petaled lotus at the crown of the head). # Investigation into the Six Chakras 71

### The Place of Shiva

Within the middle space of that [Nirvana-shakti] is the stainless and eternal Place of Shiva <note>original: Śiva-pada</note>, which is accessible only to yogis. It is known by the name of Eternal Bliss, filled with every kind of happiness, and its very nature is Pure Awakening <note>original: śuddha-bodha; pure consciousness or enlightenment</term>. Some wise ones call this the place named Brahman; others, of steady mind, speak of it as the Place of Vishnu <note>original: Vaiṣṇava-padam</note>. Some call it by the name of Hamsa <term>Hamsa; the "mystical swan," a symbol of the soul and the breath "So-ham" (I am He)</term>, and some virtuous ones call it Liberation or the Awakening of the Self. ॥ 49 ॥

The wise should meditate upon this. This is clearly stated in the *Kularnava Tantra* <note>an important historical text of the Kaula tantric tradition</note> regarding the meditation on the Supreme Brahman: "The Supreme Brahman, in the form of a Point <term>Bindu; a metaphysical seed-point of infinite potential</term>, is situated in the thousand-petaled lotus." The passage begins by saying it is "adorned by the three circles within the triangle inside the pericarp <note>the central seed-pod or core of the lotus</note>." By "three circles," it means the circles of the Sun, the Moon, and Fire. We will later clarify that this **Nirvana-shakti** is indeed the form of the Supreme Point. ॥ 48 ॥ <note>The commentator here links the previous verse's subject to the current description.</note>

Now the author describes the location of the Supreme Brahman within the **Nirvana-shakti** in the phrase *Within the middle space of that*. "Of that" refers to the **Nirvana-shakti** which is in the form of the Supreme Point. "Within the middle space" means the void situated in its center. The knowers of Truth call this the "stainless"—meaning free from the impurity of illusion <term>Maya; the power that creates the appearance of the material world</term>—"Place of Shiva" or the Abode of Brahman. It is "accessible to yogis" because, being subtler than the subtlest and beyond the reach of speech and mind, it is reachable only through the refined knowledge of practitioners. 

Some, following the path of Vedanta <note>the philosophical school focused on the ultimate unity of the soul and the Divine</note>, call it by the name "Brahman." Alternatively, it is called "Hamsa," as per the literal meaning of the term in sacred tradition. Other virtuous people describe it as the "Awakening of the Self," the place of direct realization of one's true nature. It is called "Liberation" <note>original: mokṣa</note> because it is there that one is released and stands outside the illusory expansion of the world. 

Here, one should pay attention to this: within the triangle in the pericarp of the thousand-petaled lotus, there is the Supreme Point, which consists of both the Feminine Principle <term>Prakriti; primordial matter</term> and the Masculine Principle <term>Purusha; pure consciousness</term>, though they are covered by the bonds of illusion. As it has been said: "In the World of Truth <note>original: Satyaloka; the highest plane of existence</note>, she is formless..."

<summary>This page describes the "Place of Shiva" located at the very center of the crown chakra, identifying it as the ultimate goal of yoga where the soul achieves liberation and pure consciousness. It explains that this state is given various names—Brahman, Vishnu, or Hamsa—depending on the philosophical tradition, but all refer to the same stainless reality.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the description of the Sahasrara, moving from the identity of the Supreme Point (Bindu) to its role as the source of all creation and its various names across different philosophical traditions.</meta>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 72

She is the nature of Great Light <note>original: mahājyotiḥ-svarūpiṇī</note>. Veiling herself with Illusion <note>original: māyā</note>, she takes the **shape of a chickpea** <term>caṇaka-ākāra; a chickpea has two halves (representing Shiva and Shakti) held together by a single skin (Maya)</term>. Without hands or feet, she embodies the Moon, the Sun, and Fire. When she turns upward and casts off the bonds of illusion, she splits in two; by this division into Shiva and Shakti, she manifests for the purpose of creation. This takes place in the Sahasrara, in the Realm of Truth <note>original: Satyaloka</note>.

Thus, the Point <note>original: bindu</note>, which is without qualities <note>nirguṇa</note>, is indeed the cause of all spiritual perfection. Some call this Brahma, some speak of it as Vishnu, and some as Rudra—the Great Fullness, the one unstained God. Because the Primordial Power <note>original: ādyā-śakti</note> takes the form of a chickpea, [the Point is her seed].

It is the author's view that the **Nirvana-shakti** is a portion of subtle light, equivalent to a ten-millionth part of the tip of a hair, and the central void within her is the **Place of Brahman** <note>original: Brahma-pada</note>.

As stated elsewhere:
"In its center is the Supreme Point, which embodies creation, preservation, and dissolution. Shiva is the manifest void; the Point is the Supreme Kundalini <note>original: Parama-kuṇḍalī</note>."
Likewise:
"The Kundalini power is circular, endowed with the three qualities <term>guṇas; the three fundamental forces of nature: purity, activity, and inertia</term>. The void-part, O Goddess, is the essence of Shiva and Shakti."

Some say this Point is the Lord <note>original: īśvara</note>, the cause of everything. Scholars of the Puranas <note>ancient historical and mythological texts</note> say this is the form of Great Vishnu. Others say this is the Spirit of Brahman <note>original: Brahma-puruṣa</note>. 

As it is said:
"Then there was neither day nor night, nor sky nor earth; there was neither darkness nor light nor anything else. There was only the one Primal Brahman-Spirit, ungraspable by the senses or the intellect."

The eternal Shiva is known as both without qualities <note>nirguṇa</note> and with qualities <note>saguṇa</note>. According to the *Sharada* <note>referring to the *Sharada Tilaka*, a seminal Tantric text</note>, "He who is without qualities is beyond Nature <note>Prakṛti</note>; he with qualities is known as the manifest universe." Thus, in the center [of the lotus] is the form of **Brahman with qualities**. In truth, there is only one reality, though it is sung by various names due to differences in doctrine. Let this be enough elaboration.

This concludes the determination of the meaning of the Great Sayings <note>Mahāvākyas; the core philosophical statements of the Upanishads</note>. Above the Sushumna channel is the **Thousand-petaled Lotus**, white in color and facing downward. It is beautified by red filaments and is marked with the fifty letters of the alphabet from *A* to *La*. These fifty white letters, repeated twenty times, make up the thousand petals.

It reconciles different sectarian names for this ultimate reality (Brahma, Vishnu, Shiva) and describes the physical appearance of the thousand-petaled lotus.</summary>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 73

->**Investigation into the Six Chakras**<-

In the center of this lotus is the **Swan** <term>Hamsa; a symbol of the individual soul and the breath</term>, and there also is the Guru who is the very form of **Supreme Shiva** <note>original: Parama-shiva-rupa-guru</note>. Beyond that are the solar orb and the lunar orb; then comes the **Great Breath** <note>original: Mahā-vāyu</note>, and then the **Aperture of Brahman** <term>Brahmarandhra; the spiritual opening at the top of the head through which the soul is said to depart at death</term>. Beyond that is the **Great Shankhini** <note>a specific subtle channel or energy force</note>. Within the lunar orb, there is a triangle with the brilliance of lightning.

In the center of that triangle is the subtle **sixteenth digit of the moon** <term>ṣoḍaśī kalā; also known as Amā-kalā</term>. She faces downward, is red in hue, and is as thin as the hundredth part of a single fiber of a lotus stalk. Within her "lap" (her inner space) is the **Nirvana-digit** <note>original: Nirvāna-kalā</note>, which is as subtle as the thousandth part of the tip of a hair; she too is red and faces downward. Below her is the fire known as **Nibodhika** <note>the "awakener"</note>, which consists of unmanifest sound <note>original: avyakta-nāda</note>.

Above that, in the lap of the Nirvana-digit, is the **Supreme Point** <note>original: Para-bindu</note>, which is the union of Shiva and Shakti. Within this Point is the **Nirvana-shakti** <note>the power of liberation</note>, a subtle light in the form of a swan, as fine as the ten-millionth part of the tip of a hair. Her "swan" is the individual soul <note>original: jīva</note>. The void in the very center of the Point is the **Place of Brahman** <note>original: Brahma-pada</note>.

According to the school of the *Five Branches of the Desire-Tree of Tradition* <note>original: Āgama-kalpa-druma-pañca-śākhā</note>:
In the center of the thousand-petalled lotus, within the lunar orb, is the "A-Ka-Tha" triangle <term>a-ka-tha-di trikoṇa; a mystical triangle formed by the letters of the Sanskrit alphabet</term>. Near this triangle is the **Triple-Point** <note>Tri-bindu</note>. The lower point is the syllable *Ha*, representing the **Male Principle** <note>original: puruṣa</note>. The two upper points form the *Visarga* <note>a Sanskrit grammatical mark (ः) representing two points</note>, which is the syllable *Sa*, representing **Nature** <note>original: prakṛti</note>.

The **Swan** <note>Hamsa</note>, which is the union of both Male and Nature, shines forth in the form of this Triple-Point. Within it is the *Ama* digit, and in her lap is the Nirvana-shakti; the void in her center is the **Supreme Brahman**.

As it has been said:
"Now, in the white Thousand-Petalled Lotus <note>Sahasrāra</note>, which shines like the full moon and flashes with the light of lightning, are the letters from *A* to *Ksha*. Within that is the **Supreme Void**, situated below the two points of the *Visarga*. There is the sixteenth digit, facing downward, shining like the rising sun. She truly pours forth a stream of nectar, curved like a crescent moon. Within her is the **Supreme Power** <note>Para-shakti</note>, radiant as ten million suns, as thin as a thousandth part of a lotus fiber, and composed of the very essence of consciousness. Inside her is **Being-Consciousness-Bliss** <note>Sat-Chit-Ananda</note>, who is beyond the Vedas and unstained. The Point is called the Male Principle, and the *Visarga* is considered Nature. The Swan, being the union of Male and Nature, shines by its own light."

Relying on this school of thought, above the Supreme Point and over the *Visarga* that constitutes the Swan, the Guru—

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 74

->**Investigation into the Six Chakras**<-

By the sound of **'Hum'** <note>original: huṅkāreṇa</note> alone, the Goddess is reached by one who is devoted to the practice of restraints and observances <term>Yama and Niyama; the ethical foundations and self-disciplines of Yoga</term>. This virtuous practitioner, having learned the sequence from the mouth of the **Honored Lord** <note>original: śrīnātha-vaktrāt; referring to the Guru</note>, reveals the path to Great Liberation. Being of a nature of pure intellect, he leads her into the middle of the **Aperture of Brahman** <term>Brahma-dvāra; the 'Gate of Brahman', the entry point to the highest consciousness</term>, having pierced that **Linga-form** <note>the Svayambhu-linga, a subtle pillar of energy</note> which was previously hidden by the movement of air and fire. ॥ 50 ॥

...is the state, so they say. In that regard, it is appropriate. In the **Nirvana Tantra**, it is stated that the personal Guru resides near the Power in the form of the **Point** <term>Bindu; the concentrated seed of consciousness</term>, and is devoted to her worship and meditation. Thus, sitting for the purpose of worship is fittingly done in a place below and directly in front, rather than in a high place or behind. For it is said in the *Nirvana*: "In the **World of Truth** <term>Satya-loka</term>, in the seed-vessel of the lotus, in the auspicious house of thought-jewels <note>original: cintāmaṇi-gṛhe</note>, one should meditate on the stainless Goddess seated upon a jeweled throne. Near her is one's own Guru, devoted to worship and meditation," and so on.

Furthermore, in the previously cited passage from the **Mahakali Tantra**, the position of the Guru above the two letters of the **Swan** <note>the syllables 'Ham' and 'Sah'</note> is clearly expressed. Here, any statements that seem to describe a sequence contrary to the one from the **Root Center** <term>Mūlādhāra</term> up to the **Thousand-petalled Lotus** <term>Sahasrāra</term>, or any additional details provided, should be understood as prescriptions for alternative methods according to different traditions. ॥ 48 ॥

***

Now, having described the chakras up to the Thousand-petalled Lotus, the author intends to describe the union with **Kundalini** <term>the primordial energy coiled at the base of the spine</term>, and first explains the method of awakening her.

**"By the sound of 'Hum'..."** The meaning is as follows: A person well-known as a practitioner of Yoga, having learned the sequence from the mouth of the Guru—the sequence by which the movement of air and fire is achieved through the contraction of the heart and other centers, leading to the awakening of Kundalini—having learned that sequence from the mouth of the Guru, the Honored Lord... <note>The text continues describing the manipulation of internal "Air" (Prana) and "Fire" (Agni) on the following page.</note>

<meta>This page continues the technical commentary on Verse 50 of the Shat-Chakra-Nirupana. While the previous page established that the practitioner must be ethically prepared (through Yama and Niyama) and guided by a Guru, this page details the specific yogic mechanics of moving the soul (Jivatman) and the Kundalini energy from the heart and root centers into the central channel.</meta>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 75

->**Investigation into the Six Chakras**<-

By the sequence and by the utterance of the **'Hum'** <term>Kūrccha-bīja; the 'seed-syllable' of power, often associated with piercing obstacles</term>, one raises the Kundalini. This means that after piercing the form of that **Linga** <note>the Svayambhu-linga at the root</note> and bursting open its orifice, he places or leads that Goddess Kundalini into the middle of the **Gate of Brahman** <term>Brahma-dvāra</term>, which is within the mouth of the **Citrini channel** <term>Citrini-nāḍī; the subtle innermost tube within the central spine</term>. 

Regarding the phrase **"by the sound Hum"**: "Then, remembering the **Hamsa** <term>Haṃsa; literally 'Swan', a mantra representing the breath and the soul</term>, one should slowly contract the anus." Based on such statements from the **Tree of Ritual Discovery** <note>original: Āgama-kalpadruma</note>, which prescribe the movement of Kundalini through the utterance of the Hamsa mantra, the author of the **Secret of Lalita** <note>original: Lalitā-rahasya</note> states that the mantra "Ham Hamsa" is employed in moving the Kundalini. 

However, because the instruction is to contract [the muscles] after first remembering the Hamsa, the intent is this: by remembering the mantra "Hamsa," one should bring the **individual soul** <term>Jīvātman</term>—which resides in the heart in the form of a tiny **flame of a lamp** <note>original: dīpakalikākāra</note>—down to the **Root Chakra** <term>Mūlādhāra</term>. The practitioner should then move the Kundalini along with that soul. 

This is why it is said in a later passage of the **Tree of Ritual Discovery**: "The chief of practitioners, raising the Power from her lotus-abode **with the self**..." In this context, "with the self" means "along with the individual soul." 

In the **Nectar of the Kali Family** <note>original: Kālī-kulāmṛta</note>, it is also said: "Having led the soul from the heart to the mouth of the lotus with the Hamsa mantra, and having raised the supreme Goddess Kundalini with the sound 'Hum'..." 

In the **Tantra of the Garland of Skeletons** <note>original: Kaṅkāla-mālinī-tantra</note>: "Having drawn the individual soul with the **Pranava** <term>Oṃ; the primordial sound</term>, O Daughter of the Mountain, the practitioner takes the life-force and the sense of smell along with Kundalini. With the mantra 'So'ham' <note>meaning 'I am He'</note>, he should enter the Goddess into the **Support-of-the-Self** center <term>Svādhiṣṭhāna; the second chakra, located at the pelvic region</term>." 

Thus, the wise should understand that one brings the soul from the heart to the root center using either the Hamsa or the Om mantra, and then one should raise the Kundalini specifically with the Kurcha seed-mantra ("Hum"). 

What kind of person is this practitioner? One who is devoted to the practice of **restraints and observances** <term>Yama and Niyama</term>. Yama and Niyama are limbs of Yoga that point the way to success; they destroy obstacles like lust and anger and facilitate the stability of the mind and the proper exhalation of the internal breath. Success in the actual Yoga comes through these, and not merely through the rules of restraint alone. Therefore, the author, in the fifty-fourth verse, uses the phrase "the Yogi joined with Yama, etc." to show that...

<summary>This page explains the precise mantras (Hum, Hamsa, Om) and visualizations used to move the individual soul from the heart to the root chakra to join with Kundalini before ascending the central spine. It emphasizes that these spiritual mechanics require a foundation in ethical disciplines like Yama and Niyama.</summary>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 76

The term is used in the plural. This practice of **Yama** <term>the first limb of Yoga, consisting of ethical restraints like non-violence and truthfulness</term> and the other limbs is intended only for those whose minds are agitated by desire and other passions. If, however, by the power of merit from a previous life or by one's own inherent nature, a person is naturally free from anger, desire, and the like, then they are capable of this Yoga even without the practice of those preliminary stages.

Regarding the term **Of good character** <note>original: suśīla</note>: because the practitioner is habituated to the practice of the restraints (Yama) and the others, they are therefore described as "of good character."

Regarding the phrase **From the mouth of the Holy Lord** <note>original: Śrī-Nātha-vaktrāt</note>: the implication is that knowledge of the sequence of practice cannot be attained without the oral instruction of a Guru. As it has been said: "It is to be known through the Guru’s instruction, and not through millions of scriptural interpretations."

The author further qualifies the sequence of practice: **Revealing the path to Great Liberation** <note>original: mahā-mokṣa-vartma-prakāśitam</note>. The "path of liberation" is the path in the form of the hollow center of the **Citrini channel** <term>Citrini-nāḍī; the innermost subtle tube within the spinal column through which Kundalini rises</term>. Its "revelation" means the bursting open of that path through the prescribed sequence.

**Of a nature of pure intelligence** <note>original: śuddha-buddhi-svabhāva</note>: this refers to one whose nature is established in **Brahman** <term>the ultimate reality or absolute consciousness</term> through pure intelligence. Alternatively, it means one whose state of being is centered in their own Soul through pure intellect.

**Well-concealed** <note>original: suguptaṃ</note>: this is used as an adverb. Alternatively, it describes the hidden form of the **Linga** <term>the subtle representation of Shiva at the base of the spine</term>, which is "sealed" or unmanifest because its "mouth" or opening has not yet been revealed.

Now, the author explains in detail the method for raising the **Kundalini** <term>the primordial energy coiled at the base of the spine</term>. It is stated in the fifth branch of the **Tree of Ritual Discovery** <note>original: Āgama-kalpadruma</note>:

> Having placed the body in the **Lotus Posture** <term>Padmāsana</term>, one should then place the two hands [in the lap]. Then, remembering the **Hamsa** <term>Haṃsa; a mantra representing the breath and the soul</term>, one should slowly contract the anus <note>a physical lock known as Mula Bandha</note>. One should raise the breath (Prana) through that path again and again. By raising it, one should pierce the **Chakras** <term>energy centers</term>; this is called the practice. 
> 
> In the **Root Lotus** <term>Mūlādhāra</term>, there is a most beautiful **Triangle**. In its center is **Kama** <note>the seed of desire and creation</note>, shining like tens of millions of rising suns. Above that is the **Kundalini power**, coiled around the **Self-Existent Linga** <term>Svayambhū-liṅga</term>. By the union of the **"Kurcha"** syllable <note>the 'Hum' mantra</note> and the fire of the **Vama** [energy], she longs for the Supreme Hamsa.

Similarly, in the **Purification of the Elements** <note>original: Bhūta-śuddhi</note>:

> Taking the key residing in the heart, and going into its interior, O Shive <note>addressing the Goddess</note>, reaching the gate known as the throat, and forcefully opening that door with the key, O Supreme Goddess, one should kindle it with the wind. The brilliance of the sacrificial fire...

<meta>This page continues the technical commentary on the spiritual awakening of Kundalini. While the previous page focused on the practitioner’s character and the necessity of oral instruction from a Guru, this page provides a detailed physiological and energetic "map" of the process, explaining how the practitioner uses breath and heat to move Kundalini from the Root Chakra through the central channel.</meta>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 77

Having pierced the three **Lingas** <term>symbolic pillars or marks of Shiva within the chakras that act as energetic blockages</term>, that Goddess, who is of the nature of pure being, like a flash of lightning in the subtle, luminous, and nectar-like abode of the **Supreme Shiva** <note>original: parama-rasa-śive</note>...

> Through heat, she is moved greatly upwards. One should awaken the sleeping serpent <note>Kundalini</note> at the **Linga** situated at the mouth of the **Yoni** <term>the triangular base of the root chakra</term>. Then, one should circulate the breath within the channels according to the path shown by the Guru, specifically through the practice of **Kumbhaka** <term>breath retention</term>. Having thus restrained the vital breath (**Jiva**) through the well-concealed path, one should cause the lotuses to face upwards through upward breaths, O Goddess. Slowly awakening the Sun <note>referring to the solar energy or Prana</note>, the wise one should lead it to the peak of **Mount Meru** <term>the crown of the head or the thousand-petaled lotus</term>.

Therefore, let the sequence established by the consideration of all these statements be noted. The practitioner, sitting in the prescribed posture, placing the hands upturned in the lap, and with a steady mind through the **Khechari mudra** <term>a yoga gesture where the tongue is turned back into the nasal cavity to seal energy</term>, fills the body with air. Performing breath retention (**Kumbhaka**), they should contract the heart. By this, the upward escape of the breath is prevented. Then, perceiving the downward movement of the air within the body—which, by pervading the belly and throat, bursts through the openings of the channels—one should contract the anus <note>the practice of Mula Bandha</note> to restrain the **Apana** <term>the downward-flowing vital energy</term>. 

Lifting the breath, one should turn the fire of desire (**Kama**) situated within the triangle in the pericarp of the **Root Lotus** <note>original: mūlādhāra-kamala-karṇikā</note> to the left. By the 'Hum' sound, one awakens **Kundalini**, who is heated by that blazing fire of desire. Desiring union with the **Supreme Shiva**, she bursts open the mouth of the **Self-Existent Linga** <note>original: svayambhū-liṅga</note> and enters the center of the mouth of the **Citrini channel** <term>the innermost subtle tube within the spine</term>. This is the collective meaning of verse 50.

Now, he speaks of the method of Kundalini Yoga with the words "having pierced" <note>original: bhittvā</note>. To "pierce" means to create a path where the way was previously blocked. The meaning is: that Goddess Kundalini, having pierced the three **Lingas**—named **Svayambhu**, **Bana**, and **Itara**—and having made a path through those Lingas where the way was blocked, enters the **Citrini** channel, which belongs to the auspicious one named Brahma...

<meta>This page continues the technical explanation of Kundalini’s ascent. While the previous page described the physical and breath-based methods to awaken the serpent power at the base of the spine, this page details her journey through the higher centers (chakras), the piercing of spiritual "knots," and her ultimate union with Shiva in the thousand-petaled lotus at the crown of the head, which manifests as the four levels of sound.</meta>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 78

...She is of the form of a fine thread. Having reached the entire lotus of the auspicious one <note>Shiva</note> known as **Brahman** <term>the ultimate reality or absolute consciousness</term>, she shines brilliantly; she quickly brings about the bliss known as **Liberation** <note>original: mokṣākhyānanda-rūpaṃ</note> through her characteristic of extreme subtlety. ॥ 51 ॥

This refers to that which is connected to and knotted within the **Nadis** <term>the subtle channels of energy in the body</term>. Having reached that entire lotus, she shines there; then, by her characteristic of subtlety—meaning through her subtle essence—she, whose nature is like a glittering thread, stands near the Shiva who consists of **Supreme Bliss** <note>original: parama-rasa-śive</note>. This refers to the Shiva in the form of the **Supreme Bindu** <term>the dimensionless point of pure potentiality</term> situated in the whorl of the thousand-petaled lotus. This means she suddenly grants the practitioner the liberation that consists of eternal bliss.

Regarding the **Three Lingas** <note>referring to the three symbolic pillars of Shiva—Swayambhu, Bana, and Itara—located in the root, heart, and eyebrow centers</note>: it should be understood that she pierces fourteen knots, consisting of the previously mentioned six chakras and the five Shivas. As stated in the *Ocean of the Bliss of Shakti* <note>original: Śāktānandataraṅgiṇī</note>:

> Going through the **Aperture of Brahman** <term>the Brahmarandhra, the spiritual opening at the crown of the head</term> after piercing the fourteen knots.

Regarding the distinction between the **Lingas** and **Shiva**, it is said in the *Independent Tantra* <note>original: Svatantratantra</note>:

> Having pierced the Shivas dwelling in the six chakras, the Goddess goes to the **Partless State** <term>Nishkala, the undivided, absolute reality</term>. Taking on beautiful forms according to each chakra’s station, and enchanting the **Great Lord** <note>Maheshana/Shiva</note> whose body is immersed in bliss, she enjoys herself in each place until she reaches the Eternal. Shiva is called "Shiva" <note>meaning "the auspicious" or "the sleeping"</note> precisely because he is enchanted by the Supreme Lady.

Likewise, in the *Illusion Tantra* <note>original: Māyātantra</note>:

> Having pierced the three Lingas, the Goddess goes by the path of **Shakti** <term>the path of power or energy</term>. Having reached the Partless State in the chakras through those various forms, she is liberated.

"Through those various forms" refers to the states of **Vaikhari**, **Madhyama**, and **Pashyanti**. As it is said:

> In the first <note>the root center</note> is the state of **Vaikhari** <term>articulated, audible speech</term>; **Madhyama** <term>intermediate or mental speech</term> dwells in the heart; in the space between the eyebrows is the state of **Pashyanti** <term>visualized or illuminated speech</term>; and the state of **Para** <term>transcendental, unmanifest sound</term> is within the **Bindu** <note>the point of union in the crown</note>.

The meaning of this is that there are four powers that produce sound: **Para**, **Pashyanti**, **Madhyama**, and **Vaikhari**.

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 79

...She is of the form of non-duality with **Kundalini** <term>the serpent power at the base of the spine</term>. Therefore, in the exclusive journey of Kundalini to the **Sahasrara** <term>the thousand-petaled lotus at the crown</term>, she first subdues the **Swayambhu-linga** <note>the 'self-existent' symbolic pillar of Shiva in the root center</note> through the state of **Vaikhari** <term>articulated, audible speech</term>. Then, in the heart, she subdues the **Bana-linga** <note>the 'arrow' symbolic form in the heart center</note> through the state of **Madhyama** <term>the intermediate level of mental speech or thought</term>. Seeing the **Itara-linga** <note>the 'other' symbolic form in the eyebrow center</note> through the state of **Pashyanti** <term>the 'visionary' or pre-articulated level of speech</term>, she subdues it and finally attains the state of **Para** <term>supreme, transcendent speech</term> near the **Supreme Bindu** <term>the dimensionless point of pure potentiality</term>. This is the meaning. He now describes the method of piercing the chakras:

> Within the hollow of the **Chitrinī** <term>the innermost subtle channel within the central Sushumna</term>, one should move those red lotuses that arise there, which are situated on the left and right sides of the channel. O Supreme Goddess, those lotuses block the central path of the **Nadi** <term>energy channel</term>. One should enter the lotus via the left path, and by that, the piercing is accomplished. Just as a master artist or charioteer guides the rays <note>original: raśmi; can mean rays of light or reins</note>, so the Master should lead the soul through the chakras.

Likewise, through the path of the junctions of the six chakras, via the path of the **Sushumna** <term>the central energy channel of the spine</term>:

> One should bring the Goddess to the **Sahasrara** using the **Hamsa** mantra <note>original: hamsena manunā; the 'Swan' mantra associated with the breath</note>.

The text further describes the **Shiva of Supreme Essence** <note>original: parama-rasa-śiva</note> as being in the "subtle abode of light." This "subtle abode" refers to the Supreme Swan <note>original: hamsa</note>, whose brilliance is the **Nirvana-shakti** <term>the power of ultimate liberation</term>; she resides in the center of that light. The Goddess is also described as **Pure Sattva** <note>original: śuddha-sattva</note>. There are five levels of consciousness limited by the body, each progressively more excellent: **Sattva** <term>purity or existence</term>, **Ati-sattva** <term>great purity</term>, **Parama-sattva** <term>supreme purity</term>, **Shuddha-sattva** <term>pure purity</term>, and **Vishuddha-sattva** <term>exceptionally pure purity</term>. Among these, she is the "Pure Sattva," which corresponds to the **Turiya** <term>the 'fourth' state of consciousness, beyond waking, dreaming, and deep sleep</term>.

To the question of how she brings about the bliss known as **Liberation** <note>original: mokṣākhyānanda</note>, it is said: having dissolved that Kundalini into the **Supreme Bindu**, she grants liberation. This happens for some practitioners through meditating on that Bindu as the non-duality of Shiva and the Soul; for others, through meditating on it as the essence of **Shakti** <term>the feminine creative power</term>; for others, as being composed of the **Supreme Person** <term>Purusha</term>; and for some, as the perfect union <note>original: sāmarasyam</note> of Shiva and Shakti. As stated in the **Maya Tantra**:

> Experts in Yoga know Yoga as the unity of the individual soul and the Supreme <note>Note: The original text uses 'Rahu', which in this technical context refers to the union that 'swallows' duality</note>. Others understand it as the realization of the non-difference between Shiva and the Soul. Those who follow the Agamic scriptures describe it as knowledge of the essence of Shakti. The Ancient Person...

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 80

The wise say that knowledge arises from this state. It consists of the *Samarasya* <term>the "essential identity" or blissful union</term> of Shiva and Shakti, and is not different from the original nature of things. The oneness of the individual soul and the supreme soul is the subject of *Samadhi* <term>the state of deep meditative absorption</term>. It is called Yoga because through this, one is joined or becomes one with the Supreme Soul.

This is the meaning of "that Yoga." Having spoken of *Samadhi*, the author now describes a variation of Yoga through meditation. "Of Shiva and the soul," and so on. Regarding the nature of union (*samarasya*): It is declared that the bliss found in the union of man and woman is *samarasya* <note>Tantric texts often use the metaphor of human intimacy to explain the profound, non-dual bliss of cosmic union</note>. He describes the method of contemplating this in the **Great Shri-Krama** (**Brihat Shrikrama**):

> The wise see with the eye of knowledge the Great God (*Mahadeva*) above the *Nada* <term>the primordial sound-resonance</term>. Beside him is the Goddess, her body languid with the pride of her beautiful thighs, shining with supreme light, like pure crystal, the source of the orb’s form, attaining the bliss of consciousness, the stainless *Kala*. 

Here, *Kala* <note>original: "kalām"</note> refers to **Kundalini**. "Attaining the bliss of consciousness" means she has reached Shiva in his form as the *Bindu* <term>the dimensionless point of pure potentiality</term>. The "source of the orb's form" refers to the Supreme Shiva. Elsewhere it is said:

> Having brought **Kundalini** to the Supreme Shiva, who is of the form of the Void (*Shunya*), and having effected their union (*samarasya*), let her become the supreme nectar. Then, the practitioner should lead the Goddess back through that same path to the hollow of the *Kula* <note>The Muladhara chakra at the base of the spine</note>. Having thus brought them together and contemplated their union, one should satisfy the deity with the nectar arising from that union.

He states another method in the **Garland of Celestial Musicians** (**Gandharva-Malika**):

> The **Sahasrara** <term>the thousand-petaled lotus at the crown</term> is the city of Shiva, beautiful and free from sorrow. It is decorated everywhere with divine trees that eternally bear flowers and fruit. The city of Sadashiva <note>the "Eternal Shiva"</note> is beautiful, adorned with the Wish-Fulfilling Tree (*Kalpavriksha*). This tree consists of the five elements and is endowed with the three qualities (*gunas*). The four Vedas are its four branches, and it is always covered in flowers. O Parvati, its flowers are yellow, white, black, red, and green—beautiful with various colors. Having meditated on the Wish-Fulfilling Tree in this way, one sees a jeweled altar beneath it. Upon that is a beautiful couch, decorated with various cloths, accompanied by Mandara flowers and scented with many fragrances. There, O beautiful one, the Great God always resides. One should meditate on Sadashiva...

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 81

## Having led that Kula-Kundalini through the process of absorption, together with the individual soul, 
## the wise man [leads her] to the supreme abode of liberation, the pure lotus-dwelling of Shiva.

The deity there is like pure crystal. He is surrounded by many gems, possesses long, beautiful arms, and is heart-captivating. He has a joyful and pleasant face, always wearing a gentle smile, my dear. He is adorned with earrings and a necklace of gems. His body is further graced by a garland of a thousand lotuses. With eight arms and three eyes, Shiva’s eyes are shaped like lotus petals. On his feet are beautiful anklets, and his very form is composed of the **Sound-Absolute** <term>Shabda-Brahman; the manifestation of the supreme reality as sound-vibration</term>. In this way, O lotus-eyed one, one should meditate upon his "gross" or visible form.

The deity sits in the center of the lotus, motionless like a corpse <note>original: "shavarupa-vat". In Tantric thought, Shiva represents pure, static consciousness which appears "dead" or inactive without the kinetic power (Shakti) of Kundalini</note>. Yet, the Great God (*Mahadeva*) in this corpse-like state is eternal and never perishes. 

Thus, one should meditate on the Goddess **Kundalini** as she entwines the **Self-Existent Pillar** <term>Svayambhu-linga; the symbolic representation of Shiva in the lowest chakra</term>. With the **breath-mantra** <term>Hamsa-mantra; the "Ham-Sa" sound associated with inhalation and exhalation</term>, the wise practitioner should lead the Goddess to the **Thousand-Petaled Lotus** <term>Sahasrara</term>.

O Goddess, that is where the Great God **Sadashiva** <term>"The Eternal Shiva"</term> resides. He sees the Goddess in her beautiful form, delighting in the play of divine desire. The Supreme Shiva is intoxicated by the fragrance of her lotus-like face. O Empress of the Gods, having awakened him, she should sit there with him. Indeed, O Shiva, Kundalini kisses the lotus-face of Shiva.

O Queen of the Devas, she enjoys union with the Eternal Shiva for a moment. From that union, O Supreme Goddess, nectar is immediately produced. That nectar, O Goddess, is as red as **liquid lac** <note>original: "laksha-rasa". A crimson resin used as dye, signifying the vibrant, living energy of the nectar</note>. With that nectar, the practitioner should satisfy the Supreme Deity.

The deities residing in the six chakras are to be thoroughly satiated with that stream of nectar. Then, the wise practitioner should lead her back along that same path to the **Root Support** <term>Muladhara chakra</term>. By this process of going and returning, one should achieve the absorption of the mind. 

O Parvati, one who practices this day after day is freed from the bonds of worldly existence and the sufferings of old age and death. This method is also explored in other **Tantras** <term>sacred texts outlining ritual and meditative practices</term> through different variations. 51

# Investigation into the Six Chakras 82

## The King of Yogis, clinging to the twin lotus feet of his Guru, striving in deep meditation, should meditate upon the Supreme Goddess who is the form of consciousness and who bestows all desired fruits, as she resides with her Lord. || 52 ||

This is the meaning: The wise King of Yogis, through the process of awakening mentioned earlier, brings the Goddess Kundalini—who has reached the **Gate of Brahma** <term>Brahma-dvara; the entrance to the central channel through which the energy rises</term>—together with the **individual soul** <term>Jiva; the finite spark of life within the person</term>. 

By the process of absorption, he leads her to the pure lotus-dwelling within the **Thousand-Petaled Lotus** <term>Sahasrara; the highest energy center at the crown of the head</term>, into the presence of the Lord. This Lord is Shiva, who exists there in the form of the **Supreme Point** <term>Para-bindu; the infinitesimal point of infinite potential and concentrated consciousness</term>. 

Having brought her into a state of complete oneness with the Lord (who is that Supreme Point), he should meditate on the Supreme Goddess, who takes the form of one's chosen deity and grants all desired fruits. Regarding the phrase **"form of consciousness"**: the summary of the meaning is that the practitioner should then dissolve even the Supreme Point into the **Void** <term>Shunya; the primordial emptiness</term> situated within the **Soul-Consciousness** <term>Chid-atma</term>, and meditate on her as Pure Consciousness. 

Afterward, recognizing the unity of the individual soul and the supreme soul through the realization **"I am He"** <note>original: "So-ham-bhava"; the mantra of self-identification with the Absolute</note>, and dissolving the mind into that state, he should remain with a steady mind as the essence of all-pervading, perfect knowledge. 

As the **Great Teacher** <note>original: "Srimad-Acharya-svami"; likely a reference to the philosopher Adi Shankara</note> has said: 
"The wise man should dissolve the 'M' sound—the causal vibration—into the Soul-Consciousness. I am the Soul-Consciousness, eternally pure, enlightened, liberated, true, and non-dual. I am **Vasudeva** <note>original: "Vasudevo-ham"; a name for the supreme deity, indicating 'I am the Divine'</note>, the mass of supreme bliss, OM." 

Having distinguished the discriminating mind, one should dissolve it into the **Witness** <term>Sakshin; the silent, observing part of the self that does not change</term>. Once the mind is dissolved in the Soul-Consciousness, it should not be moved at all. One should remain as the essence of perfect wisdom, like the full and motionless ocean. 

In the context of the sacred syllable **OM** <term>Pranava</term>, the sound "M" is the cause, the Supreme Point. Devotees of Vishnu chant "I am Vasudeva" to express this. By this same logic, a devotee of any deity should understand "I am He" while practicing the meditation on OM, realizing that the form of Kundalini is made of their specific mantra.

<vocab>षट्चक्रनिरूपणम्, कुण्डलिनी, समाधि, सहस्रार, परविन्दु, जीवात्म, चिदात्म, वासुदेव, प्रणव, श्रीमदाचार्य</vocab>

<summary>This page describes the final stage of yoga where Kundalini unites with Shiva (as the Para-bindu) in the crown chakra, leading to the dissolution of the individual mind into universal consciousness. It emphasizes the "So-ham" (I am He) realization, where the practitioner identifies their own soul with the supreme, non-dual reality.</summary>
<keywords>Samadhi, Sahasrara, Kundalini-Shakti, Para-bindu, Jiva, Chid-atma, So-ham, Vasudeva, Adi Shankara, Dissolution of Mind</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the King of Yogis. Having established the goal of union in the Sahasrara on the previous page, the text now details the specific "Sequence of Dissolution" (Laya-krama), where the practitioner systematically draws the elements and the individual soul upward through the chakras.</meta>

<page-num>83</page-num>
-># Investigation into the Six Chakras<-

...within. "Situated in the rays, situated in the fire, situated between the moon and the sun. Having made this of great value, the King of Yogis stands complete/fulfilled." <note>This quote describes the state of the yogi who has integrated the solar, lunar, and fiery energies of the body.</note> Based on the previously shown statement, "leading it together with the individual soul," the **individual soul** <term>Jivatma</term>—which resides in the heart and is shaped like the **flame of a lamp** <term>Dipakalika</term>—is called the **Swan** <term>Hansa</term>. Using the "Hansa" mantra, one should bring it to the **Root Support** <term>Muladhara; the lowest chakra at the base of the spine</term> and, along with it, lead the **Coiled Power** <term>Kundalini</term> upward.

Regarding the phrase **"by means of dissolution"**: He now describes the sequence of dissolution: "Then, O Queen of the Gods, one should meditate on the **syllable Lam** <term>Langkara; the seed-sound of the Earth element</term> in the region called the **Triangle**. Having meditated on the principle of the universe, one should then meditate on **Kamadeva** <note>The god of desire/love, representing the creative impulse at the root</note>. 

Having fixed the individual soul there and by the utterance of the **Sacred Sound** <term>Pranava; the syllable 'Om'</term>, the Wife <note>referring to the Kundalini Shakti</note>, who desires to destroy the **limited release of breath** <term>Visarga</term>, moves to that place. O Queen of the Gods, O mistress of my life, O Maheshvari, having meditated on the sense of **Smell**, and then on **Dakini** <note>the presiding goddess of the Muladhara</note> and the supreme worshipful Power, O Mother, Daughter of the Mountain, and having dissolved these into **Earth**, O Ganesvari; then, O Great Goddess, having dissolved that blessed Earth into **Scent** <term>Gandha</term>; 

Having drawn the individual soul by the power of the Sacred Sound, O Daughter of the Mountain Lord, the practitioner, taking the breath and the Scent along with the Kundalini, should cause them to enter the **Self-Abiding Place** <term>Svadhishthana; the second chakra located at the genitals</term> by means of the **'So-ham'** <note>‘I am He’</note> mantra. 

Likewise, in its pericarp <note>the center of the lotus</note>, one should meditate on **Varuna** <note>the god of the Water element</note> and also on **Hari** <term>Vishnu</term> there. Having meditated on the Power who is the **Ancient Creatrix** <term>Purana-karini Shakti</term>, O Fair-faced One, and having dissolved these and the Scent into **Water**, O Fair-faced One, the practitioner should lead the individual soul, the Kundalini, and **Taste** <term>Rasa</term> into the **City of Jewels** <term>Manipura; the third chakra at the navel</term>. 

Then, in its pericarp, O woman of beautiful hips, having meditated on **Fire** <term>Vahni</term>, where **Rudra** himself is the agent of the destruction of all things, one should meditate on that beautiful **Sakini Power** <term>Sakini Shakti</term> joined with him. Then, having meditated on the sense of **Sight**, which is composed of light, O Goddess, and having dissolved these and Taste into **Form** <term>Rupa</term>, O Blessed One, he should lead the individual soul, the Kundalini, and Form into the **Unstruck Sound** <term>Anahata; the fourth chakra at the heart</term>. 

Likewise, in its pericarp, he should lead the **Air** <term>Vayu</term>, which destroys the attachment to the seat of life. There, he should meditate on the **Region of the Womb** <term>Yoni-mandala</term> adorned with the **Arrow-Linga** <term>Bana-linga; a naturally occurring stone linga representing Shiva</term>, joined with the **Rakini Power** <term>Rakini-shakti</term>..."

<summary>This page describes the Laya-krama (dissolution process), where the practitioner moves Kundalini and the individual soul through the first four chakras, dissolving each element (Earth, Water, Fire) into the next more subtle one.</summary>
<keywords>Laya-krama, Jivatma, Hansa mantra, Muladhara, Svadhishthana, Manipura, Anahata, Kundalini, elements, Rudra, Vishnu</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the King of Yogis. Having established the goal of union in the Sahasrara on the previous page, the text now details the specific "Sequence of Dissolution" (Laya-krama), where the practitioner systematically draws the elements and the individual soul upward through the chakras.</meta>

<page-num>८४</page-num>
-># Investigation into the Six Chakras<-

<warning>Ink stains and paper damage at the top edge partially obscure the header and the first two lines of text.</warning>

...the element of **Air** and the **sense of touch**. Having united these forms into the purifying principle of **Touch**, one should then establish the **Individual Soul** <term>Jiva</term> and the **Coiled Power** <term>Kundalini</term> within the **Purification Center** <term>Vishuddha Chakra; the throat center</term>. Furthermore: within its pericarp <note>the center of the lotus-like chakra</note>, one should establish **Space** and the god **Shiva** along with the goddess **Sakini**. O Daughter of the Mountain, having established **Speech** and the **sense of hearing** within Space, and having drawn **Touch** into **Sound**, O Shankari, my beloved, one should then place the Individual Soul, the Coiled Power, and Sound into the **Command Center** <term>Ajna Chakra; the third eye center between the eyebrows</term. This concludes the quote from the *Kankalamalini Tantra*.

**In the place called the Triangle** refers to the triangle previously mentioned in the **Root Support** <term>Muladhara</term>. Within that, one should meditate on the **seed-syllable Lam** <term>Langkara</term>. There is another school of thought that suggests bringing the Soul upward by the utterance of the **Sacred Sound** <term>Pranava; the syllable Om</term>.

Regarding the phrase **"She who desires to destroy the Visarga"**: the <term>Visarga</term> <note>original: "विसर्ग" (visarga)</note> here refers to the outward flow or the agitation of desire. The **Coiled Power** <term>Kundalini</term>, whose nature is to destroy that agitation, must be led by the practitioner to her **Husband** <note>Shiva</note> in the supreme station—the **Point** <term>Bindu</term> within the **Thousand-Petalled Lotus**.

The practitioner should take these—the syllable **Lam**, the creator **Brahma**, the god of love **Kamadeva**, the goddess **Dakini**, and the **sense of smell**—into the **Earth** element. Dissolve the Earth into the **Principle of Scent**. Then, having drawn the Individual Soul, the Coiled Power, and the Principle of Scent upward with the Sacred Sound <term>Om</term>, one should bring them into the **Self-Abode** <term>Svadhisthana; the sacral chakra</term> using the mantra **"I am He"** <term>So-ham</term>. This logic should be understood to apply at every stage.

Having led the Individual Soul, the Coiled Power, and the **Principle of Sound** into the **Command Center** <term>Ajna Chakra</term>, one should dissolve Sound into the **Ego** <term>Ahankara</term>, the Ego into the **Great Intellect** <term>Mahat-tattva</term>, the Intellect into the **Subtle Nature** called the **Golden Womb** <term>Hiranyagarbha</term>, and finally dissolve Nature into the **Supreme Point** <term>Para-bindu</term>.

As stated in the *Illumination of Mantras and Tantras* <note>original: "Mantratantraprakashe"</note>:
"One should dissolve Space along with Sound into the Ego, and the Ego into the Intellect. The Intellect and all its powers should be dissolved into the **Unmanifest** <term>Avyakta</term>, the Supreme Cause. One should meditate on everything being dissolved into that Supreme Cause of the All-Pervader <note>Vishnu</note>, which is the very form of **Being, Consciousness, and Bliss** <term>Saccidananda</term>."

This means one should dissolve all powers into the Unmanifest—the subtle nature known as the **Golden Womb**—and dissolve that Nature into the Cause, which is the form of the **Supreme Point**. Here, before the time of **Deep Absorption** <term>Samadhi</term>, one should meditate in this way with great effort. Following the sequence from the **gross to the subtle**, everything is dissolved into the **Soul of Consciousness** <term>Cidatman</term>...

<summary>This page describes the "Laya-krama" or sequence of dissolution, explaining how elements, senses, and deities are systematically merged into one another as the Kundalini moves from the throat to the third eye and finally into the supreme consciousness.</summary>
<keywords>Laya-krama, dissolution, Vishuddha Chakra, Ajna Chakra, Kundalini, Jivatma, Bindu, Mahat-tattva, Saccidananda, Samadhi</keywords>

--- Page 85 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical description of the "Sequence of Dissolution" (Laya-krama). Having previously described the movement of the Kundalini through the lower centers, the text now details how the elements, senses, and mental faculties are systematically absorbed into one another as the practitioner ascends toward the highest consciousness.</meta>

<page-num>85</page-num>
<header>Investigation into the Six Chakras</header>

One should cause **dissolution** <term>Laya; the process of absorption into a cause</term> according to the instructions of the teachers, merging the **gross** into the **subtle**. Regarding the elements from **Earth** up to the **Heart Center** <term>Anahata</term>, they are dissolved in the stated order. Because they are based on the Earth, one should dissolve the collection of the Earth-expansion—consisting of the **feet** and the **sense of smell**—into the **Earth center** <note>the Root Support or Muladhara</note>.

In the same way, one dissolves the expansion of **Water**—consisting of the **hands** and the **sense of taste**—into the **Water center** <note>the Svadhisthana</note>. Into the **Fire center** <note>the Manipura</note>, one dissolves the expansion of **Fire**—the **anus** and the **sense of sight**. Into the **Air center** <note>the Anahata</note>, one dissolves the expansion of **Air**—the **genitals** and the **sense of touch**. Into the **Space center** <note>the Vishuddha</note>, one dissolves the expansion of **Space**—the **speech** and the **sense of hearing**.

Within the **Command Center** <term>Ajna Chakra</term>, the subtle elements, the **Great Principle** <term>Mahat</term>, and the **Ego** <term>Ahankara</term> are dissolved into their cause in the sequence mentioned. Then, after dissolving the letters of the alphabet in reverse order—from the letter *Ksha* back to the letter *A*—one moves from the gross to the subtle. Since the text says "everything," it implies that to remain in the state of **Pure Brahman** <term>the Absolute</term>, one must also dissolve the entire "causal body" (consisting of the **Point** <term>Bindu</term>, the **Awakener** <term>Bodhini</term>, and so on, which were previously shown to exist above the Command Center) in reverse order into the **Supreme Point** <term>Para-bindu</term>, the primal cause.

Regarding this method, it is said:
"Having formed this intention, the practitioner should then withdraw the qualities of inertia and activity. In reverse order, dissolve the letters *La* and *La* into the letter *Ha*. Dissolve the letter *Ha* into *Sa*, and dissolve *Sa* into the letter *Sha*. By following this sequence with effort until the end, one produces dissolution." Furthermore, for that purpose:

> One should withdraw into the **Point** <term>Bindu</term> and join it to the **Digital Power** <term>Kala</term>. Move that into the **Sound** <term>Nada</term>, and then within the Sound, dissolve again at the end of Sound. Join that to the **State Beyond Mind** <term>Unmaniya</term> and lead it to the **Face of Vishnu**. Then the best of practitioners should join that to the **Face of the Guru**. Finally, join the letters and let them dissolve into the **Supreme Shiva**.

Regarding the **Face of Vishnu** <note>original: "विष्णुवक्त्र" (Vishnu-vaktra)</note>:
This refers to the **Masculine Point** <term>Pum-bindu</term>. In various teachings, it is said: "The Sun-point is called the Face, while the Moon and Fire are below." Because of the unity of these statements, which say "the Point is the Person <term>Purusha</term>" and "the Visarga <note>the two dots of Sanskrit grammar</note> is known as Nature <term>Prakriti</term>," that very Point is considered the "Face."

As the teacher **Keshavacharya** said:
"Let the practitioner lead that power into the Person <term>Purusha</term> who is the nature of the Point; then into the Supreme Self; then into the Principle of Time; then into that very Power <term>Shakti</term> which is the nature of Consciousness; and finally into the absolute, peaceful Abode."

<vocab>Investigation into the Six Chakras, Command Center, Point, Sound, State Beyond Mind, Face of Vishnu, Shiva, Keshavacharya, Dissolution, Earth, Air, Space</vocab>

<summary>This page describes the technical "Sequence of Dissolution" (Laya-krama), where the practitioner systematically dissolves the physical elements, sensory organs, and the Sanskrit alphabet into the higher spiritual centers. It explains the progression from the Ajna Chakra into the Supreme Point (Para-bindu) and eventually into the absolute state of Supreme Shiva.</summary>
<keywords>Laya-krama, dissolution of elements, Ajna Chakra, Para-bindu, Vishnu-vaktra, Sanskrit alphabet dissolution, subtle body anatomy, Keshavacharya</keywords>

--- Page 86 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion on the "Sequence of Dissolution" (Laya-krama) of the chakras. The previous page established that the practitioner must dissolve the gross elements (Earth, Water, Fire, etc.) into their respective centers within the body.</meta>

<page-num>86</page-num>
<header>Investigation into the Six Chakras</header>

...and because of the unity of these statements. Here, by observing the dissolution of each effect into its respective cause, one should understand the progression: from the **Resonance** <term>Nada; the subtle inner sound</term> into the **Pervasive Power** <term>Vyapika-shakti</term>, from her into the **Trans-mental** <term>Unmani</term>, from her into the **Equanimous** <term>Samana</term>, and from her into the **Mouth of Vishnu** <note>original: "Vishnu-vaktre"; a technical term for the final gateway to the supreme state</note>. In this manner, by the dissolution of the letters of the alphabet, the dissolution of the six chakras is achieved, because the lotus petals are themselves composed of these letters.

Thus it is said in the *Vishvasara*: "The petals of the lotuses are declared to be composed of the primal letters."

Now, the *Sammohana Tantra* describes the method for dissolving both the letters and the lotus centers:

"One should dissolve the letters located on the petals, from *Va* to *Sa*, into the **Lotus of the Seat** <note>the Muladhara or Root Center</note>. Merging that into the six-petaled lotus <note>the Svadhisthana</note> which is endowed with the letters from *Ba* to *La*, one should awaken it by the command of the Guru," and so forth. "After joining them into the two-petaled lotus <note>the Ajna Chakra</note> adorned with the letters *Ha* and *Ksha*, the wise practitioner should dissolve that into the **Point** <term>Bindu</term> within the subtle particle, and then place that into the petal-less state"—so ends the statement.

Therefore, after dissolving the four letters in the **Root Lotus** <note>Muladhara</note>, one should dissolve that lotus into the **Self-Abode** <note>Svadhisthana</note>. In this sequence, up to the letters *Ha* and *Ksha* located in the **Command Center** <term>Ajna Chakra</term>, having dissolved them, one dissolves that lotus into the Point, the Point into the **Awakening Power** <term>Bodhini</term>, and as before, dissolves everything into the **Supreme Point** <term>Para-Bindu</term>.

Furthermore, during the dissolution of the Command Center, because it is impossible for the deity **Hakini**, the **Itara-Linga** <note>a symbolic form of Shiva</note>, and the **Pranava** <note>the sacred syllable OM</note>—which are located in the center of the lotus—to remain without a support, one should dissolve them also into the Supreme Point after the dissolution of **Nature** <term>Prakriti</term>.

In the "Abode of the Pure Lotus"—what kind of place is this? It is the **Abode of Liberation**, meaning the place that accomplishes final release.

The text qualifies the "Lord of Yogis" as "one who leans upon the pair of the Guru's lotus feet." The sense is that because all success <term>Siddhi</term> comes only through the Guru's instruction, he has taken refuge at those feet.

Regarding the phrase "restrained in **Absorption**" <term>Samadhi</term>, the *Kularnava Tantra* provides a definition: "When no external objects appear and the mind is steady like curds <note>meaning the mind has 'set' or solidified into an unmoving state</note>; that meditation which is void of its own form is ordained as Samadhi."

Likewise, it is said elsewhere: "The constant feeling of identity between the **Individual Soul** <term>Jivatman</term> and the **Supreme Soul** <term>Paramatman</term> is what sages call Samadhi, declared as the final characteristic of the Eight Limbs of Yoga." Furthermore, the *Yoga Sutras* of Patanjali state: "Yoga is the cessation of the fluctuations of the mind..."

<summary>This page details the "Laya-krama" or dissolution process, where the letters of the alphabet and the symbolic components of the chakras are systematically absorbed into higher subtle states, culminating in the Supreme Point (Para-Bindu). It also provides classical definitions of Samadhi from the Kularnava Tantra and other sources, emphasizing the role of the Guru.</summary>
