--- Page 87 ---
<meta>This page continues from page 86, which detailed the "Sequence of Dissolution" (Laya-krama), where the practitioner dissolves the subtle elements and letters of the alphabet into the higher centers of the body. Page 87 describes the return journey: the descent of the Kundalini energy back to the root center after it has been nourished by the "supreme nectar" of union at the crown.</meta>

<page-num>87</page-num>
<header>Investigation into the Six Chakras</header>

> The beautiful **Kundalini** <term>the primordial energy coiled at the base of the spine</term>, having drunk the **Supreme Nectar** <note>original: "paramāmṛta"</note> from **Supreme Shiva** <note>original: "paraśiva"</note>, returns from that state of the Great Dawn of Eternal Bliss through the **Path of Kula** <term>the Chitrini channel within the spine</term> and re-enters the **Root** <note>the Muladhara chakra</note>. The yogi, possessing a steady mind, should satisfy the deity residing in the vessel of the universe <note>the human body as a microcosm</note> with that stream of divine nectar, according to the method known through the lineage of yoga tradition. ॥ 53 ॥

...blazing and subtle. In that [state], because he is engaged in that practice—this is the meaning. ॥ 52 ॥

After the meditation on all the various yogic processes previously described, the author states what is to be done next in the verse beginning with **"Drinking the lac-colored..."**

The meaning is as follows: The beautiful Kundalini, having drunk the Supreme Nectar from Supreme Shiva, and having emerged from the **Great Dawn of Eternal Bliss** <note>original: "nityānanda-mahodaya"; the state of supreme realization at the crown</note>, reaches the **Path of Brahma** <note>original: "brahma-patha"</note> located within the **Chitrini** nerve via the Path of Kula, and should then enter the **Root** <note>original: "mūlādhāra"</note> again. This is the connection of the words.

Regarding the word **"Drinking"**: after the previously mentioned meditation on the principles <note>the Tattvas</note>, and having visualized the **Perfect Union** <term>Samarasya; the state where Shiva and Shakti are experienced as one</term> of Shiva and Shakti, one should nourish Kundalini with the Supreme Nectar produced from Supreme Shiva through the bliss of that union. The meaning is: "having drunk that nectar which brings satisfaction."

In a previously cited passage, the phrase "having made her drink" implies the causal action of drinking. The author describes the nectar as **lac-colored** <note>original: "lākṣābha"</note>. This means it has a reddish glow, similar to the juice of red lac <note>a resin used as a crimson dye</note>.

The phrase **"From the Great Dawn of Eternal Bliss"** refers to the place where the Great Light of Eternal Bliss originates.

**"Should enter the Root"**: the same method used to raise her is used to bring her back to the Muladhara. Thus, just as she ascends through the sequence of piercing the **Lingas** <term>the "signs" or pillars of light in the chakras</term> and other centers, she returns to the Root Center in that very same form.

As has been said by the venerable Master <note>referring to Adi Shankaracharya in the Saundarya Lahari, verse 9</note>:
"Drenching the world with streams of nectar flowing from between your two feet, and [returning] again to the tradition of essence..."

<summary>This page describes the final stage of the Kundalini process, where the awakened energy returns from the Sahasrara (crown) to the Muladhara (root), carrying divine nectar to nourish the yogi's body. It emphasizes the "Samarasya" or the blissful union of Shiva and Shakti as the source of this revitalizing nectar.</summary>
<keywords>Kundalini descent, Paramamrita (Supreme Nectar), Samarasya (Union), Muladhara (Root Center), Chitrini Nadi, Path of Kula, Saundarya Lahari</keywords>

--- Page 88 ---
<meta>This page continues the commentary on Verse 53 of the *Shat-chakra-nirupana*. The previous page described the Kundalini energy drinking the supreme nectar at the crown (Sahasrara) and beginning her descent back to the base of the spine (Muladhara). This page explains the mechanics of that return journey and the "Sequence of Creation" (Srishti-krama).</meta>

<page-num>88</page-num>
<header>Investigation into the Six Chakras</header>

**By radiance.** <note>original: "mahasā"</note> Having reached her own land, having made herself a coiled ring in the center of the serpent’s sky, you sleep in the hollow of the **Root Center** <term>Kula-kunda; the cavity in the Muladhara where Kundalini rests</term>. In this verse, the phrase "the secret of the nectar tradition" <note>original: "rasāmnāya-rahasya"</note> is used. The word *Amnaya* means that which is spoken, told, or ordained. Therefore, *Amnaya* refers to a ritual injunction or traditional teaching. By the word "radiance" <note>original: "mahaḥ"</note>, light is meant. Furthermore, by reaching "her own land," it is understood to mean reaching the **Root Center** <note>original: "mūlādhāra"</note> through the manifestation of the previously described ritual of nectar. 

While the journey upward follows the **Sequence of Dissolution** <term>Laya-krama; the process of dissolving lower elements into higher ones</term>, the return journey follows the **Sequence of Creation** <term>Srishti-krama; the process of re-manifesting the elements as the energy descends</term>. It is said: 

> "Then that **Kundalini Shakti** <term>the primordial energy</term>, the Queen of the Gods, in the form of a ring, returns in that same manner to the **Base Lotus** <note>the Muladhara</note>." 

Likewise, in the chapter on the **Purification of the Elements** <note>original: "Bhūta-śuddhi"</note>: 

> "One should gradually bring the individual soul and the Kundalini from the Supreme Self back to their own places, following the sequence of the elements such as earth and the rest." 

The specific detail here is that while she is "shining" during the first journey (upward), during the return journey she is "becoming like nectar" <note>original: "amṛtāyamānām"</note>. Immediately after that, she flows in a divine stream of nectar—the supreme nectar produced by the union of **Shiva and Shakti** previously shown—dripping in a continuous flow from the **Opening of Brahma** <term>Brahmarandhra; the fontanelle at the crown of the head</term> down to the **Root Center**. 

This is why it was said in the third verse: "The **Gate of Brahma** <note>original: "brahma-dvāra"</note> shines at her mouth, a place to be reached by the stream of nectar." 

**Steady-minded.** <note>original: "sthira-matiḥ"</note> This means the yogi should satisfy the deity—both his own personal deity and the deities such as **Dakini** <note>the goddess of the Muladhara</note> and others who reside in the six chakras. As the previous saying goes: 

> "With that nectar, O Goddess, let him satisfy the Supreme Deity. Having satisfied the deities of the six chakras there with the stream of nectar..." 

He further describes this stream of nectar as **"known through the lineage of yoga tradition."** This is said because yoga is obtained through a continuous succession of instructions from a Guru. The intended meaning is that this is known through the traditional transmission of a teacher's instructions. 

He then further describes the nectar as **"situated in the vessel of the universe."** <note>original: "brahmāṇḍa-bhāṇḍa-sthitam"</note> The "vessel" or "pot" of the universe is Kundalini herself, because she is the **Source** <term>Yoni; the cosmic womb or origin</term> of all things. Thus, it means "situated in the vessel that is Kundalini." ॥ 53 ॥

<vocab>षट्‌चक्रनिरूपणम् (Shat-chakra-nirupana), कुण्डलिनी (Kundalini), मूलाधार (Muladhara), शिवशक्ति (Shiva-Shakti), ब्रह्मरन्ध्र (Brahmarandhra), षट्चक्र (Shat-chakra), लयक्रम (Laya-krama), सृष्टिक्रम (Srishti-krama)</vocab>

<summary>This page concludes the commentary on Verse 53, explaining how the Kundalini energy returns to the Muladhara chakra. It details the "Sequence of Creation" where divine nectar flows from the crown to satisfy the deities of the six chakras through traditional yogic practice.</summary>
<keywords>Kundalini descent, Srishti-krama, divine nectar (amrita), Muladhara, Guru tradition (Parampara), chakra deities, Bhuta-shuddhi</keywords>

--- Page 89 ---
<meta>This page concludes the "Shat-chakra-nirupana" (Investigation into the Six Chakras) by detailing the spiritual rewards for those who master this knowledge. It shifts from the technical description of Kundalini's movement, discussed on the previous page, to the final blessings of the practice and the importance of the Guru's grace.</meta>

<page-num>88</page-num>
<header>Investigation into the Six Chakras</header>

Knowing this supreme sequence, the **yogi** <term>a practitioner of yoga</term> of controlled mind, endowed with the foundational restraints and observances <term>Yama and Niyama; the ethical disciplines of yoga</term>, through the rising flow of joy from the pair of lotus feet of the venerable initiation Guru—he is never born again in this world, nor is he ever destroyed even at the time of universal dissolution. Delighted by the continuous stream of eternal bliss, he is peaceful and the foremost among the righteous. **54**

The yogi who, situated in his own true nature, reads this stainless, pure, and supreme secret—the very source of the knowledge of liberation—at night, at the two twilights <note>dawn and dusk</note>, or by day, and who rests upon the pair of lotus feet of the venerable Guru with a controlled inner mind—the heart of such a one surely dances at the feet of his chosen deity. **55**

---

The author now describes the result of knowing the aforementioned sequence of yoga with the words: **"Knowing this..."** Regarding the phrase "from the rising flow of joy from the pair of lotus feet of the venerable initiation Guru" <note>original: "śrī-dīkṣā-guru-pāda-padma-yugalamoda-pravāhodayāt"</note>: **Joy** <note>original: "āmoda"</note> means bliss. Its **flow** is an uninterrupted and ever-increasing connection; in other words, it is eternal bliss. The "rising" of this bliss comes from the pair of lotus feet of the one from whom this knowledge is gained. 

The **Initiation Guru** <note>original: "dīkṣā-guru"</note> is specifically mentioned because he is the first point of contact and holds the highest gravity of respect. However, in his absence, this knowledge may be sought from another Guru. As it is said:

> "Just as a bee, greedy for honey, wanders from flower to flower, so the disciple, greedy for knowledge, wanders from Guru to Guru."

The phrase **"delighted by the continuous stream of eternal bliss"** means being filled with the joy that takes the form of a constant stream of eternal happiness. **"Foremost among the righteous"** refers to being the most excellent among those worthy individuals who are yogis. **54**

Now the author describes the benefit of studying these verses on the six chakras with the words: **"The one who reads..."** **"Situated in his own nature"** means being firmly established in the state of the Self <note>the Atman or soul</note>. **"With a controlled inner mind"** means having a mind that is restrained...

<summary>This concluding section of the manuscript describes the ultimate spiritual liberation and eternal bliss attained by a yogi who studies these teachings and remains devoted to their Guru. It emphasizes that such a practitioner escapes the cycle of rebirth and finds peace even during the dissolution of the universe.</summary>
<keywords>liberation, Guru, eternal bliss, yoga practice, bee metaphor, initiation, spiritual rewards</keywords>

The phrase means: one whose mind has been made steady within the **inner self** <note>original: "antarātmani"</note> through the practice of yoga. The rest of the verse is clear. **55**

Thus ends the eighth chapter, titled "A Clarification of the Meaning of the Verses Describing the Six Chakras" <term>Ṣaṭ-cakra-vivaraṇa-ślokārtha-pariṣkāriṇī</term>, which is contained within the *Venerable Jewel of Reality-Truth* <term>Śrī-Tattva-cintāmaṇi</term>, composed by the venerable **Purnananda Yati** <note>A 16th-century Bengali saint and scholar of the Tantric tradition</note>.

# c) Investigation into the Six Chakras

---

->**Now, following the tradition of the Tantras, the sequence of the six chakras and other centers is presented.**<-
->**This is said to be the first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss.**<-

May the eldest and best of the wise—who, through the knowledge of Great Yoga, is familiar with the glory of the six-petaled centers <note>The chakras are often described as lotus flowers with varying numbers of petals</note>, who alone is capable of revealing the inner truth, and who possesses the compassion of the Lord of the Night <note>original: "kshapanatha," a poetic name for the Moon, symbolizing cooling grace</note>—manifest the inner truth of the six chakras along with their sixfold glory. 

Now, the most compassionate Purnananda Swami, wishing to uplift the world which is sunk in the mire of suffering, desires to speak of the practice of **Kundalini Yoga** <term>the yoga of the "coiled" primal energy</term> within the six chakras for the purpose of attaining the knowledge of reality <term>tattva-jnana</term> which brings about liberation <term>mukti</term>. For the attention of practitioners <term>sadhakas</term>, he makes this declaration, beginning with the word "Now." 

"Now" <note>original: "Atha"</note> refers to the sequence following the description of those rituals previously shown in his own work, the *Venerable Jewel of Reality-Truth* <term>Sri Tattva-chintamani</term>, prior to this investigation of the six chakras. 

The meaning of the opening verse is as follows: "The first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss is being told." Supreme Bliss is Brahman <note>The Ultimate Reality or Universal Consciousness</note>, as the sacred scriptures <term>Shruti</term> say: "Brahman is eternal and supreme bliss." The "realization" <note>original: "nirvaha"</note> refers to the accomplishment in the form of direct experience <term>sakshatkara</term> of that Bliss. The "first sprout" for that realization refers to the nature of the first manifested practices that serve as the cause for that experience; these are now being told by me, Purnananda. 

What is the nature of this "sprout"? It is "the sequence of the six chakras and other centers extracted according to the Tantras." "According to the Tantras" means following the established doctrines of the Tantric tradition regarding the six chakras known as: the **Muladhara** <note>the root center</note>, **Svadhishthana** <note>the pelvic center</note>, **Manipura** <note>the navel center</note>, **Anahata** <note>the heart center</note>, **Vishuddha** <note>the throat center</note>, and **Ajna** <note>the brow center</note>. By the word "etc." <note>original: "adi"</note>, the author also includes the related energy channels <term>nadis</term>, the symbolic pillars <term>lingas</term>, the five spaces, and the manifestations of Shiva and Shakti.

<summary>This page serves as the formal introduction to the "Shat-chakra-nirupana," where Purnananda Swami declares his intent to explain Kundalini Yoga and the six chakras as the foundational "sprout" or starting point for achieving liberation and the direct experience of Supreme Bliss (Brahman).</summary>
<keywords>Shat-chakra-nirupana, Purnananda Swami, Kundalini Yoga, Brahman, Tantra, Muladhara, realization, Sri Tattva-chintamani</keywords>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras

---

## Now, following the tradition of the Tantras, the sequence of the six chakras and other centers is presented.
## This is said to be the first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss.

May the best of the wise, the eldest—who through the knowledge of **Great Yoga** <term>Maha-yoga</term> is familiar with the glory of the six lotus-centers, who alone is capable of revealing the inner truth, and who possesses the grace of the **Lord of the Night** <note>original: "kshapanatha," a poetic name for the Moon, symbolizing a cooling, compassionate presence</note>—manifest the inner truth of the six components along with their sixfold glory. 

Now, the most compassionate **Purnananda Swami**, wishing to uplift the world which is sunk in the mire of suffering, desires to speak of **Kundalini Yoga** <term>the yoga of the "coiled" primal energy</term> within the six chakras for the purpose of attaining the knowledge of reality <term>tattva-jnana</term> which brings about liberation <term>mukti</term>. For the attention of practitioners <term>sadhakas</term>, he makes his formal declaration, beginning with the word **"Now"** <note>original: "Atha," a traditional opening word in Sanskrit treatises implying auspiciousness and continuity</note>.

"Now" means "hereafter," specifically following the description of various <unclear>ritual</unclear> acts previously shown in his own work, the *Shritattva-chintamani*. 

The grammatical connection of this verse is: "The first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss is being described." **Supreme Bliss** <term>Paramananda</term> is the Absolute <term>Brahman</term>, according to the sacred revelation: "The Absolute is eternal, Supreme Bliss" <note>original: "nitiyam paramanandam brahma iti shruteh"</note>. The "realization" refers to the successful accomplishment of the direct experience of that Bliss. For that realization, this text is the "first sprout"—the first visible form of the practice that acts as the cause of that ultimate experience. This is what I, Purnananda, am describing. 

What is the nature of this "sprout"? It is the "sequence of the six chakras and other centers presented according to the Tantras." "According to the Tantras" means following the established doctrines of the Tantric schools. The phrase "six chakras and others" refers to the centers known as:
1. **Muladhara** <note>the root support center</note>
2. **Svadhisthana** <note>the self's own abode</note>
3. **Manipura** <note>the city of jewels</note>
4. **Anahata** <note>the unstruck sound</note>
5. **Vishuddha** <note>the pure center</note>
6. **Ajna** <note>the center of command</note>

By the word "others," the author includes related concepts such as the energy channels <term>nadis</term>, the symbolic pillars <term>lingas</term>, the five spaces <term>vyoma-panchaka</term>, and the union of **Shiva and Shakti** <note>the masculine and feminine principles of divinity</note>.

<summary>This page introduces the "Shat-chakra-nirupana," framing the study of the six energy centers as the essential starting point for attaining spiritual liberation and supreme bliss. The author, Purnananda Swami, defines the chakras and emphasizes that his work follows traditional Tantric systems.</summary>

# Investigation into the Six Chakras

-> Now, according to the tradition of the Tantras, the sequence of the six chakras and other centers is presented. <-
-> This is said to be the first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss. <-

Through the knowledge of **Great Yoga** <term>Maha-yoga</term>, he became familiar with the glory of the six lotus-centers; he alone is capable of revealing the inner truth, and no other. May that best of the wise, the eldest, who is touched by the compassion of the **Lord of the Night** <note>original: "kshapa-natha," a name for the Moon, symbolizing cooling, enlightening grace</note>, manifest the inner truth of the six components along with their full glory. 

Now, the most compassionate **Purnananda Swami**, wishing to uplift the world which is sunk in the mire of suffering, desires to speak of **Kundalini Yoga** <term>the yoga of the primal energy "coiled" at the base of the spine</term> within the six chakras for the purpose of attaining the knowledge of reality <term>tattva-jnana</term> which brings about liberation <term>mukti</term>. To focus the attention of the practitioners <term>sadhakas</term>, he makes his formal declaration, beginning with the word **"Now"** <note>original: "Atha," a traditional auspicious opening for Sanskrit treatises</note>. 

"Now" signifies that this follows the description of the various rituals and actions previously shown in his own work, the **Shritattva-chintamani** <note>original title translates to "The Precious Gem of the Truth of Reality"</note>, and precedes this specific investigation into the six chakras. 

The meaning of the opening is as follows: This is called the "first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss." **Supreme Bliss** <term>Paramananda</term> is the Eternal Brahman <note>The ultimate, infinite reality in Hindu philosophy</note>, as stated in the Vedas: "Brahman is eternal Supreme Bliss." Its "realization" <term>nirvaha</term> refers to the accomplishment of direct experience or perception of that reality. The "first sprout" for that realization refers to the primary, manifest practices and knowledge which serve as the cause of that experience; these are now being spoken of by me, Purnananda. 

What is the nature of this "sprout"? It is the "sequence of the six chakras and other centers presented according to the Tantras." "According to the Tantras" means in accordance with the Tantric teachings. This refers to the sequence of the six chakras known as: **Muladhara** <note>the root center</note>, **Svadhisthana** <note>the self-base center</note>, **Manipura** <note>the city of jewels center</note>, **Anahata** <note>the unstruck sound center</note>, **Vishuddha** <note>the pure center</note>, and **Ajna** <note>the command center</note>. By the word "others," he includes the related energy channels <term>nadis</term>, the symbolic markers <term>lingas</term>, the five-fold spaces <term>vyoma-panchaka</term>, and the union of **Shiva** and **Shakti** <note>the masculine and feminine principles of the divine</note>.

<summary>Purnananda Swami introduces his study of the six chakras as a "sprout" or starting point for achieving union with the divine (Supreme Bliss). He frames this work as a continuation of his larger philosophical text, the Shritattva-chintamani, focusing on Kundalini Yoga.</summary>

# 1) Investigation into the Six Chakras

---

-> Now, following the tradition of the Tantras, the sequence of the six chakras and other centers is presented. <-
-> This is said to be the first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss. <-

Through the knowledge of **Great Yoga** <term>Maha-yoga</term>, he became familiar with the glory of the six lotus-centers; he alone—and no other—is capable of revealing the inner truth. May that best of the wise, the eldest, who is touched by the compassion of the **Lord of the Night** <note>original: "kshapa-natha", a title for the Moon, symbolizing cooling, enlightening grace</note>, manifest the inner truth of the six components along with their sixfold glory. 

Now, the most compassionate **Master Purnananda** <note>Purnananda Swami, the 16th-century author</note>, wishing to uplift the world which is sunk in the mire of suffering, desires to speak of the **Yoga of the Coiled Power** <term>the yoga of Kundalini, the primal energy at the base of the spine</term> within the six chakras for the purpose of attaining the knowledge of reality <term>tattva-jnana</term> which brings about liberation. To focus the attention of the practitioners <term>sadhakas</term>, he makes his formal declaration, beginning with the word **"Now"** <note>original: "Atha," a traditional auspicious opening for Sanskrit philosophical texts</note>. 

**"Now"** signifies: following the description of <unclear>certain</unclear> rituals in his own work, the *Precious Gem of the Truth of Fortune* <term>Shritattva-chintamani</term>, and immediately prior to this investigation of the six chakras.

The meaning of the verse is as follows: The "first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss" is being told. **Supreme Bliss** is the Absolute <term>Brahman</term>, following the sacred scripture <term>shruti</term> which says: "The Absolute is eternal and Supreme Bliss." Its **realization** <note>original: "nirvahah"</note> refers to the accomplishment of direct experience or perception of that state. The **first sprout** for that realization is the nature of the first manifested practice <note>karma</note> which acts as the cause. This is what is being told by me, Purnananda. 

What is the nature of this "sprout"? It is described as **"the sequence of the six chakras and other centers presented according to the Tantras."** "According to the Tantras" means following the school of Tantric thought. The "six chakras" refers to those known as the **Root-Support** <term>Muladhara</term>, **One’s Own Abode** <term>Svadhishthana</term>, **City of Jewels** <term>Manipura</term>, **Unstruck** <term>Anahata</term>, **Pure** <term>Vishuddha</term>, and **Command** <term>Ajna</term>. By the phrase "and other centers" <note>original: "adi"</note>, he includes the energy channels <term>nadis</term>, the symbolic pillars <term>lingas</term>, the five spaces, and the union of **the Benevolent** <term>Shiva</term> and **Power** <term>Shakti</term> associated with them...

# Investigation of the Six Chakras

---

-> Now, following the tradition of the Tantras, the sequence of the six chakras and other centers is presented. <-
-> This is said to be the first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss. <-

Through the knowledge of **Great Yoga** <term>Maha-yoga; the highest form of meditative union</term>, he became familiar with the glory of the six lotus-centers; he alone—and no other—is capable of revealing the inner truth. May that best of the wise, the eldest, who is touched by the compassion of the **Lord of the Night** <note>original: "kshapa-natha", a title for the Moon, symbolizing cooling, enlightening grace</note>, manifest the inner truth of the six components along with their thousandfold glory. 

Now, the most compassionate **Master Purnananda** <note>Purnananda Swami, the 16th-century author</note>, wishing to uplift the world which is sunk in the mire of suffering, desires to speak of the **Yoga of the Coiled Power** <term>Kundalini-yoga; the practice of awakening the primal energy at the base of the spine</term> within the six chakras for the purpose of attaining the knowledge of reality <term>tattva-jnana</term> which brings about liberation. To focus the attention of the practitioners, he begins his formal declaration with the word "Now." 

"Now" <note>original: "Atha"; traditionally used to signal a transition to a new, auspicious topic</note> signifies "after this." This means after the description of the <unclear>...</unclear> rituals previously shown in his own work, the *Shritattva-chintamani* <note>The "Auspicious Thought-Gem of Reality," Purnananda's encyclopedic Tantric text</note>. The meaning of the verse is as follows: "The first sprout for the realization of **Supreme Bliss** is now spoken." 

**Supreme Bliss** is the Absolute <term>Brahman</term>, according to the sacred scripture: "The Absolute is eternal Supreme Bliss." The "realization" <term>nirvaha</term> refers to the successful accomplishment of perceiving that Bliss. The "first sprout" is the very first manifest practice or action that acts as the cause for that realization. Purnananda states, "This is being spoken by me." 

What is the nature of this "sprout"? It is the "sequence of the six chakras and other centers as set forth in the tradition of the Tantras." "According to the Tantras" means according to the established Tantric systems. The "six chakras" refer to those named: **Moola-adhara** <term>Root-support</term>, **Svadhisthana** <term>One's own abode</term>, **Manipura** <term>City of jewels</term>, **Anahata** <term>Unstruck sound</term>, **Vishuddha** <term>Pure</term>, and **Ajna** <term>Command</term>. By the word "others," he includes the related energy channels <term>nadis</term>, the symbolic markers <term>lingas</term>, the five spaces <term>vyoma-panchaka</term>, and the principles of **Shiva** and **Shakti** <note>The masculine and feminine cosmic principles</note>.

<summary>This page introduces the "Shat-Chakra-Nirupana," framing it as a guide to Kundalini Yoga and a path to realizing the Absolute (Brahman). It defines the six chakras as the "first sprout" or foundational step toward spiritual liberation.</summary>

# Investigation of the Six Chakras

---

-> Now, following the tradition of the Tantras, the sequence of the six chakras and other centers is presented. <-
-> This is said to be the first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss. <-

Through the knowledge of **Great Yoga** <term>Maha-yoga; the highest form of meditative union</term>, he became familiar with the glory of the six lotus-centers <note>The "six lotuses" refer to the primary chakras</note>; he alone—and no other—is capable of revealing the inner truth. May that best of the wise, the eldest, who is touched by the compassion of the **Lord of the Night** <note>original: "kshapa-natha", a title for the Moon, symbolizing cooling, enlightening grace</note>, manifest the inner truth of the six components along with their thousandfold glory. 

Now, the most compassionate **Master Purnananda** <note>Purnananda Swami, the 16th-century Bengali scholar and yogi</note>, wishing to uplift the world which is sunk in the mire of suffering, desires to speak of the **Yoga of the Coiled Power** <term>Kundalini-yoga; the practice of awakening the primal energy at the base of the spine</term> within the six chakras for the purpose of attaining the knowledge of reality <term>tattva-jnana</term> which brings about liberation. To focus the attention of the practitioners, he begins his formal declaration with the word "Now." 

"Now" means following the description of various ritual acts previously shown in his own work, the *Shritattva-chintamani* <note>The "Auspicious Jewel of Reality," the author's larger compendium on Tantra</note>, and before the detailed investigation of the six chakras begins. 

The meaning of the phrase is this: "It is said to be the first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss." **Supreme Bliss** <term>Paramananda</term> is **Brahman** <term>The Ultimate Reality</term>, as the **Scriptures** <term>Shruti; specifically the Upanishads</term> state: "Brahman is eternal and Supreme Bliss." The "realization" <note>original: "nirvaha"</note> refers to the accomplishment of directly perceiving that Reality. For that realization, this text is the "first sprout"—the first manifestation of the necessary spiritual practice being spoken of by me, Purnananda. 

What is the nature of this "sprout"? It is the "sequence of the six chakras and other centers, raised in accordance with the Tantras." "In accordance with the Tantras" means following the established Tantric doctrines. The "six chakras" refer to those named:
1. **Muladhara** <note>The "Root Support" at the base of the spine</note>
2. **Svadhishthana** <note>The "Self's Abode" at the genital region</note>
3. **Manipura** <note>The "City of Jewels" at the navel</note>
4. **Anahata** <note>The "Unstruck Sound" at the heart</note>
5. **Vishuddha** <note>The "Pure" center at the throat</note>
6. **Ajna** <note>The "Command" center between the eyebrows</note>

The word "others" <note>original: "adi"</note> includes the associated energy channels <term>nadis</term>, the symbolic pillars <term>lingas</term>, the five voids or spaces <term>vyoma-panchaka</term>, and the union of the masculine and feminine principles, **Shiva and Shakti**.

-> ## Now, following the tradition of the Tantras, the sequence of the six chakras and other centers is presented. <-
-> ## This is said to be the first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss. <-

Through the knowledge of **Great Yoga** <term>Maha-yoga; the highest form of meditative union</term>, he became familiar with the glory of the six lotus-centers <note>The "six lotuses" refer to the primary energy centers or chakras</note>; he alone—and no other—is capable of revealing the inner truth. May that best of the wise, the eldest, who is touched by the compassion of the **Lord of the Night** <note>original: "kshapa-natha", a title for the Moon, symbolizing cooling, enlightening grace</note>, manifest the inner truth of the six components along with their thousandfold glory. 

Now, the most compassionate **Master Purnananda** <note>Purnananda Swami, the 16th-century Bengali scholar and yogi who authored this text</note>, wishing to uplift the world which is sunk in the mire of suffering, and for the purpose of attaining the knowledge of reality <term>tattva-jnana</term> which brings about liberation, desires to speak of the **Yoga of the Coiled Power** <term>Kundalini-yoga</term> within the six chakras. To focus the attention of the practitioners, he begins with the word **"Now"** <note>Sanskrit: "Atha", a traditional opening word signifying a transition to a new, auspicious topic</note>. 

The word "Now" signifies that this follows the description of those specific rituals and duties already shown in his own work, the **Jewel of the Truth of Reality** <term>Shritattva-chintamani</term>, prior to this investigation of the six chakras.

"Following the tradition of the Tantras" means according to the established systems of those scriptures. The "six chakras and others" refers to those known as the **Root-Support** <term>Muladhara</term>, **One's Own Abode** <term>Svadhishthana</term>, **City of Jewels** <term>Manipuraka</term>, **Unstruck** <term>Anahata</term>, **Pure** <term>Vishuddha</term>, and the **Command** <term>Ajna</term>. -> Now, the sequence of the six chakras and other centers is set forth, <-

<margin>Part 3)</margin> # Investigation of the Six Chakras

---

->**Now, the sequence of the six chakras and other centers is set forth,**<-
->**following the tradition of the Tantras.**<-
->**This is said to be the first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss.**<-

Through the knowledge of **Great Yoga** <term>Mahayoga; the supreme state of meditative union</term>, he became familiar with the glory of the six lotus-centers <note>The "six lotuses" are the primary energy centers or chakras located along the spine</note>; he alone—and no other—is capable of revealing the inner truth. May that best among the wise, the eldest, who is touched by the compassion of the **Lord of the Night** <note>original: "kshapa-natha", a title for the Moon, symbolizing the cooling, enlightening grace of Shiva or the Guru</note>, manifest the inner truth of the six limbs along with their thousandfold glory. 

Now, the most compassionate **Master Purnananda** <note>Purnananda Swami, the 16th-century Bengali scholar and initiate who authored this text</note>, wishing to uplift the world which is sunk in the mire of suffering, and for the purpose of attaining the knowledge of reality <term>tattva-jnana</term> which brings about liberation, desires to speak of the **Yoga of the Coiled Power** <term>Kundalini-yoga</term> within the six chakras. To ensure the focused attention of the practitioners, he makes a formal declaration, beginning with the word **"Now"** <note>original: "Atha", a traditional opening word signifying an auspicious beginning and a transition from a previous topic</note>. 

The word "Now" implies that this follows the description of the <unclear>rituals</unclear> previously shown in his own work, the *Jewel of the Truth of Reality* <note>original: "Shri-Tattva-Chintamani", the massive Tantric compendium of which this text forms the sixth chapter</note>. The meaning of the verse is as follows: it is said that this work is the **first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss**. 

"Supreme Bliss" refers to **Brahman** <term>the ultimate, non-dual reality</term>, according to the scripture: "Brahman is eternal, Supreme Bliss." The "realization" <note>original: "nirvaha"</note> refers to the fulfillment of its direct perception. For that realization, the "first sprout" <note>original: "prathamankura"</note> is the nature of the first manifested activity that serves as the cause—this is what is being spoken of by me, Purnananda. 

What is the nature of this "sprout"? It is "the sequence of the six chakras and other centers set forth according to the Tantras." "According to the Tantras" means following the established doctrines of the Tantric tradition regarding the six chakras known as: 
1. **Muladhara** <note>Root Support center</note>
2. **Svadhisthana** <note>One’s Own Abode center</note>
3. **Manipura** <note>City of Jewels center</note>
4. **Anahata** <note>Unstruck Sound center</note>
5. **Vishuddha** <note>Center of Purity</note>
6. **Ajna** <note>Center of Command</note>

The word "and others" <note>original: "adi"</note> refers to the related channels <term>nadis</term>, the symbolic forms <term>lingas</term>, the five types of space <term>vyoma-panchaka</term>, and the union of **Shiva and Shakti**...

<summary>This page introduces the "Investigation of the Six Chakras," defining it as the foundational "sprout" or starting point for achieving spiritual liberation and the experience of Supreme Bliss (Brahman). The author, Purnananda, contextualizes this work as a continuation of his larger text, the Shri-Tattva-Chintamani.</summary>

# C) Investigation of the Six Chakras

->---o---<-

> **Now, following the tradition of the Tantras, the sequence of the six chakras and other centers is set forth.**
> **This is said to be the first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss.**

Through the knowledge of **Great Yoga** <term>Mahayoga; the highest state of meditative union</term>, he became familiar with the glory of the six lotus-centers <note>The "six lotuses" refers to the chakras, which are visualized as lotus flowers within the subtle body</note>. He alone—and no other—is capable of revealing the inner truth. May that best among the wise, the eldest, who is touched by the compassion of the **Lord of the Night** <note>original: "kshapa-natha", a title for the Moon, symbolizing the cooling, enlightening grace of the Guru or the deity Shiva</note>, manifest the inner truth of the "six limbs" <note>the components of the subtle system</note> along with their thousandfold glory. 

Now, the most compassionate **Master Purnananda** <note>Purnananda Swami, the 16th-century Bengali scholar who composed this text in 1526 CE</note>, wishing to uplift the world which is sunk in the mire of suffering, and for the purpose of attaining the knowledge of reality <term>tattva-jnana</term> which brings about liberation, desires to speak of the **Yoga of the Coiled Power** <term>Kundalini Yoga</term> within the six chakras. To ensure the focused attention of practitioners <term>sadhakas</term>, he begins with the word "**Now**" <note>original: "Atha"; traditionally used in Sanskrit texts to mark an auspicious beginning and a transition from a previous topic</note>. 

"Now" signifies that this follows the description of <unclear>those</unclear> rituals previously shown in his own work, the *Shri-Tattva-Chintamani* <note>The Precious Gem of the Truth of Reality</note>. The meaning is this: the sequence is described as the "**first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss**." "**Supreme Bliss**" <term>Paramananda</term> refers to the Absolute <term>Brahman</term>, according to the sacred scripture <term>Sruti</term> which states, "The Absolute is eternal, supreme bliss." The "**realization**" refers to the direct experience or accomplishment of that state. I, Purnananda, am declaring the nature of this practice, which acts as the "**first sprout**"—the first visible stage or cause of that realization. 

What is the nature of this "sprout"? It is the sequence of the six chakras and other centers, set forth according to the **Tantras** <note>The body of esoteric scriptures and traditions focusing on ritual and yoga</note>. According to the Tantric systems, this refers to the centers known as: **Muladhara** <note>the Root-Support at the base of the spine</note>, **Svadhishthana** <note>the Self-Abode in the pelvic region</note>, **Manipura** <note>the City of Jewels at the navel</note>, **Anahata** <note>the Unstruck Sound at the heart</note>, **Vishuddha** <note>the Purified at the throat</note>, and **Ajna** <note>the Command center between the eyebrows</note>. By the phrase "and other centers," the author includes the related channels <term>nadis</term>, the symbolic markers <term>lingas</term>, the five spaces <term>vyoma-panchaka</term>, and the union of **Shiva and Shakti** <note>the masculine and feminine principles of consciousness and energy</note>.

<summary>This page introduces the Shat-Chakra-Nirupana as a practical guide for liberation, extracted from Purnananda Swami's larger work. It defines the study of the six chakras (from the Root to the Brow) as the essential first step in realizing the Supreme Bliss of Brahman.</summary>

# 1. Investigation of the Six Chakras.

---

->Now, following the tradition of the Tantras, the sequence of the six chakras and other centers is set forth.<-
->This is said to be the first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss.<-

Through the knowledge of **Great Yoga** <term>Mahayoga; the highest state of meditative union</term>, he became familiar with the glory of the six lotus-centers <note>The "six lotuses" refers to the chakras, which are visualized as lotus flowers within the subtle body</note>. He alone—and no other—is capable of revealing the inner truth. May that best among the wise, the eldest, who is touched by the compassion of the **Lord of the Night** <note>original: "kshapa-natha", a title for the Moon, symbolizing the cooling, enlightening grace of the Guru or the deity Shiva</note>, manifest the inner truth of the "six limbs" <note>the components of the subtle system</note> along with their thousandfold glory. 

Now, desiring to lift the world—which is submerged in the mire of suffering—the most compassionate **Master Purnananda** <note>Purnananda Swami, the 16th-century Bengali scholar who composed this text in 1526 CE</note> begins to speak of the Yoga of the **Coiled Power** <term>Kundalini; the dormant spiritual energy at the base of the spine</term> within the six chakras, for the purpose of attaining the knowledge of reality that brings about liberation. To focus the attention of the practitioners, he makes a formal declaration, saying "Now" (*Atha*). 

"Now" means "afterwards": specifically, after the description of those rituals presented earlier in his own work, the *Shri Tattva Chintamani* <note>The Auspicious Jewel of Reality, the larger encyclopedic work from which this text is extracted</note>. The meaning of the verse is as follows: the "first sprout" for the realization of Supreme Bliss is being described. "Supreme Bliss" is **Brahman** <term>the ultimate, infinite reality</term>, as the scriptures say: "Brahman is eternal Supreme Bliss." The "realization" of this is its direct experience or final accomplishment. The "first sprout" is the primary form of the practice that acts as the cause for that realization; this is what I, Purnananda, am describing. 

What is the nature of this sprout? It is the "sequence of the six chakras and others set forth according to the Tantras." This means according to the views of the Tantric scriptures, covering the centers named **Muladhara**, **Svadhisthana**, **Manipura**, **Anahata**, **Vishuddha**, and **Ajna**. By the word "others" <note>original: "adi"</note>, it includes associated matters such as the energy channels (*nadis*), the symbolic markers (*lingas*), the five spaces (*vyoma*), and the union of Shiva and Shakti <note>the masculine and feminine principles of divinity</note>.

<summary>This page introduces the "Shat-Chakra-Nirupana" as a practical guide for Kundalini Yoga, extracted from Purnananda Swami's larger work. It defines the study of the six energy centers as the essential "first sprout" or beginning stage for achieving spiritual liberation and the direct experience of Supreme Bliss (Brahman).</summary>

# Investigation of the Six Chakras

---

-> Now, following the tradition of the Tantras <term>Tantra; a body of esoteric ritual and meditative teachings</term>, the sequence of the six chakras <term>Chakra; 'wheel' or energy center in the subtle body</term> and other related elements is set forth. <-
-> This is said to be the first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss. <-

Through the knowledge of **Great Yoga** <term>Mahayoga; the highest state of meditative union</term>, he became familiar with the glory of the six lotus-centers <note>The "six lotuses" refers to the chakras, which are visualized as lotus flowers within the subtle body</note>. He alone—and no other—is capable of revealing the inner truth. May that best among the wise, the <unclear>eldest, though un-</unclear>touched by the cooling grace of the **Lord of the Night** <note>original: "kshapa-natha", a title for the Moon, symbolizing the cooling, enlightening grace of the Guru or the deity Shiva</note>, manifest the inner truth of the "six limbs" <note>the components of the subtle system</note> along with their thousandfold glory. 

Now, desiring to lift the world—which is submerged in the mire of suffering—the most compassionate **Master Purnananda** <note>Purnananda Swami, the 16th-century Bengali scholar and yogi credited with this text</note> intends to speak on **Kundalini Yoga** <term>Kundalini; the 'coiled' spiritual energy at the base of the spine</term> within the six chakras for the sake of accomplishing that knowledge of truth which produces liberation. For the attention of the practitioners, he makes his declaration, beginning with the word "Now." 

"Now" means: immediately following the description of the <unclear>previously demonstrated</unclear> rituals in his own work, the *Shri-Tattva-Chintamani* <note>The "Auspicious Jewel of Reality-Contemplation," the larger encyclopedia from which this text is taken</note>, and before the investigation of the six chakras begins. 

The connection is as follows: "The first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss is [now] spoken." **Supreme Bliss** is the Absolute <note>original: "Brahma"</note>, for the Scripture says, "The Absolute is eternal Supreme Bliss." The "realization" thereof is the completion of the process in the form of direct experience. The "first sprout" for that end is the nature of the first manifest practice which serves as the cause. This is what is <unclear>being spoken by me, Purnananda</unclear>. 

What is the nature of this "sprout"? It is the "sequence of the six chakras and other centers, extracted according to the Tantras." This means, following the system of the Tantras, the sequence of the six chakras—named **Muladhara** <note>Root center</note>, **Svadhisthana** <note>Sacral center</note>, **Manipura** <note>Solar plexus</note>, **Anahata** <note>Heart center</note>, **Vishuddha** <note>Throat center</note>, and **Ajna** <note>Third eye center</note>. By the word "and others," he refers to the associated energy channels <term>Nadis</term>, the symbolic pillars <term>Lingas</term>, the five voids of space, and the union of Shiva and Shakti <note>The masculine and feminine principles of the universe</note>.

<summary>Purnananda Swami introduces his study of the six chakras as an essential "sprout" or starting point for attaining spiritual liberation and supreme bliss. He frames this work as a specific extraction from his larger text, the Shri-Tattva-Chintamani, intended to guide practitioners through the complexities of Kundalini Yoga.</summary>

# Investigation of the Six Chakras

---

-> Now, following the tradition of the **Tantras** <term>Tantra; a body of esoteric ritual and meditative teachings</term>, the sequence of the six chakras <term>Chakra; 'wheel' or energy center in the subtle body</term> and other related elements is set forth. <-
-> This is said to be the first sprout for the realization of **Supreme Bliss**. <-

Through the knowledge of **Great Yoga** <term>Mahayoga; the highest state of meditative union</term>, he became familiar with the glory of the six lotus-centers <note>The "six lotuses" refer to the chakras, which are visualized as lotus flowers within the subtle body</note>. He alone—and no other—is capable of revealing the inner truth. May that best among the wise, the eldest, even if <unclear>...</unclear> by the cooling grace of the **Lord of the Night** <note>original: "kshapa-natha", a title for the Moon, symbolizing the cooling, enlightening grace of the Guru or the deity Shiva</note>, manifest the inner truth of the six-fold system and the power of the six lotuses. 

Now, desiring to lift up the world submerged in the mire of suffering, the most compassionate **Purnananda Swami**, for the sake of attaining the knowledge of truth that brings liberation, wishes to speak of the **Yoga of Kundalini** <term>Kundalini; the "Coiled One," the primal energy or goddess-power residing at the base of the spine</term> within the six chakras. To ensure the attention of the practitioners, he makes this vow: "Now." 

"Now" means "subsequently"—specifically, after the description of the <unclear>...</unclear> actions previously shown in his own work, the ***Shri-Tattva-Chintamani*** <note>original: "The Auspicious Jewel of the Thought of Truth"</note>, and before this investigation of the six chakras. 

The meaning of the verse is: "This is said to be the first sprout for the realization of Supreme Bliss." **Supreme Bliss** is the Absolute <note>original: "Brahma"</note>, for the scripture <note>original: "Shruti"</note> says: "The Absolute is eternal, supreme bliss." Its "realization" is the completion consisting of its direct experience. The "first sprout" for that is the first appearing form of the practices which cause it. This is what I, Purnananda, am speaking of. 

What is this "sprout" like? It is "the sequence of the six chakras and others set forth following the tradition of the Tantras." "Following the tradition of the Tantras" means according to the Tantric school of thought. "The six chakras and others" refers to those named **Muladhara**, **Svadhisthana**, **Manipura**, **Anahata**, **Vishuddha**, and **Ajna**. By the word "others," he includes the channels <term>Nadi; subtle energy channels like Ida and Pingala</term>, the symbolic forms <term>Linga; representative pillars of energy</term>, the five spaces <note>original: "vyoma-panchaka"</note>, and the union of **Shiva and Shakti** <note>Shiva represents pure consciousness and Shakti represents cosmic energy; their union is the goal of Tantra</note>.

<summary>Purnananda Swami introduces the "Investigation of the Six Chakras" as a primary method for achieving spiritual liberation and bliss. He defines the chakras (Muladhara to Ajna) as the "first sprout" or foundation for the practitioner to realize the Absolute through Kundalini Yoga.</summary>
